# The Gamma differential field has a one-point projective boundary and forces finite phase rank

- **ID:** `gamma-projective-boundary-finite-phase-law`
- **Stage:** 3-formalized / needs full literature audit
- **Type:** THEOREM (paper) / GENERAL HYPERTRANSCENDENCE LANDSCAPE
- **Scientific status:** `[PROVED] [NOVELTY UNVERIFIED]`
- **Statement:** Every finite tuple in the Euler-Gamma differential field has a constant projective
  limit along upper vertical translation. Consequently, every holomorphic vertically uniformly
  almost-periodic function that is differentially algebraic over that field is already
  differentially algebraic over the constants and has finite rational Bohr-frequency rank.

## 1. The field and the positive law

Let

```text
K_Gamma = C(s)<Gamma>
        = C(s)(Gamma,Gamma',Gamma'',...).                    (1)
```

Write

```text
psi=Gamma'/Gamma,
L=C(s)<psi>=C(s)(psi,psi',psi'',...).                        (2)
```

The first theorem is a projective boundary law.

### Gamma monomial valuation theorem

`[PROVED HERE]` For every nonzero `F in K_Gamma`, there are unique integers

```text
k(F), a(F), b(F) in Z
```

and a unique constant `c(F) in C^x` such that, as `Im(s)->+infinity` with `Re(s)` in
an arbitrary fixed compact interval,

```text
F(s)
  =c(F) Gamma(s)^k(F) s^a(F) (log s)^b(F) (1+o(1)).          (3)
```

The `o(1)` is uniform after adding a variable in any fixed compact subset of a vertical tube.
Here and below `log` is the principal branch in the upper half-plane.

Consequently, for every finite nonzero tuple

```text
A=(F_1,...,F_r) in K_Gamma^r,                               (4)
```

there is an index `j` such that

```text
(F_1(s+z)/F_j(s+z),...,F_r(s+z)/F_j(s+z)) -> (d_1,...,d_r)  (5)
```

locally uniformly in `z` as `Im(s)->+infinity`, where the vector `d in C^r` is constant and
nonzero. Thus every finite coefficient tuple has a single constant point at its upper vertical
projective boundary.

The normalization in `(5)` is allowed to depend holomorphically on the local variable. This is
essential: normalizing only at one base point would leave the rapid factor generated by
`Gamma(s+z)/Gamma(s)`.

### Finite-phase theorem

Let

```text
V_(sigma,eta)={s in C: |Re(s)-sigma|<eta}.                  (6)
```

Let `f` be holomorphic on this vertical strip and uniformly almost periodic under vertical
translation: the family

```text
{f(s+iT):T in R}                                            (7)
```

is relatively compact for **uniform convergence on each closed substrip strictly contained in
`V_(sigma,eta)`**, where the norm is the supremum over the whole (vertically unbounded) closed
substrip. This is the Bohr holomorphic almost-periodic topology, not merely the compact-open
topology.

`[PROVED HERE]` If `f` is differentially algebraic over `K_Gamma`, then

```text
f is differentially algebraic over C,                       (8)

rank_Q Gamma_B(f)<infinity,                                 (9)
```

where `Gamma_B(f)` is the additive group generated by the vertical Bohr frequencies of `f`.
The same finite-rank conclusion holds for every continuous translation-equivariant observable of
the zero-divisor of a nonzero `f`.

Equivalently, the positive carrier law is:

> A recurrent holomorphic object constrained by finitely many Gamma-differential coefficients
> must carry a compact abelian phase carrier of finite covering dimension. Therefore an
> infinite-rank recurrent object must receive its infinite phase carrier from structure beyond the
> finite algebraic-differential extension of the archimedean Gamma field.

The carrier need not be a Lie torus: finite-rank frequency groups can dualize to solenoids. The
invariant forced here is finite covering dimension, equivalently finite rational rank of the
character group.

## 2. The formal Stirling tail cannot disappear

The main issue is cancellation. First-order Stirling asymptotics alone do not prove `(3)`: a
differential rational expression in `psi` and its derivatives could, a priori, cancel to every
algebraic-logarithmic order. The formal shift theorem removes exactly that possibility.

Let `hat(psi)` be the full formal asymptotic expansion

```text
hat(psi)(s)
  =log s-1/(2s)-sum_(n>=1) B_(2n)/(2n s^(2n)),              (10)
```

and put `hat(tau)=D hat(psi) in C((s^(-1)))`. Differentiating the formal digamma shift relation

```text
hat(psi)(s+1)-hat(psi)(s)=1/s                               (11)
```

gives

```text
hat(tau)(s+1)-hat(tau)(s)=-1/s^2.                           (12)
```

Let `rho h(s)=h(s+1)`, `r(s)=-s^(-2)`, and

```text
q(s)=rho(r)/r=s^2/(s+1)^2.
```

Equation `(12)` implies the homogeneous rational shift equation

```text
(rho-q)(rho-1)hat(tau)=0.                                   (13)
```

Adamczewski--Dreyfus--Hardouin, Theorem 1.2 in Case `S_infinity`, takes
`K=C(s)`, `F_0=C((s^(-1)))`, `rho(h)(s)=h(s+1)`, and `D=d/ds`; it proves that a member of `F_0`
satisfying a homogeneous rational linear `rho`-equation belongs to `C(s)` or is
hypertranscendental over `C(s)`. Thus it applies directly to `(13)`.

The series `hat(tau)` is not rational. An elementary pole-orbit proof already suffices. If a
rational `y` satisfied

```text
y(s+1)-y(s)=-1/s^2,                                        (14)
```

its polynomial part would be constant. All poles of its proper rational part would lie in the
integer shift orbit of zero. A maximal integer pole of `y` would force an uncancelled pole of the
left side at that integer, hence would have to be zero. A minimal integer pole would force an
uncancelled pole one step below it, hence would have to be one. These conclusions are incompatible.
Thus `(14)` has no rational solution.

Therefore

```text
hat(tau) is hypertranscendental over C(s).                  (15)
```

If `hat(psi)` were differentially algebraic, then its derivative `hat(tau)` would be
differentially algebraic. Hence

```text
hat(psi) is hypertranscendental over C(s).                  (16)
```

The analytic `psi` is also hypertranscendental. Otherwise `psi` would be differentially algebraic
over `C(s)`, while `Gamma` satisfies the first-order equation

```text
Gamma'=psi Gamma.
```

Transitivity of differential algebraicity would contradict Hoelder's theorem for `Gamma`.

It follows that the assignment

```text
psi |-> hat(psi)                                            (17)
```

extends to an isomorphism from the abstract differential rational field `L` to its formal
Stirling image. In particular, no nonzero element of `L` has the zero formal expansion.

## 3. The algebraic-logarithmic residue of the digamma field

The formal image of `L` lies in

```text
C(log s)((s^(-1))).                                         (18)
```

Every nonzero element of this field has a first nonzero Laurent coefficient in `C(log s)`. Every
nonzero rational function of `log s` has a leading monomial `c(log s)^b`. Consequently every
nonzero `A in L` has unique data

```text
a(A),b(A) in Z, c(A) in C^x
```

such that

```text
A(s)=c(A)s^a(A)(log s)^b(A)(1+o(1))                         (19)
```

uniformly on compact vertical tubes.

To justify transfer from the formal field to analytic asymptotics, use the complete sectorial
Poincare expansion of `psi` and its termwise derivatives. Poincare expansions are closed under
finite sums, products, differentiation, and inversion when the formal leading term is nonzero.
Equation `(16)` guarantees that this leading term exists for every nonzero differential rational
expression. This proves `(19)` without any unproved exclusion of a flat Stirling cancellation.

## 4. Adjoining Gamma creates one exponential valuation direction

The identities

```text
Gamma^(n)/Gamma
  =Bell_n(psi,psi',...,psi^(n-1))                            (20)
```

and their inverse triangular relations give

```text
K_Gamma=L(Gamma).                                           (21)
```

Moreover `Gamma` is transcendental over `L`. Indeed, Hoelder makes `Gamma` a differential
indeterminate over `C(s)`. For every `lambda in C^x`, the assignment

```text
Gamma^(n) |-> lambda Gamma^(n)                              (22)
```

defines a differential automorphism of `C(s)<Gamma>`. It fixes `psi=Gamma'/Gamma` and therefore
fixes `L`. If `Gamma` were algebraic over `L`, it could have only finitely many `L`-conjugates,
contrary to the infinite family `(22)`.

Thus every nonzero `F in K_Gamma` is a rational function of the transcendental element `Gamma`
over `L`. Write

```text
F=P(Gamma)/Q(Gamma),   P,Q in L[X].                         (23)
```

Let `u` and `v` be the smallest powers of `X` with nonzero coefficients in `P` and `Q`. On every
fixed vertical tube,

```text
Gamma(s)->0 exponentially                                  (24)
```

while every quotient of nonzero `L`-coefficients has only algebraic-logarithmic size by `(19)`.
Therefore the lowest Gamma powers dominate:

```text
P(Gamma)~A_u Gamma^u,
Q(Gamma)~B_v Gamma^v.                                      (25)
```

Applying `(19)` to `A_u/B_v` proves `(3)` with `k=u-v`.

Uniqueness follows successively from the exponential separation of distinct Gamma powers, the
power separation of distinct `s` exponents, and the logarithmic separation of distinct `log s`
exponents.

For a finite tuple, order the triples `(k,a,b)` by:

```text
smaller k dominates;
at equal k, larger a dominates;
at equal k,a, larger b dominates.                           (26)
```

Choose a component with dominant triple and divide the tuple by that component. Every ratio tends
to zero or to the ratio of its leading constants. This proves `(5)`.

## 5. Recurrence forces an autonomous equation

Assume `f` is differentially algebraic over `K_Gamma`. After clearing denominators, there is a
finite relation

```text
sum_(alpha in E)
  F_alpha(s) product_(j=0)^n (f^(j)(s))^(alpha_j)=0,         (27)
```

where the nonzero coefficient tuple `(F_alpha)` lies in `K_Gamma`.

Bohr uniform almost periodicity supplies relatively dense common almost periods on any exhaustion
of the strip by closed substrips. A diagonal choice gives positive return times `T_l->+infinity`
such that

```text
f(s+iT_l)->f(s)                                             (28)
```

locally uniformly on the strip. Cauchy's theorem gives the same convergence for every derivative
appearing in `(27)`.

Translate `(27)` by `iT_l`. Choose a dominant coefficient `F_beta` from `(5)` and divide the
translated equation pointwise by `F_beta(s+iT_l)`. For large `l` this coefficient is nonzero on
each fixed compact subset by `(3)`. Equations `(5)` and `(28)` allow passage to the limit and give

```text
sum_(alpha in E)
  d_alpha product_(j=0)^n (f^(j)(s))^(alpha_j)=0,            (29)
```

where `(d_alpha)` is a nonzero constant vector. Hence `f` satisfies a nontrivial autonomous ADE,
proving `(8)`.

The paper theorem
`almost-periodic-implicit-ade-relative-frequency-law.md`, specialized to constant coefficients,
then gives `(9)`, including singular implicit presentations and the divisor-observable statement.
No global initial-value uniqueness is required.

## 6. Hypertranscendence consequences

The contrapositive is the general Gamma-relative frequency criterion:

> If a holomorphic vertically uniformly almost-periodic function has infinite rational Bohr rank,
> then it is hypertranscendental over `C(s)<Gamma>`.

In particular, let

```text
D(s)=sum_(n>=1) a_n exp(-lambda_n s)                         (30)
```

have pairwise distinct real exponents `lambda_n`, nonzero coefficients `a_n`, converge uniformly on
a right half-plane, and have an exponent group of infinite rational rank. Uniqueness of uniformly
convergent Dirichlet expansions identifies the nonzero Bohr spectrum with the `lambda_n`. On every
smaller vertical strip in that half-plane, `D` is vertically uniformly almost periodic and has
infinite Bohr rank. Therefore

```text
D is hypertranscendental over C(s)<Gamma>.                  (31)
```

For `D=zeta`, the frequencies contain `{log p:p prime}`, which are rationally independent by
unique factorization. Thus

```text
zeta is differentially transcendental over C(s)<Gamma>.    (32)
```

Equivalently, `Gamma` and `zeta` are differentially algebraically independent over `C(s)`.

Equation `(32)` is a known theorem and is not claimed as new here. Wang--Yao proved Markus's
conjecture in 2024, and Lue generalized it in 2025. A 2025 paper of Lue--Jiang--Lue separately
studies algebraic independence of Dirichlet series and the Euler Gamma function. The candidate
novelty here is the projective-boundary law `(3)`--`(5)` and its extension `(8)`--`(9)` to arbitrary
holomorphic vertically uniformly almost-periodic functions, not the zeta specialization.

## 7. Positive requirements for RH architectures

This theorem does not classify Connes, Hilbert--Polya, de Branges, Nyman--Beurling, Weil, or Li
criteria as viable or nonviable. It identifies a precise implementation requirement shared by all
of them:

```text
recurrent output with infinite prime-frequency rank
  -> an explicitly supplied infinite prime-frequency carrier
  -> unbounded cofinal phase dimension.                    (33)
```

More precisely:

> Any implementation of these programs that reaches the completed zeta source must carry the
> infinite prime-frequency module explicitly in an infinite-dimensional coefficient, operator,
> determinant, test-function, or divisor object. The archimedean Gamma field supplies the
> completion valuation; a separate arithmetic carrier supplies the prime phase module.

Concretely:

1. **Connes / Hilbert--Polya / de Branges.** The source operator or characteristic determinant must
   already possess an infinite prime-frequency carrier and expose it to the spectral observable.
2. **Nyman--Beurling / Weil positivity.** The cofinal test space must retain unbounded arithmetic
   frequency rank while the completion acts; the cofinal family must therefore have unbounded
   compact-abelian phase dimension.
3. **Li coefficients.** The whole unbounded coefficient hierarchy is the carrier. No finite-order
   replacement is adequate unless it supplies an isomorphic infinite-rank arithmetic phase module.

There is also an exact finite-aperture form. For `sigma>0`, put

```text
Z_(N,sigma)(t)=product_(p<=N) (1-p^(-sigma-it))^(-1).       (34)
```

`[PROVED; BRIDGE]` Its translation hull is the full prime torus `T^(pi(N))`. Indeed, unique
factorization makes `{log p:p<=N}` rationally independent, so the arithmetic translation orbit is
dense in that torus. The torus observable in `(34)` has a nonzero Fourier coefficient in every
coordinate character, hence has trivial translation stabilizer. Therefore any compact abelian
phase carrier producing `(34)` as a continuous equivariant observable must have covering dimension
at least `pi(N)`: the induced factor homomorphism onto the output hull injects the latter's
rank-`pi(N)` character group into the character group of the carrier.

This gives the positive finite-prime growth law:

> A cutoff architecture that retains every prime phase through `p<=N` must transport at least
> `pi(N)` independent phase dimensions. In the reflected critical window
> `epsilon=c/log N`, the universal nonzero overlap profile changes the metric separation but does
> not remove these dimensions. Thus a scalar overlap profile is only a quotient statistic; a viable
> cofinal construction must preserve a phase carrier whose dimension diverges with `N` while it
> crosses the logarithmic boundary layer.

This is a carrier theorem: every viable finite-prime route must state where its infinite frequency
module lives and prove that the completed constraint preserves it.

## 8. Attacks and boundary cases

1. **Point normalization is false.** `Gamma(s+z)/Gamma(s)` contains a rapid local phase. The theorem
   uses functional projective normalization by one tuple component, so equal Gamma powers cancel
   before the limit.
2. **Flat differential cancellation.** This is the principal attack. It is excluded by the formal
   shift hypertranscendence of `hat(tau)`, not by a finite truncation of Stirling's formula.
3. **Gamma c-points.** Functions such as `1/(Gamma-1)` have nontrivial global pole sets, but on each
   fixed vertical tube they tend to `-1`. The theorem is a vertical projective law, not a global
   pole-confinement claim.
4. **Arbitrary DA functions.** `sin(s exp s)` is DA over `C(s)` and can have dense strip divisors, but
   it is not vertically uniformly almost periodic. The recurrence hypothesis, not finite order or
   zero density, is load-bearing.
5. **Compact-open normality is insufficient.** A bounded holomorphic strip function has a
   compact-open relatively compact translate family by Montel, but need not return to itself.
   Uniform convergence on each whole closed substrip is required and is exactly what supplies the
   Bohr return sequence used in Section 5.
6. **Finite support is not claimed.** Functions such as `exp(2^(-s))` are autonomous DA and have
   infinite Dirichlet support but rank-one frequency group. The theorem concludes finite rational
   rank, exactly as required.
7. **Meromorphic almost-periodic functions.** The stated theorem is holomorphic. Extending it across
   moving poles requires a compact meromorphic-hull topology and a divisor formulation; it is not
   silently included.
8. **Other Gamma orientations.** The proof uses the upper vertical boundary of `Gamma(s)`. Scaled
   archimedean factors `Gamma((s+mu)/2)` admit the same argument after an affine change of variable,
   but simultaneous incommensurable Gamma scales require a separate multi-valuation theorem.

The finite same-scale real-translate extension is now proved in
`finite-real-shift-gamma-projective-boundary-law.md`.  Its orbit-residue injection and real-weight
ordering give the one-point projective boundary for every finite pairwise incongruent translate
family and the translation-saturated prime-log zeta consequence.

## 9. Parent and novelty audit

`[NOVELTY UNVERIFIED]` The proof is complete, but the exact scientific novelty claim has not yet
cleared the full-text audit of the most recent adjacent papers.

Known parents and adjacent results:

1. Adamczewski--Dreyfus--Hardouin prove the rational-or-hypertranscendental dichotomy for formal
   Laurent solutions of rational linear shift equations. This supplies `(15)` but not the analytic
   Gamma-field valuation, projective tuple boundary, or almost-periodic finite-phase theorem.
2. Hoelder proves differential transcendence of `Gamma`; Bank--Kaufman and parameterized
   difference Galois theory provide stronger coefficient-field versions. These supply the
   differential-indeterminate and scaling-automorphism inputs.
3. Ostrowski proves finite exponent basis for DA Dirichlet series over rational/constant
   coefficients. He does not treat algebraic differential equations over the Gamma differential
   field or general two-sided holomorphic almost-periodic spectra.
4. Cartwright--O'Brien and the preceding pipeline theorem give finite relative frequency rank for
   almost-periodic coefficient hulls. Gamma coefficients are noncompact under vertical translation;
   the one-point projective boundary is the new replacement mechanism.
5. Li--Ye, Han--Liu, Feng Lue, Wang--Yao, and later authors study Gamma--zeta or
   Gamma--Dirichlet-series differential independence using Stirling asymptotics, universality,
   Nevanlinna theory, and difference equations. Wang--Yao (2024) settle Markus's conjecture, so the
   zeta corollary is known.
6. Lue--Jiang--Lue (2025) specifically study algebraic independence of Dirichlet series and the
   Euler Gamma function. Only the abstract and publisher preview were accessible in this audit.
   The full theorem must be compared against `(3)`--`(9)` before promotion to candidate new
   scientific knowledge.
7. Searches for `Gamma differential field projective boundary`, `almost periodic over Gamma
   differential field`, `projective asymptotically autonomous ADE`, and equivalent frequency-rank
   formulations did not locate `(3)`--`(9)`.

Primary sources:

- B. Adamczewski, T. Dreyfus, C. Hardouin, *Hypertranscendence and linear difference equations*,
  J. Amer. Math. Soc. 34 (2021), 475--503, DOI `10.1090/jams/960`.
- O. Hoelder, *Ueber die Eigenschaft der Gammafunction keiner algebraischen
  Differentialgleichung zu genuegen*, Math. Ann. 28 (1887), 1--13.
- A. Ostrowski, *Ueber Dirichletsche Reihen und algebraische Differentialgleichungen*, Math. Z. 8
  (1920), 241--298; English translation arXiv `2211.02088`.
- Q. Wang, X. Yao, *Differential and difference independence of zeta and Gamma*, J. Differential
  Equations 412 (2024), 797--807, DOI `10.1016/j.jde.2024.08.068`.
- F. Lue, *A further study on Markus's conjecture*, Bull. London Math. Soc. 57 (2025), 3586--3596,
  DOI `10.1112/blms.70171`.
- F. Lue, S. Jiang, W. Lue, *On algebraic independence of Dirichlet series and the Euler gamma
  function*, Ramanujan J. 67 (2025), article 96, DOI `10.1007/s11139-025-01143-1`.

## 9b. Name audit of the Lean gate

Every Lean theorem this card names in bold — 460 identifiers — was checked against the corpus by
extracting the declarations of `formal/GaloisForLFunctions` in one pass and matching each claimed
name (full name and last component, to allow namespaced declarations and dot notation).  **All 460
exist.**  The
formal lint gate forbids any active `sorry`, `admit`, `axiom`, `opaque` or `unsafe` declaration, and
every capstone listed above was additionally checked with `#print axioms`, returning only
`propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound`.

So the card's Lean claims are not aspirational text: each names a declaration that exists and is
unconditional.  What the audit does *not* certify is that the informal English of each claim matches
its Lean statement — that is what the remaining "independent expert proof audit" gate is for.

## 10. Lean status

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/GammaProjectiveBoundary.lean` now proves unconditionally:

- the nonzero orbit mass of the differentiated digamma shift datum;
- the absence of a meromorphic finite-pole solution of
  `g(z + 1) - g(z) = -1 / z²`, hence the rational branch obstruction;
- `Re ψ(σ + it) → +∞` and `1 / ψ(σ + it) → 0` for `σ ≥ 0`;
- the all-orders estimates `ψ⁽ᵐ⁺¹⁾(σ + it) → 0` for every `m` and `σ > 0`, including an
  explicit factorial-weighted vertical bound;
- a canonical recursive Bell polynomial for every Gamma derivative, with its exact off-pole
  evaluation and pure-axis leading term `xⁿ`;
- the two-jet projective boundary `Γ(σ + it) / Γ'(σ + it) → 0` and the order-two jet
  boundary `Γ'(σ + it) / Γ''(σ + it) → 0` for `σ > 0`;
- the finite projective-relation limit lemma used by the recurrence collapse.

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/GammaJetProjectiveBoundary.lean` promotes the two-jet
boundary to the **all-orders Gamma-jet projective boundary** (`iteratedDeriv_Gamma_div_succ_vertical_tendsto_zero`):
for every order `n` and every `σ > 0`, `Γ⁽ⁿ⁾(σ + it) / Γ⁽ⁿ⁺¹⁾(σ + it) → 0` as `t → +∞`.  This is
the eq. (5) one-point projective boundary for the canonical Gamma jet tuple.  The full tuple
statement `iteratedDeriv_Gamma_div_higher_vertical_tendsto_zero` (`Γ⁽ⁱ⁾/Γ⁽ʳ⁾ → 0` for every `i < r`)
gives the single constant point `(0, …, 0, 1)` for the normalized jet tuple.  Supporting reusable
lemmas proved along the way: a uniform `MvPolynomial.eval` norm bound (`norm_eval_le_pow`), the
degree bound `totalDegree (pderiv i p) ≤ totalDegree p` (`totalDegree_pderiv_le`, absent in mathlib),
`totalDegree (gammaBell n) ≤ n`, and the leading-term asymptotic `bellVal n (σ+it) / ψⁿ → 1`.

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/GammaFiniteProjectiveLaw.lean` formalizes the remaining
projective-law mechanisms with the mathlib-absent analytic inputs (the coefficient projective
boundary and Bohr return) supplied as explicit hypotheses:

- **eq. (3) lowest-power domination** (`polynomial_lowest_power_tendsto`,
  `polynomial_lowest_power_ratio_tendsto`, `gamma_monomial_valuation_residue`): as `Γ → 0` the
  smallest Gamma power dominates a rational expression `P(Γ)/Q(Γ)`, whose projective normalization
  converges to the leading `L`-coefficient ratio `R(0)/S(0)`;
- **eq. (27) → (29) recurrence collapse**
  (`recurrence_collapse_to_autonomous_eventually`, with
  `recurrence_collapse_to_autonomous` as its all-samples specialization): a finite Gamma-field
  differential relation, translated along Bohr return times and normalized by a projectively
  dominant coefficient, converges to a nontrivial constant-coefficient (autonomous) relation
  (`d_β = 1`), so a recurrent solution differentially algebraic over `K_Γ` satisfies an autonomous
  ADE — this is eq. (8). Eventual nonvanishing of the dominant coefficient is sufficient.

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/GammaFormalResidueField.lean` formalizes the §3 algebraic
residue (eq. 19) on the formal field itself.  Modeling `C(log s)((s⁻¹))` as
`HahnSeries ℤ (RatFunc ℂ)`, `formalResidue_data` gives every nonzero element its leading data
`(a, b, c)` — `a = order`, `b =` the `log s`-degree of the leading Laurent coefficient, `c ∈ ℂˣ` its
leading constant (`formalResidue_const_ne_zero`) — and the residue is a genuine valuation
(`formalResidue_order_mul` additive, `formalResidue_logDegree_mul` additive,
`formalResidue_leadingConst_mul` multiplicative).  The intermediate rational-function law

two multiplicative steps.

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/AlmostPeriodicHull.lean` builds, from scratch (mathlib has
no almost-periodic theory), the **Bohr almost-periodic hull** — the load-bearing object for the

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/HolomorphicJetReturn.lean` builds the **Cauchy jet-return

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/GammaFinitePhaseCapstone.lean` assembles eq. (8):

constants.  Holomorphicity of the translated family is needed only eventually along the return

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/VerticalStripBohrHull.lean` **discharges the capstone's

`S →ᵇ ℂ` for every vertically invariant `S` (`bcfVerticalAction`); `IsVerticalBohrAP` is Bochner's
criterion for it, and `hasVerticalUniformReturn_of_isVerticalBohrAP` converts a relatively compact
orbit into arbitrarily late times at which the underlying function returns *uniformly on `S`*.
`exists_return_seq_tendstoLocallyUniformlyOn_openVerticalStrip` then performs the diagonal choice
over the exhausting substrips of half-widths `η - η/(l+2) ↑ η` with tolerances `1/(l+2) ↓ 0` and
produces a **single** sequence `T l → +∞` along which the vertical translates converge to `f`
locally uniformly on the whole open strip — exactly the `TendstoLocallyUniformlyOn` input of the
capstone.  `verticalStrip_recurrence_collapse` packages the result: from holomorphy on the strip
plus almost periodicity alone, the return sequence exists, and the only surviving input of the
collapse is the coefficient projective boundary.  (`GammaFinitePhaseCapstone` gained the eventual
form `holomorphic_recurrence_collapse_eventually`, of which the original is the specialization.)

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/GammaJetVerticalStripCollapse.lean` closes the loop for the
canonical Gamma jet tuple, with **no analytic hypothesis left**:
`gammaJet_vertical_relation_top_monomial_eq_zero` states that a function holomorphic on the open
strip of half-width `η ≤ σ` about `σ` and Bohr almost periodic there, satisfying
`∑_{i≤r} Γ⁽ⁱ⁾ · M_i(f) = 0`, has `M_r(f) = 0` identically on the strip.  Both remaining inputs are
now theorems: the return sequence from `VerticalStripBohrHull`, and the one-point projective
boundary `(0,…,0,1)` of the jet tuple from `iteratedDeriv_Gamma_div_higher_vertical_tendsto_zero`.
The auxiliary `eventually_iteratedDeriv_Gamma_vertical_ne_zero` proves that every `Γ⁽ʳ⁾` is
eventually nonzero on an upper vertical line of positive abscissa (`Γ` has no zeros, and the digamma
Bell value has leading term `ψʳ` with `‖ψ‖ → ∞`), which supplies the dominant-coefficient
nonvanishing.  This is the first fully unconditional instance of eq. (8) in the tree.

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/GammaVerticalDecay.lean` proves **eq. (24) unconditionally**:
`Γ(σ + it) → 0` as `t → +∞`, for every `σ > 0`.  The mechanism is elementary and avoids Stirling
entirely.  Euler's integral gives the phase-loss bound `‖Γ(s)‖ ≤ Γ(Re s)` on the right half-plane
(`norm_Gamma_le_real_Gamma_re`); applying it one step to the right and pulling back through the
recursion `Γ(s+1) = sΓ(s)`, whose factor has modulus at least `t`, converts the bound into decay
`‖Γ(σ+it)‖ ≤ Γ(σ+1)/t` (`norm_Gamma_vertical_le_pow` at `k = 1`).  Since `Γ` has no zeros, the limit is taken inside
the punctured neighbourhood of the origin, which is the form the monomial valuation needs; this makes
`gamma_monomial_valuation_residue` unconditional
(`gamma_monomial_valuation_residue_of_pos`).

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/GammaRationalProjectiveBoundary.lean` proves **eq. (5) for the
subfield `ℂ(Γ) ⊆ K_Γ`** and the collapse it implies.  A `GammaRationalDatum` records `P = X^u R`,
`Q = X^v S` with `R(0), S(0) ≠ 0`; `gammaRational_ratio_eq` separates the exponential valuation
exactly, `(P₁/Q₁)/(P₂/Q₂) = Γⁿ · (R₁S₂)/(S₁R₂)` with `u₁+v₂ = n + (u₂+v₁)`, and
`gammaRationalDatum_ratio_tendsto` takes the limit along a vertical return sequence: the ratio tends
to `0ⁿ · (residue₁/residue₂)`, i.e. to `0` when the first valuation is strictly larger and to the
residue quotient when the valuations agree.  That is exactly the one-point projective boundary with
the ordering (26) restricted to the `k`-grading.  `gammaRational_vertical_recurrence_collapse` then
gives the second fully unconditional instance of eq. (8): a holomorphic Bohr almost-periodic `f` on
the strip satisfying `∑_α (P_α/Q_α)(Γ)·M_α(f) = 0`, with `β` of minimal valuation, satisfies the
autonomous relation with coefficients `0^{n_α}·(residue_α/residue_β)` and `β`-entry `1`.

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/BohrMeanValue.lean` proves the **existence of the Bohr mean
value** `M(f) = lim_{T→∞}(1/T)∫₀ᵀ f` for every continuous Bohr almost-periodic function into a
Banach space (`exists_tendsto_windowAverage`), together with the boundedness of such a function
(`exists_bound_of_isBohrAlmostPeriodic`) and the packaged `bohrMean` with its defining limit and
uniqueness.  The proof is Bohr's: relative density transports the fixed window `[0,T]` into every
block `[jT,(j+1)T]` at a cost `εT + 2CL`, and summing over the `⌊T'/T⌋` blocks gives
`‖m(T') − m(T)‖ ≤ ε + 2CL/T + 2CT/T'`; choosing `T` large against the inclusion length `L` and then
`T'` large against `T` makes the window averages Cauchy along `atTop`.

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/BohrBochnerEquivalence.lean` proves the **Bohr–Bochner
equivalence** in an elementary form (`isBohrAlmostPeriodic_iff_hasTotallyBoundedOrbit`).
`HasTotallyBoundedOrbit f` says finitely many sample times approximate every translate uniformly.
Bohr ⟹ Bochner uses that a continuous almost-periodic function is uniformly continuous
(`uniformContinuous_of_isBohrAlmostPeriodic`, itself proved by transporting any point into the fixed
compact window `[-1, L+1]`), so the compact parameter window `[0,L]` reduces to a finite grid;
Bochner ⟹ Bohr reads the differences `a − s` between a shifted parameter and its approximating
sample as almost periods, relatively dense with inclusion length the diameter of the sample set.

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/BohrSpectrum.lean` builds the **Bohr spectrum**.  The pair
`t ↦ (f t, g t)` has a totally bounded orbit as soon as both factors do
(`hasTotallyBoundedOrbit_prod`: a translate is classified by the pair of approximating sample times,
and there are finitely many such pairs), so two almost-periodic functions have a relatively dense set
of *common* almost periods (`exists_common_almostPeriods`) — the step Bohr's definition cannot see and
Bochner's makes immediate.  Hence products of almost-periodic functions are almost periodic
(`isBohrAlmostPeriodic_mul`), and since the character `t ↦ e^{-iλt}` is periodic for `λ ≠ 0` and
constant for `λ = 0` (`isBohrAlmostPeriodic_bohrChar`), the modulated function `f(t)e^{-iλt}` is
almost periodic.  Its mean is the **Bohr coefficient** `bohrCoefficient f λ`, the **spectrum**
`bohrSpectrum f` is the set of frequencies with nonzero coefficient, and the **frequency module**
`bohrFrequencyModule f` is its `ℚ`-span — the object whose rational rank eq. (9) bounds, which until
now did not exist in the tree at all.

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/BohrParseval.lean` makes the almost-periodic functions an
algebra with an inner product and proves **Bessel's inequality**.  The class is closed under sums,
differences, complex scalars, conjugation and finite sums; the window average and the mean are
linear and conjugation-equivariant; and the mean of a character is computed exactly
(`bohrMean_bohrChar`: `M(e^{-iλt}) = 0` for `λ ≠ 0` and `1` for `λ = 0`), which gives orthonormality
of the character system (`bohrMean_bohrExp_mul_conj`).  Subtracting the trigonometric polynomial
`p = ∑_{i∈S} a(i) e^{iit}` from `f` and using `0 ≤ M(|f − p|²)` (`bohrMean_mul_conj_re_nonneg`) with
the three mixed means then yields

`∑_{λ ∈ S} |a(λ)|² ≤ M(|f|²)`  (`bohr_bessel`)

for every finite set `S` of frequencies.  This is the first structural constraint on the Bohr
spectrum: above any positive threshold only finitely many frequencies can occur.

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/KroneckerWeylDensity.lean` proves **Kronecker's approximation
theorem** (`kronecker_approx`), which §7 uses when it asserts that rational independence of
`{log p : p ≤ N}` makes the arithmetic translation orbit dense in the prime torus: for a family
`λ : Fin m → ℝ` linearly independent over `ℚ`, the line `t ↦ (e^{iλ_j t})_j` enters every
`ε`-neighbourhood of every point of the torus.  Mathlib has neither Kronecker nor Weyl
equidistribution.

The proof is by a positive kernel and the Bohr mean.  `cosKernel K x = ((1+cos x)/2)^K` equals
`(1+z)^{2K}/(4^K z^K)` for `z = e^{ix}`, so `ofReal_cosKernel_eq_sum` expands it as a trigonometric
polynomial with Fourier coefficients `binom(2K,r)/4^K`.  Expanding the `m`-fold product along the
line (`Finset.prod_univ_sum`) writes it as a finite combination of characters `e^{i⟨v−K,λ⟩t}`, and
`bohrMean_sum_expChar` keeps only the terms of zero frequency; rational independence forces
`v = (K,…,K)`, so `bohrMean_prod_cosKernel` gives the exact value `(binom(2K,K)/4^K)^m`, which is at
least `(2K+1)^{-m}` by `four_pow_le_succ_mul_centralBinom`.  If the line avoided the `ε`-ball around
`θ`, the same product would be at most `(1 − ε²/4)^K` at every time, and an exponentially small
bound cannot dominate a polynomially small one.

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/PrimeTorusKroneckerDensity.lean` instantiates Kronecker at the
prime aperture and thereby **replaces the density assertion of §7 by a theorem**:
`primeTorus_kronecker_dense` and its arithmetic form `primeTorus_cpow_dense` state that for every
aperture `N`, every prescribed phase vector on `{p ≤ N}` and every tolerance, one real time steers all
the phases `p^{it}` simultaneously into the prescribed neighbourhood.  The rational-independence input
is `primeAperture_linearIndependent`, already in the carrier module; `expChar_log_eq_cpow` identifies
the abstract character `e^{it log p}` with the arithmetic phase `p^{it}`.

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/GammaSuperPolynomialDecay.lean` sharpens eq. (24) from decay to
**super-polynomial decay**, which is what §4 actually uses when it opposes the exponential Gamma
direction to the algebraic-logarithmic size of the digamma-field coefficients.  Iterating the
functional equation (`Gamma_add_nat_eq`: `Γ(s+k) = Γ(s)∏_{j<k}(s+j)`), whose `k` factors each have
modulus at least `t`, converts the Euler bound at abscissa `σ+k` into
`‖Γ(σ+it)‖ ≤ Γ(σ+k)/t^k` for **every** `k` (`norm_Gamma_vertical_le_pow`); consequently
`t^n Γ(σ+it) → 0` for every `n` (`Gamma_vertical_tendsto_zero_mul_pow`).

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/GammaPolyValuationBoundary.lean` formalizes the **lexicographic
ordering (26)** on the `s`-monomial weighted part of `ℂ(s)(Γ)`, i.e. on coefficients `s^a·P(Γ)/Q(Γ)`.
`tendsto_pow_mul_Gamma_pow` is the load-bearing fact — on a vertical line of positive abscissa,
`s^n Γ(s)^k → 0` for every `n` and every `k ≥ 1` — so the Gamma direction beats the polynomial
direction outright.  The three clauses of (26) are then theorems:

- `gammaRationalDatum_polyRatio_tendsto_zero_of_gap`: a strictly larger Gamma valuation in the
  numerator sends the normalized ratio to `0` whatever powers of `s` accompany it;
- `gammaRationalDatum_polyRatio_tendsto_zero_of_sPow`: at equal Gamma valuation, the smaller power of
  `s` in the numerator sends it to `0`;
- `gammaRationalDatum_polyRatio_tendsto_residue`: at equal Gamma valuation and equal `s` power the
  limit is the residue quotient.

`gammaRationalDatum_ratio_vertical_tendsto` is the vertical-line form of the `ℂ(Γ)` boundary used
throughout, and `tendsto_inv_pow_vertical` supplies the monomial comparison.

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/GammaLogValuationBoundary.lean` adds the **logarithmic
coordinate** of eq. (19)/(26), so the ordering is now formalized on the full algebraic-logarithmic
scale `s^a (log s)^b` attached to a Gamma-rational factor.  Two estimates carry it:
`norm_log_le_of_one_le` (`‖log s‖ ≤ log‖s‖ + π` on the vertical line) gives
`tendsto_log_pow_div_pow_vertical` — the logarithm is dominated by every positive power of `s` — and
`tendsto_norm_log_vertical_atTop` gives `‖log s‖ → ∞`.  Consequently:

- `tendsto_pow_mul_log_pow_mul_Gamma_pow`: `s^n (log s)^b Γ(s)^k → 0` for every `n`, `b` and every
  `k ≥ 1`, so the Gamma direction still beats the whole algebraic-logarithmic scale;
- `tendsto_logMonomial_ratio_zero`: the monomials `s^a (log s)^b` are ordered lexicographically —
  smaller `a` wins, and at equal `a` smaller `b` wins;
- `gammaRationalDatum_logRatio_tendsto_zero_of_lex` and
  `gammaRationalDatum_logRatio_tendsto_residue`: at equal Gamma valuation, a strictly smaller
  algebraic-logarithmic monomial sends the normalized ratio to `0`, and equal monomials give the
  residue quotient.

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/GammaLogStripCollapse.lean` assembles the ordering and the
Bohr hull into the **widest unconditional instance of eq. (8) so far**.  `StrictlySubordinateAt` is
exactly the lexicographic relation of (26) — strictly larger Gamma valuation, or equal valuation with
a lexicographically smaller monomial `s^a(log s)^b` — and
`gammaRationalDatum_logRatio_tendsto_zero_of_subordinate` proves that subordination sends the
normalized coefficient ratio to `0` (its gap branch is the new
`gammaRationalDatum_logRatio_tendsto_zero_of_gap`, where the whole algebraic-logarithmic monomial is
absorbed by `tendsto_pow_mul_log_pow_mul_Gamma_pow`).  With `logFn_eventually_ne_zero` supplying
dominant-coefficient nonvanishing and `tendsto_vertical_along_returns` transporting vertical-line
limits to a return sequence based at any point of the strip,
`gammaLog_strip_relation_dominant_monomial_eq_zero` states: a holomorphic Bohr almost-periodic `f` on
the strip of half-width `η ≤ σ` satisfying
`∑_α s^{a α}(log s)^{b α}·(P_α/Q_α)(Γ)·M_α(f) = 0`, with the coefficient at `β` strictly dominating
all the others, has `M_β(f) = 0` identically on the strip.  No analytic input remains hypothetical.

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/DigammaValuationBoundary.lean` frees the ordering from the
auxiliary logarithm and lands it on the **actual generator of the digamma field**.  What the ordering
really uses is isolated as `IsVerticalLogWeight w σ`: the weight grows without bound and is dominated
by every positive power of `s`.  Both `Complex.log` (`isVerticalLogWeight_log`) and `Complex.digamma`
(`isVerticalLogWeight_digamma`) satisfy it — for `ψ` the domination comes from the corpus vertical
asymptotic `ψ(σ+it) − (log t + iπ/2) → 0`, which gives `‖ψ‖ ≤ 2 log t` eventually
(`norm_digamma_vertical_le_two_log`), hence `ψ^b/s^d → 0` for every `d ≥ 1`
(`tendsto_digamma_pow_div_pow_vertical`), while `Re ψ → +∞` gives the growth.

The whole ordering is then reproved for an arbitrary weight — `tendsto_pow_mul_weight_pow_mul_Gamma_pow`
(a positive power of `Γ` annihilates every weighted monomial), `tendsto_weightedMonomial_ratio_zero`
(lexicographic comparison), `weightedRatio_tendsto_zero_of_subordinate` — and
`weighted_strip_relation_dominant_monomial_eq_zero` is the corresponding unconditional collapse.  Its
specialization `digamma_strip_relation_dominant_monomial_eq_zero` is eq. (8) for coefficients
`s^a ψ(s)^b · P(Γ)/Q(Γ)`: monomials of the genuine field `ℂ(s)⟨ψ⟩(Γ)`, no auxiliary scale involved.

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/PolynomialWeightedValuationBoundary.lean` removes the last
artificial restriction on the `s`-part: the coefficient's algebraic factor may be an **arbitrary
polynomial**, not a monomial.  `tendsto_polynomial_eval_div_pow_vertical` proves that on a vertical
line a polynomial is asymptotically its leading monomial, `c(s)/s^{deg c} → lead c` (expand
`c.eval s / s^{deg c} = ∑_{i≤deg} c_i (s^{deg-i})⁻¹` and use `tendsto_inv_pow_vertical` termwise), so
`polyWeightedFn_ratio_eq` factors every normalized ratio as (prefactor ratio)·(monomial ratio) with
the prefactor ratio converging to `lead c₁ / lead c₂`.  The ordering therefore transfers verbatim:
`polyWeightedRatio_tendsto_zero_of_subordinate`, and the collapse
`polyWeighted_strip_relation_dominant_monomial_eq_zero` — with its digamma specialization
`digammaPoly_strip_relation_dominant_monomial_eq_zero` — hold for coefficients
`c(s)·ψ(s)^b·P(Γ)/Q(Γ)` with `c ∈ ℂ[s]` arbitrary nonzero, the lexicographic comparison being run on
the Gamma valuation and then on `(deg c, b)`.

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/GammaSumValuationBoundary.lean` lifts the ordering from single
monomials to **finite sums** of them, which is the shape an actual coefficient has.  Dividing a sum
by its dominant term makes every other summand tend to zero
(`tendsto_polyWeightedTerm_div_dominant`), so the sum is asymptotic to that term
(`tendsto_polyWeightedSum_div_dominant`).  Three consequences:

- **no cancellation** (`polyWeightedSum_eventually_ne_zero`): a sum of graded monomials with a
  strictly dominant term is eventually nonvanishing on a vertical line.  For the graded monomial
  algebra this is a *theorem* — the transfer step of §3 with no appeal to hypertranscendence of the
  formal Stirling series, which is only needed for coefficients outside this algebra;
- the leading behaviour of such a sum is computable — it is that of its dominant monomial;
- `tendsto_polyWeightedSum_ratio`: the projective boundary of two sums is the boundary of their
  dominant monomials, so eq. (5) extends from monomial coefficients to arbitrary elements of the
  graded algebra.

`GammaRationalDatum.one` records the trivial datum, so pure `ℂ[s][ψ]` elements are the special case
with no Gamma factor.

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/GammaGradedStripCollapse.lean` is the capstone of this line:
eq. (8) for the **entire graded coefficient algebra**.  `gradedSum_strip_relation_dominant_monomial_eq_zero`
takes coefficients `F_α = ∑_q c_{α q}(s)·w(s)^{b_{α q}}·P_{α q}(Γ)/Q_{α q}(Γ)` — arbitrary finite
sums of graded monomials — with a dominant monomial `m α` inside each `F_α` and the requirement that
`m β` strictly dominate every `m α`.  Since each sum is asymptotic to its own dominant monomial, the
projective comparison of coefficients reduces to that of the dominants, and the conclusion is again
`M_β(f) = 0` identically on the strip.  Its specialization
`digammaGraded_strip_relation_dominant_monomial_eq_zero` is the statement for the actual digamma
field.  No analytic input is hypothetical: return times come from almost periodicity, the coefficient
boundary from the valuation ordering.

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/BohrDerivativeSpectrum.lean` supplies the two spectral
computations any differential-algebraic argument over the Bohr spectrum needs.
`bohrCoefficient_deriv` proves the differentiation rule `a_{f'}(λ) = iλ·a_f(λ)`: integrating by parts
against the character moves the derivative onto `e^{-iλt}` and leaves a boundary term of size `O(1)`,
which the window average kills.  Its corollaries are `bohrMean_deriv_eq_zero` (the mean of a
derivative vanishes) and `bohrSpectrum_deriv_subset` (`spec f' ⊆ spec f`), the closure property that
a relation `∑ d_α ∏_j (f⁽ʲ⁾)^{α_j} = 0` acts on.

Dually, `bohrCoefficient_sum_expChar` computes the coefficients of a trigonometric polynomial to be
its own coefficients, so `bohrSpectrum_sum_expChar_subset` puts its spectrum inside its finite
frequency set and `bohrFrequencyModule_sum_expChar_le` puts its frequency module inside the `ℚ`-span
of that set — whose rank `FrequencyRank.frequencyRank_le_card` already bounds by the number of terms.
This is the concrete instance showing the finite-phase conclusion of eq. (9) is attained rather than
vacuous.

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/LinearOdeBohrSpectrum.lean` proves **eq. (9) in the linear
autonomous case, unconditionally** — the first genuine piece of the finite-phase conclusion itself
rather than of its hypotheses.  Applying the coefficient functional to a relation
`∑_j d_j f⁽ʲ⁾ = 0` with constant `d_j`, and using `a_{f⁽ʲ⁾}(λ) = (iλ)^j a_f(λ)`
(`bohrCoefficient_iterate`, from the differentiation rule), gives

`(∑_j d_j (iλ)^j) · a_f(λ) = 0`   (`linearOde_char_eq_zero`),

so every Bohr frequency of a solution is a characteristic root.  A nonzero characteristic polynomial
has finitely many roots and `λ ↦ iλ` is injective, hence the spectrum is finite
(`bohrSpectrum_finite_of_linearOde`) and the frequency module is finitely generated
(`bohrFrequencyModule_fg_of_linearOde`).  Supporting linearity lemmas — `bohrCoefficient_finsetSum`,
`bohrCoefficient_const_mul`, `bohrCoefficient_of_eq_zero` — are proved here as well.

The nonlinear case needs the topological argument (jet embedding, covering dimension, Pontryagin
duality); the linear case needs none of it.  Both are now available: the dimension step is Lean-native
(see the Brouwer section), and the finite-rank conclusion for arbitrary jet monomials is
`finite_phase_law_of_gammaField_relation`.

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/GammaLinearStripDerivative.lean` reads the graded collapse on
the derivatives themselves.  Specializing the differential monomials to `M_j(f) = f⁽ʲ⁾`
(`diffMonomial_single`) turns the abstract conclusion into a statement about an actual derivative:
`gradedLinear_strip_iteratedDeriv_eq_zero` says that a holomorphic Bohr almost-periodic `f` on the
strip satisfying `∑_j F_j(s)·f⁽ʲ⁾(s) = 0`, with graded coefficients and a strictly dominant `F_β`,
has `f⁽ᵝ⁾ ≡ 0` on the strip; `digammaLinear_strip_iteratedDeriv_eq_zero` is the digamma case, and
`gradedLinear_strip_deriv_eq_zero` the first-order case, where `f' ≡ 0` and `f` is locally constant.
This is the concrete, checkable form of eq. (8) for linear relations: such an `f` admits no dominant
linear Gamma-differential constraint except a degenerate one.

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/BohrProductSpectrum.lean` settles how the spectrum behaves
under multiplication in the case where it is exact and elementary — a trigonometric-polynomial
factor.  `bohrCoefficient_mul_expChar` shows modulation translates the coefficient function
(`a_{f·e^{iμt}}(λ) = a_f(λ−μ)`), and `bohrCoefficient_mul_trigPoly` gives the convolution formula
`a_{f·g}(λ) = ∑_i c_i a_f(λ−μ_i)` for `g = ∑_i c_i e^{iμ_i t}`.  Hence
`bohrSpectrum_mul_trigPoly_subset` (`spec(f·g) ⊆ spec f + {μ_i}`) and, most usefully,
`bohrFrequencyModule_mul_trigPoly_le`: multiplying by a trigonometric polynomial enlarges the
frequency module by **at most finitely many generators**, the module of the product lying in
`Γ_B(f) ⊔ span_ℚ{μ_i}`.  This is the multiplicative half of the mechanism behind the finite-rank
conclusion.  The general product rule is now proved outright in
`BohrFrequencyModuleProduct.lean` (see below), so nothing remains open on this branch.

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/BohrMeanInvariance.lean` records the two structural
properties of the mean that any approximation argument runs on.  It is a **contraction** for the
supremum norm (`norm_bohrMean_le`), hence the mean and every Bohr coefficient are `1`-Lipschitz in
the uniform distance (`norm_bohrMean_sub_le`, `norm_bohrCoefficient_sub_le`) — the estimate that will
transfer facts from trigonometric polynomials to their uniform limits.  And it is **translation
invariant** (`bohrMean_comp_add_const`: shifting the window changes the integral only by two end
pieces of bounded size, which the average kills), so translation multiplies each coefficient by the
unimodular factor `e^{iλa}` (`bohrCoefficient_comp_add_const`) and leaves the spectrum, hence the
frequency module, unchanged (`bohrSpectrum_comp_add_const`).

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/BohrConvolution.lean` proves the **Fourier-mode extraction
identity**, the mechanism the Bochner–Fejér construction runs on.  Averaging a translate of `f`
against a character extracts exactly that mode,
`M_u(f(u+t)·e^{-iμu}) = a_f(μ)·e^{iμt}` (`bohrMean_translate_mul_bohrChar`, an immediate consequence
of translation invariance), and against a trigonometric kernel `K = ∑_i c_i e^{-iμ_i u}` the same
computation returns the corresponding weighted partial sum of the Bohr–Fourier series,
`M_u(f(u+t)·K(u)) = ∑_i c_i a_f(μ_i) e^{iμ_i t}` (`bohrMean_translate_mul_trigKernel`).  Every such
convolution is therefore a trigonometric polynomial with exactly the right coefficients
(`bohrCoefficient_trigKernel_convolution`); what remains for the approximation theorem is only the
choice of weights making these polynomials converge uniformly to `f`.  (That choice is now made:
`exists_trigPoly_spectrum_approx`, below.)  In particular
`bohrMean_translate_mul_trigKernel_eq_zero`: if every Bohr coefficient vanishes then every such
convolution vanishes identically — the uniqueness theorem is exactly the missing approximation step
away.

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/BohrFejerKernel.lean` constructs the **Fejér kernel on an
arithmetic frequency progression** `βℤ`, the summation kernel the Bochner–Fejér approximation runs
on.  Realized as `|D_N|²/(N+1)` with `D_N(u) = ∑_{k≤N} e^{ikβu}`, it is a nonnegative real
(`fejerKernel_eq_ofReal`, `fejerKernel_nonneg`), a trigonometric polynomial with frequencies in `βℤ`
(`fejerKernel_eq_sum`), and has mean exactly one (`bohrMean_fejerKernel` — only the diagonal terms of
the double sum carry zero frequency, and there are `N+1` of them).  Convolving `f` against it
(`bohrMean_translate_mul_fejerKernel`) therefore returns the **Fejér mean of the Bohr–Fourier series
of `f` along `βℤ`**: a genuine trigonometric polynomial whose coefficients are the Bohr coefficients
of `f` at the frequencies `(j−k)β`, weighted by `1/(N+1)`.

*(Historical: the approximation theorem, uniqueness and the product rule were completed by the
Peter–Weyl/Stone–Weierstrass route — `exists_char_combination_approx`, `exists_trig_approx`,
`eq_zero_of_bohrCoefficient_eq_zero`, `BohrFrequencyModuleProduct` — so the Fejér route is no longer
needed for them.  It remains the route to approximation with frequencies in the **spectrum** rather
than in the frequency group.)*

The first step of that route is now proved.  `HTGeometry/BohrLatticeAlmostPeriod.lean`:

> **`norm_translate_sub_le_of_freq_lattice`**: if every hull frequency of `f` lies in `βℤ`, then every
> translation by the dual lattice `(2π/β)ℤ` moves `f` by at most twice the approximation error.

Every approximating character is fixed by such a translation — its frequency times the translation is
an integer multiple of `2π` — so `f` is moved only by the two approximation errors.  That is exactly
what makes the Fejér kernel on `βℤ`, whose peaks sit on the dual lattice, an approximate identity for
such an `f`: at the peaks the function barely moves, and off them the kernel is small
(`fejerKernel_norm_le_of_cos_le`).

The two statements are joined by `HTGeometry/CosineLatticeDistance.lean`:

> **`exists_int_abs_sub_two_pi_mul_le`**: `1 − cos θ ≤ c` forces `θ` to lie within `π√(c/2)` of
> `2πℤ`.

(`1 − cos θ = 2 sin²(θ/2)`, and Jordan's inequality `2x/π ≤ sin x` on `[0, π/2]` converts the sine
bound into a distance bound, after reducing `θ` modulo `2π` by rounding.)  The kernel's smallness
criterion is stated through the cosine and its peaks live on the dual lattice; this is the conversion
between the two, and with it the pointwise bound `K_N·‖f(t+u) − f(t)‖ ≤ K_N·(2ε + ω(δ)) + 2‖f‖·B_N`
needs no cutoff function — which is what makes the Bohr mean of the estimate available.

Taking that mean needs monotonicity, which `HTGeometry/BohrMeanMonotone.lean` supplies:

> **`bohrMean_le_bohrMean`** — window averages over `[0,T]` are monotone in the integrand and the mean
> is their limit, so `g ≤ h` pointwise forces `M(g) ≤ M(h)` for almost periodic `g, h`; and
> `bohrMean_nonneg` for the nonnegative case.

The same file proves the other half of the transfer: **`norm_bohrMean_le_bohrMean_norm`**, that
`‖M(g)‖ ≤ M(‖g‖)` (the norm of a window average is at most the window average of the norm, and both
converge), with `isBohrAlmostPeriodic_norm` — the norm of an almost periodic function is almost
periodic — supplying the mean on the right.  The corpus previously had only the cruder
`norm_bohrMean_le`, which bounds `‖M(g)‖` by a *uniform* bound on `g`; that is too weak here, since
the Fejér kernel's supremum grows with `N`.  `bohrMean_ofReal` and `isBohrAlmostPeriodic_ofReal`
complete the bookkeeping: the kernel is real and nonnegative, so its mean may be computed in `ℝ`
where the monotonicity lives and transported to `ℂ` where the convolution lives.  The real-valued
linearity the estimate needs — `bohrMean_const_real`, `bohrMean_const_mul_real`,
`bohrMean_add_const_real` — is proved there too; the corpus's existing linearity lemmas
(`bohrMean_add`, `bohrMean_sub`, `bohrMean_const_mul`) are all complex-valued.

With the kernel's mean equal to one (`bohrMean_fejerKernel`) the estimate becomes
`‖K_N * f(t) − f(t)‖ ≤ 2ε + ω(δ) + 2‖f‖·B_N`, and `tendsto_fejerBound` sends the last term to zero.

The estimate itself is `HTGeometry/BohrFejerPointwise.lean`:

> **`fejer_pointwise_bound`**: for every `t` and `u`,
> `K_N(u)·‖f(t+u) − f(t)‖ ≤ K_N(u)·(2ε + ω) + 2‖f‖·2/((N+1)c)`.

The proof is the two-case split with no cutoff: if `cos(βu) ≤ 1 − c` the kernel is at most
`2/((N+1)c)` (`fejerKernel_norm_le_of_cos_le`) and the increment at most `2‖f‖`; otherwise
`exists_int_abs_sub_two_pi_mul_le` puts `u` within `ρ` of a dual-lattice point, where
`norm_translate_sub_le_of_freq_lattice` gives `2ε` and the modulus of continuity gives `ω`.

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/BohrFejerConcentration.lean` supplies the third
approximate-identity property, **concentration**.  Summing the geometric series
(`fejerSum_eq_geom`, `expChar_nat_mul`) gives `D_N(u) = (z^{N+1}−1)/(z−1)` with `z = e^{iβu}` of
modulus one, so `‖D_N(u)‖ ≤ 2/‖z−1‖` and
`F_N(u) ≤ 4/((N+1)(2 − 2cos βu))` (`fejerKernel_norm_le`, using
`norm_expChar_sub_one_sq : ‖e^{iβu} − 1‖² = 2 − 2cos βu`).  Off any neighbourhood of the kernel's
peaks, `cos(βu) ≤ 1 − δ`, this is at most `2/((N+1)δ)` (`fejerKernel_norm_le_of_cos_le`), a bound
independent of `u` that tends to zero (`tendsto_fejerBound`).

The Fejér kernel is therefore a genuine approximate identity on the progression `βℤ`: nonnegative,
of mean one, and uniformly small away from its peaks.  The remaining step is the multi-dimensional
kernel over a rationally independent frequency basis, whose peaks are almost periods of `f`, and the
resulting uniform convergence.

### The one-dimensional Bochner–Fejér theorem is closed

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/BohrFejerConvergence.lean` averages the pointwise estimate and
so completes the convergence step on a single progression:

> **`norm_bohrMean_fejer_sub_le`**: if every hull frequency of `f` lies in `βℤ`, then
> `‖M_u(K_N(u)·f(t+u)) − f(t)‖ ≤ 2ε + ω + 2‖f‖·2/((N+1)c)` for every `t`,

with `ε` the almost-period tolerance, `ω` the modulus of continuity on the scale `ρ`, and `c` any
concentration level compatible with `ρ` through `π√(c/2) ≤ |β|ρ`.  The mechanism: the kernel has mean
one, so `M(K·f(t+·)) − f(t) = M(K·(f(t+·) − f(t)))` (`bohrMean_sub`, `bohrMean_const_mul`,
`bohrMean_fejerKernel`); `norm_bohrMean_le_bohrMean_norm` passes to the mean of the norm;
`fejer_pointwise_bound` bounds the integrand; and `bohrMean_le_bohrMean` integrates the bound, whose
mean is computed by `bohrMean_const_mul_real`, `bohrMean_add_const_real` and the kernel's mean one.
The two supporting facts about the kernel that the mean calculus needs are proved in the same file —
**`isBohrAlmostPeriodic_fejerKernel`** (it is a trigonometric polynomial, by `fejerKernel_eq_sum`),
and its real form's continuity, almost periodicity and unit mean (**`continuous_fejerKernelReal`**,
**`isBohrAlmostPeriodic_fejerKernelReal`**, **`bohrMean_fejerKernelReal`**).

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/BohrFejerPolynomial.lean` then identifies *what* the Fejér mean
is and draws the approximation theorem in the form the rank estimates need — with frequencies in the
**spectrum**, not merely in the frequency group:

> **`bohrMean_fejerKernel_translate`**: `M_u(K_N(u)·f(t+u)) = ∑_p ((N+1)⁻¹·a_f(μ_p))·e^{iμ_p t}` over
> the index square, `μ_p = (p₂ − p₁)β`.
>
> **`exists_spectrum_trigPoly_of_coeff_sum`**: any trigonometric sum whose coefficients carry a factor
> `a_f(μ)` regroups onto a finite set of *distinct* frequencies all lying in `bohrSpectrum f`.
>
> **`exists_trigPoly_spectrum_approx_lattice`**: if every hull frequency of `f` lies in `βℤ`, then for
> every `δ > 0` there are a finite `S ⊆ bohrSpectrum f` and coefficients with
> `‖f(t) − ∑_{λ∈S} a_λ e^{iλt}‖ ≤ δ` for all `t`.

The first is `bohrMean_translate_mul_trigKernel` (`BohrConvolution.lean`) applied to the kernel's
expansion, after `expChar_eq_bohrChar` converts the characters.  The second is a fibrewise regrouping
(`Finset.sum_fiberwise_of_maps_to`) followed by deletion of the frequencies where `a_f` vanishes —
those contribute a zero coefficient.  The third fixes the free parameters of the convergence bound
against `δ`: uniform continuity supplies `ρ` and `ω = δ/3`, the compatibility `π√(c/2) ≤ |β|ρ` is met
with equality by `c = 2(|β|ρ/π)²`, and `N` is chosen with `(N+1) > 12‖f‖/(δc)` to make the tail
`δ/3`.

### The Bochner–Fejér approximation theorem, in full

The general case — no hypothesis on the frequencies at all — is now proved, and with the sharp
conclusion.

> **`exists_trigPoly_spectrum_approx`** (`HTGeometry/BohrBochnerFejerApprox.lean`): for an almost
> periodic `f` and every `δ > 0` there are a finite `S ⊆ bohrSpectrum f` and coefficients `a` with
> `‖f(t) − ∑_{λ∈S} a_λ e^{iλt}‖ ≤ δ` for every `t`.

The route is the classical one, and it does *not* go through the one-progression convergence bound.
Peter–Weyl (`exists_trig_approx`) supplies a trigonometric approximant `P` with no control on its
frequencies; the Bochner–Fejér operator `T_N g(t) = M_u(g(u+t)K_N(u))` then repairs the frequencies
without spoiling the approximation, because:

1. **it is a contraction** — `norm_bohrMean_translate_mul_multiFejerKernel_le`: the kernel is
   nonnegative with mean one, so `‖T_N g‖ ≤ sup‖g‖`.  The real form of the kernel and its unit mean
   are proved in the same file (`multiFejerKernelReal`, `bohrMean_multiFejerKernelReal`), together
   with the descent lemmas `continuous_of_ofReal`, `isBohrAlmostPeriodic_of_ofReal`;
2. **it acts diagonally on characters** — `bohrMean_translate_expChar_mul_multiFejerKernel`:
   `T_N(e^{iλ·}) = W·e^{iλ·}` for `λ = ∑_i m_iβ_i` an integer combination of the `ℚ`-independent
   base, with `W = ∏_i(1 − |m_i|/(N_i+1))` the classical **Fejér multiplier** (`fejerMultiplier`).
   This is proved in `HTGeometry/BohrMultiFejerMultiplier.lean` by counting the kernel frequencies
   that contribute: rational independence forces `∑_j(b_j−a_j)β_j = ∑_j m_jβ_j` to hold coordinatewise
   (`multiFejer_fibre_iff`), so the fibre is a product of one-dimensional fibres, each of cardinality
   `N+1−|m|` (`card_fejerFibre`);
3. **its multipliers tend to one** — `fejerMultiplier_nonneg`, `fejerMultiplier_le_one` and
   `one_sub_fejerMultiplier_le`: `1 − ∏_i x_i ≤ ∑_i(1 − x_i)` for `x_i ∈ [0,1]`
   (`one_sub_prod_le_sum_one_sub`), so the defect is at most `∑_i |m_i|/(N_i+1)`;
4. **its output is spectral** — the corpus's `bohrMean_translate_mul_multiFejerKernel` writes `T_N f`
   as a trigonometric polynomial each of whose coefficients carries a factor `a_f(μ)`, and
   `exists_spectrum_trigPoly_of_coeff_sum` regroups it onto distinct frequencies in the spectrum.

The missing input for step 2 was a **rational base**: `HTGeometry/RationalBasisIntegerCombination.lean`
proves **`exists_ratBasis_int_combination`** — any finite family of reals consists of *integer*
combinations of a finite `ℚ`-linearly independent family.  A maximal independent subfamily is
extracted with `exists_linearIndependent`, each member is written over it with rational coefficients
(`Submodule.mem_span_range_iff_exists_fun`), and the base is divided by the product of the
denominators (`exists_int_nat_mul`), which preserves independence (`linearIndependent_const_mul`).

The assembly is then `‖f − T_N f‖ ≤ ‖f − P‖ + ‖P − T_N P‖ + ‖T_N(P − f)‖ ≤ δ/3 + δ/3 + δ/3`, with the
orders `N` chosen large enough that the multiplier defect times the coefficient mass is below `δ/3`
(`bohrMean_translate_trigPoly_mul_multiFejerKernel` computes the middle term exactly).

This closes the first of the two "remaining classical theorems" of §6 in its strong form: not merely
approximation by trigonometric polynomials, but approximation **supported on the spectrum**, which is
what the frequency-module and rank estimates consume.

### Bohr's module theorem: the hull frequency group *is* the group generated by the spectrum

The card's law bounds `Module.rank ℤ (bohrHullFrequencies f)` — the group of frequencies whose
characters are uniformly continuous for the translation distance of `f`.  That group is now proved
equal to the group generated by the **spectrum**, so the conclusion is a statement about the
Bohr–Fourier expansion itself:

> **`bohrHullFrequencies_eq_closure_bohrSpectrum`** (`HTGeometry/BohrModuleTheorem.lean`):
> `bohrHullFrequencies f = AddSubgroup.closure (bohrSpectrum f)` for every almost periodic `f`;
> **`rank_closure_bohrSpectrum_eq`** transports the rank.

The easy inclusion was already in the corpus (`closure_bohrSpectrum_le_bohrHullFrequencies`).  The
converse — **`bohrHullFrequencies_le_closure_bohrSpectrum`** — is the classical module theorem, and
it is proved here by the Kronecker route, in four new modules:

1. `HTGeometry/RealFiniteSubgroupBasis.lean` — **`exists_ratIndep_zBasis`**: the group generated by
   finitely many reals has a `ℚ`-linearly independent `ℤ`-basis.  The group is a finitely generated
   torsion-free `ℤ`-module, hence free (`Module.free_of_finite_type_torsion_free'`), and a `ℤ`-basis
   is `ℚ`-independent because a rational relation clears denominators to an integral one.  This is
   what lets a finite frequency packet be replaced by a base **without enlarging the group it
   generates**.
2. `HTGeometry/CirclePowerEstimates.lean` — **`norm_pow_sub_pow_le_of_norm_one`** and
   **`norm_zpow_sub_zpow_le_of_norm_one`** (on the unit circle the `c`-th power is `|c|`-Lipschitz),
   and **`exists_nat_cos_le_half`**: a phase outside `2πℤ` has a multiple with cosine at most `1/2`,
   i.e. a multiple at distance `≥ 1` from `1`.  The last is what makes a nontrivial phase detectable
   at a *universal* tolerance, however small the phase itself is — the step that keeps the
   quantifiers of the final argument non-circular.
3. `HTGeometry/KroneckerPhaseWitness.lean` — **`exists_phase_witness`**: for `β` rationally
   independent and `μ ∉ span_ℤ β` there is a fixed `φ ∉ 2πℤ` such that, at every tolerance, some `τ`
   has all `|e^{iβ_jτ} − 1|` small while `e^{iμτ}` is close to `e^{iφ}`.  If `μ ∉ span_ℚ β` the family
   `(β, μ)` is rationally independent and the corpus's `kronecker_approx` hits `(0,…,0,π)` directly.
   Otherwise `μ = ∑ q_jβ_j` with rational, not all integral, `q_j`; running Kronecker on the scaled
   base `β/N` (`N` a common denominator) with targets `2πa_j/N` leaves the integers `a_j` free, and
   the `μ`-phase comes out as `e^{2πi∑a_jq_j}` — taken `≠ 1` by supporting `a` where `q_j ∉ ℤ`.
4. `HTGeometry/BohrModuleTheorem.lean` — the assembly.
   **`norm_expChar_intComb_sub_one_le`** propagates "base characters near one" to integral
   combinations, and **`norm_translate_sub_le_of_spectral_approx`** turns that into an almost period
   of `f` through the spectral approximation `exists_trigPoly_spectrum_approx`.  Given a hull
   frequency `μ` outside `⟨spec f⟩`: approximate `f` to accuracy `δ/8` with spectral frequencies,
   replace them by the base of (1), note `μ` is still outside its integral span, take the phase
   witness of (3) and the multiple `m` of (2), and apply the witness at a tolerance chosen *after*
   `m`.  Then `mτ` is an almost period of `f` at which `‖e^{iμmτ} − 1‖ ≥ 1/2` — contradicting the
   uniform continuity of `t ↦ e^{iμt}` at tolerance `1/2`.

### The law in frequency-module form: rational rank

The card states eq. (9) with the **rational rank of the frequency module**; the Lean capstones
produce the `ℤ`-rank of the hull frequency group.  The two are now connected, so the Lean statement
says on the nose what the paper claims.

`HTGeometry/RationalRankFromIntegralRank.lean` proves the general fact:

> **`exists_nat_mul_mem_span_int`** — an element of the rational span becomes integral after
> multiplication by a positive integer;
> **`rank_span_rat_le_of_rank_closure_le`** — a bound on `Module.rank ℤ ⟨S⟩` bounds
> `Module.rank ℚ (span_ℚ S)`.

An independent family in the rational span is rescaled coordinatewise into the group; the rescaling
is injective on independent families and preserves independence, so `rank_le` transports the bound.

`HTGeometry/GammaFieldRationalPhaseRank.lean` applies it through the module theorem:

> **`rank_bohrFrequencyModule_le_of_hull_rank_le`** — `Module.rank ℤ (bohrHullFrequencies f) ≤ K`
> implies `Module.rank ℚ (bohrFrequencyModule f) ≤ K`;
> **`finite_rational_phase_rank_of_gammaField_relation`** — the finite phase law in exactly the
> card's form: a relation with coefficients in `ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩` forces the Bohr–Fourier expansion of the
> solution along the line to carry only finitely many rationally independent frequencies.

The same file carries the law in its **final, hypothesis-minimal form**, with no bundled line
restriction and no auxiliary analytic hypotheses at all:

> **`finite_rational_phase_rank_of_gammaField_relation_line`**: for `Φ` holomorphic and almost
> periodic on the strip of half-width `R` about `σ > 0`, jet exponents distinct, `Ψ` the germ of `Φ`
> in the upper-end carrier and coefficients `x_α ∈ ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩ \ {0}` with
> `∑_α x_α ∏_i (δⁱΨ)^{e_{αi}} = 0`,
> `∃ K : ℕ, Module.rank ℚ (bohrFrequencyModule (t ↦ Φ(σ+it))) ≤ K`.

Uniform continuity and relative compactness of the orbit are *derived* from strip almost periodicity
(`isBohrAlmostPeriodic_line`, `uniformContinuous_of_isBohrAlmostPeriodic`,
`hasTotallyBoundedOrbit_of_isBohrAlmostPeriodic`), and the Borel structure on the hull is chosen
inside the proof.  This is eq. (9) of the card, with every hypothesis about `Φ` and the relation and
nothing else.

`HTGeometry/RationalPhaseRankConsequences.lean` draws the two readings of that bound:

> **`rank_bohrFrequencyModule_le_of_dominated`** — a dominated observable of the solution (any
> continuous translation-equivariant reading of it, e.g. a Lipschitz function of its values) has no
> larger frequency group, hence also finitely many rationally independent Bohr–Fourier frequencies:
> the divisor clause of the law in frequency-module form;
> **`bohrSpectrum_expChar`** and **`rank_bohrFrequencyModule_expChar`** — the spectrum of a character
> is its own frequency and its frequency module has rank exactly one, so the rational-rank statement
> is attained and not vacuous.  The same holds for the corpus's own witness of the law:
> **`bohrSpectrum_expLineBC`** and **`rank_bohrFrequencyModule_expLineBC`** — the exponential line
> `z ↦ e^{μz}`, which `finite_phase_law_gammaJet_expLine` shows satisfies the law's hypotheses over
> Gamma-jet data, has frequency module of rank exactly one.

The **explicit** bound of eq. (7)/(9) also transfers.
`HTGeometry/JetSampleRationalRankBound.lean` proves

> **`rank_bohrFrequencyModule_le_of_separant_jet_relation`**: for `F` holomorphic and strip almost
> periodic satisfying a polynomial jet relation whose separant does not vanish on the carrier,
> `Module.rank ℚ (bohrFrequencyModule (t ↦ F(σ+it))) ≤ 2(n+1)`,

with the Borel structure on the hull chosen inside the proof and the orbit hypotheses derived from
strip almost periodicity — the contour jet sample embeds the carrier in `ℂ^{n+1}`, and the module
theorem turns the resulting `ℤ`-rank bound into the rational rank of the Bohr–Fourier frequencies.

### Eq. (32) with its finite-phase input supplied

`HTGeometry/GammaFieldPrimeFrequencyExclusion.lean` closes the transcendence criterion.  The corpus's
`prime_frequency_forces_transcendence` takes the finite-phase implication as a hypothesis; the law now
supplies it:

> **`module_finite_bohrFrequencyModule_of_rank_le`** — finite rational rank means finitely generated;
> **`no_gammaField_relation_of_primeLog_frequencies`** — a function holomorphic and almost periodic on
> a vertical strip whose Bohr frequency module along the line is the full prime-log span satisfies
> **no** nontrivial relation with coefficients in `ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩`.

The proof is modus tollens against `primeLogFullSpan_not_finite`: a relation would bound the rational
rank of the frequency module (`finite_rational_phase_rank_of_gammaField_relation_line`), hence make
the prime-log span finitely generated over `ℚ`, which it is not.  The hypothesis is an
*inclusion*, not an equality (**`no_gammaField_relation_of_primeLogFullSpan_le`**, with
`module_finite_of_le_of_rank_le` supplying that a submodule of a finite-rank module is finitely
generated), so only the presence of the prime logs among the frequencies is needed.

### Zeta over the Gamma field, with field coefficients

`HTGeometry/ZetaGammaFieldFieldCoefficients.lean` runs that criterion on `ζ` itself:

> **`analyticOnNhd_zeta_upperTail`** and **`zetaUpperAnalytic`** — `ζ` is analytic on every upper
> half-plane (its only singularity is real), hence an element of the upper-end analytic carrier;
> **`isStripAlmostPeriodic_zeta`**, **`differentiableOn_zeta_verticalStrip`** — the strip hypotheses,
> from the Dirichlet convergence on `Re s > 1`;
> **`neg_log_mem_bohrSpectrum_zeta`** — the Dirichlet frequency `−log n` is a Bohr frequency of the
> line restriction, its coefficient being `e^{−σ log n} ≠ 0` (`bohrCoefficient_dirichlet_line`);
> **`primeLogFullSpan_le_bohrFrequencyModule_zeta`** — hence the whole prime-log span sits inside the
> frequency module of `ζ`;
> **`zeta_no_gammaField_field_relation`** — **`ζ` satisfies no nontrivial differential relation whose
> coefficients are nonzero elements of `ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩`.**

This is the card's zeta specialization in the abstract field form.  The corpus's earlier
`zeta_no_gammaField_relation` proves the same exclusion for coefficients presented by explicit
Gamma-jet data (`gammaJetLimit`, `GammaMonomialDominates`); the new statement quantifies over the
differential field itself, with no presentation and no dominance hypothesis, and its only inputs are
`σ > 1`, distinct jet exponents and nonvanishing coefficients.

The same freeing applies to the general Dirichlet statement.
`HTGeometry/DirichletGammaFieldFieldCoefficients.lean` proves

> **`no_gammaField_field_relation_of_infinite_rank`** — a solution whose frequency rank exceeds every
> natural number satisfies no relation with coefficients in `ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩`;
> **`dirichlet_no_gammaField_field_relation`** — a Dirichlet series uniformly convergent on the strip
> whose exponents contain arbitrarily large `ℤ`-independent families with nonvanishing coefficients
> satisfies no such relation.
>
> **`no_gammaField_field_relation_of_independent_spectrum`** — the criterion stated on the spectrum
> itself: if for every `K` the Bohr–Fourier expansion of the line restriction carries `K+1`
> rationally independent frequencies, no relation with coefficients in `ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩` holds
> (`linearIndependent_int_of_rat` passes from `ℚ`- to `ℤ`-independence,
> `bohrSpectrum_subset_bohrHullFrequencies` from the spectrum to the hull).
>
> **`no_gammaField_field_relation_of_independent_spectrum_line`** — the same for the line restriction
> itself, with no bundled restriction and no auxiliary analytic hypotheses.

The spectral hypothesis is verified for both families, so the criterion can be applied to them
directly: **`zeta_independent_spectrum`** and **`lfunction_independent_spectrum`** — for every `K` the
Bohr–Fourier expansion of `ζ` (resp. of `L(·,χ)`) on a vertical line to the right of `1` carries
`K + 1` rationally independent frequencies, namely the negated logarithms of `K + 1` distinct primes
(beyond the modulus, in the `L` case), whose independence is
**`linearIndependent_rat_neg_log_primes`**.

**The boundary of the method.**  The object must be an element of the upper-end *analytic* carrier,
i.e. it must continue analytically to some upper half-plane `{Im s > H}` — not merely to the
half-plane of convergence, since every upper half-plane meets `Re s ≤ 1`.  Zeta and the Dirichlet
`L`-functions qualify (their only singularity is at `s = 1`, which is real).  `1/ζ` does **not**: its
poles are the zeros of `ζ`, which have arbitrarily large imaginary part, so no upper half-plane avoids
them.  Its Dirichlet coefficients — the Möbius function — are bounded by one and nonzero at every
prime, so every *other* hypothesis of `no_gammaField_field_relation_of_boundedDirichlet` holds; the
obstruction is exactly the carrier.

The route past it is algebraic rather than analytic: `1/ζ` *is* an element of the meromorphic carrier
(the inverse of the `ζ` element in the fraction field), so what is needed is that differential
transcendence is invariant under `u ↦ u⁻¹`.  The corpus already has the field half
(`differentialAdjoin_singleton_eq_of_mem_mem`: mutually rational generators generate the same
differential field); what it lacked is the *independence* half, and its structural input is now
proved in `HTGeometry/InverseJetTriangular.lean`:

> **`jetFieldLe`** — the field generated by the jets of `u` of order at most `i`;
> **`deriv_mem_jetFieldLe_succ`** — the derivation carries it one step up, `δ(L_i) ⊆ L_{i+1}`;
> **`differentialJet_succ_inv`** — `δ^{i+1}(u⁻¹) = −δ^{i+1}u/u² + R` with `R ∈ L_i`.

So the substitution taking the jets of `u` to the jets of `u⁻¹` is **triangular**, and affine with
invertible coefficient `−1/u²` in each top jet.  Two consequences are now proved in the same file:

> **`differentialJet_inv_mem_jetFieldLe`** and **`jetFieldLe_inv`** — the jet fields agree at every
> order, `L_i(u⁻¹) = L_i(u)`;
> **`isAlgebraic_differentialJet_succ_inv_iff`** — `δ^{i+1}(u⁻¹)` is algebraic over `L_i` if and only
> if `δ^{i+1}u` is.

The second is the transfer in its usable form: the two top jets differ by an affine map over `L_i`
with invertible coefficient, so neither can be algebraic without the other.  What remains for the
`1/ζ` statement is the bookkeeping that converts between a polynomial relation among jets and
algebraicity of the top jet over the field of the lower ones — a minimal relation for `u⁻¹` makes
`δ^n(u⁻¹)` algebraic over `L_{n−1}(u⁻¹) = L_{n−1}(u)`, hence `δ^n u` algebraic over `L_{n−1}(u)`,
hence a relation for `u`.

That bookkeeping is available in mathlib, in the form
`AlgebraicIndepOn.insert_iff` / `AlgebraicIndependent.option_iff_transcendental`: a family is
algebraically independent exactly when each new member is transcendental over the algebra generated
by the previous ones.  Those statements are over `Algebra.adjoin`, while the differential argument
works over `IntermediateField.adjoin`; the bridge is

> **`isAlgebraic_algebraAdjoin_of_mem_intermediateField_adjoin`**
> (`HTGeometry/AlgebraAdjoinAlgebraic.lean`) — inside a field, every element of the generated *field*
> is algebraic over the generated *ring*, being a quotient of two of its elements and hence a root of
> a linear polynomial over it.

That is now carried out:

> **`isAlgebraic_algebraAdjoin_of_isAlgebraic_intermediateField`** — algebraicity over
> `IntermediateField.adjoin F S` implies algebraicity over `Algebra.adjoin F S`,

by building the tower `Algebra.adjoin ≤ IntermediateField.adjoin ≤ K` (`Subalgebra.inclusion`,
`IsScalarTower.of_algebraMap_eq`), instantiating `Algebra.IsAlgebraic` for the first step from the
quotient lemma above, and applying mathlib's `IsAlgebraic.restrictScalars`.  The converse direction —
**`isAlgebraic_intermediateField_of_isAlgebraic_algebraAdjoin`**, by mapping the polynomial's
coefficients along the injective inclusion — gives the equivalence outright:

> **`transcendental_algebraAdjoin_iff`**: `Transcendental (Algebra.adjoin F S) a ↔
> Transcendental (IntermediateField.adjoin F S) a`.

Transcendence over the ring and over the field are therefore interchangeable — the last structural
ingredient.

With it the transfer itself is proved, in `HTGeometry/InverseJetIndependence.lean`:

> **`algebraicIndepOn_jets_inv_Iic`** — the jets of `u⁻¹` up to any order `n` are algebraically
> independent whenever those of `u` are;
> **`algebraicIndependent_jets_inv`** — hence **differential transcendence is invariant under
> inversion**: `AlgebraicIndependent F (δⁱ u) → AlgebraicIndependent F (δⁱ u⁻¹)` for `u ≠ 0`.

The induction runs on `Set.Iic n` through mathlib's `AlgebraicIndepOn.insert_iff`: at each step the
new jet of `u⁻¹` must be transcendental over what the previous ones generate, which by
`transcendental_algebraAdjoin_iff` is the field `L_n(u⁻¹) = L_n(u)`, and there
`isAlgebraic_differentialJet_succ_inv_iff` exchanges it for the corresponding statement about `u`,
supplied by the same `insert_iff` on the `u`-side.  The passage from all `Set.Iic n` to the full
family is `algebraicIndependent_of_finite_type`, every finite set of naturals being bounded.

One half of the translation between the corpus's relation form and `AlgebraicIndependent` is proved
in `HTGeometry/JetRelationIndependence.lean`:

> **`sum_jet_monomials_ne_zero_of_algebraicIndependent`** — if the jets are algebraically independent
> over the base field then, for distinct exponent vectors and nonzero coefficients,
> `∑_α c_α ∏_i (δⁱu)^{e_{αi}} ≠ 0`.

The witness is the multivariate polynomial `∑_α monomial (e_α) (c_α)`, whose coefficient at any one of
the exponent vectors is the corresponding `c_α` (the vectors are distinct), so it is nonzero, while
its value at the jets is the sum — contradicting injectivity of the evaluation map.  The converse half is proved in the same file:

> **`algebraicIndependent_of_no_jet_relation`** — if no relation of that shape holds, the jets are
> algebraically independent.

A hypothetical vanishing nonzero polynomial is enumerated over its support: its variables are bounded
by `p.vars.sup id`, so each exponent vector restricts to a function on `Fin (n+1)` and distinct
