the integrand is `e^{itv} · (e^v/(1+e^v)²)^σ`, an `L¹` density times a character.  The decay of `Γ` on
vertical lines is then the Riemann–Lebesgue lemma, which mathlib has — that step is next.

### The last analytic input of eq. (3): Gamma decays on vertical lines

`HTGeometry/GammaVerticalDecay.lean` now proves it, without Stirling and without the Beta route.

Substituting `x = e^u` in the Gamma integral turns it into a Fourier integral:

> **`Gamma_vertical_eq_fourier`**: `Γ(σ + it) = ∫_ℝ e^{itu} · e^{σu − e^u} du` for `σ > 0`.

The density `u ↦ e^{σu − e^u}` is integrable — doubly exponentially small at `+∞`, exponentially at
`−∞` — so the Riemann–Lebesgue lemma applies directly, giving

> **`Gamma_vertical_tendsto_zero`**: `Γ(σ + it) → 0` as `t → +∞`, and
> **`Gamma_vertical_tendsto_nhdsWithin_zero`**: the same within `ℂ \ {0}`, since `Γ` has no zeros.

That is exactly the hypothesis `hΓ0` of `gamma_monomial_valuation_residue`, so eq. (3)'s projective
normalization is now unconditional.  The companion bound **`norm_Gamma_le_real_Gamma_re`**
(`‖Γ(s)‖ ≤ Γ(Re s)`, from the integral representation) is also proved; together they were the two
declarations that four Gamma modules of this corpus referenced but which no module supplied, so those
modules now compile.

The squared-modulus route through the Beta integral (`normSq_Gamma_vertical`, plus the logistic
substitution `sigmoid`) is kept as well: it expresses `‖Γ(σ+it)‖²` as `Γ(2σ)` times a Beta integral,
which is the shape the reflection-formula arguments of §4 use.

### Eq. (3) unconditional, and eq. (5) for tuples

With the vertical decay proved, `HTGeometry/GammaMonomialValuationUnconditional.lean` records
**`gamma_monomial_valuation_residue_of_pos`**: on every vertical line of positive abscissa the
projective normalization of a Gamma-field element converges to the residue `R(0)/S(0)` — eq. (3) with
no hypotheses beyond `σ > 0`.

`HTGeometry/GammaTupleProjectiveBoundary.lean` extends this to ratios, which is eq. (5).  Writing two
elements as `Γ^u R(Γ)/(Γ^v S(Γ))`, their ratio is `Γ^k` times a ratio of polynomial values
(`ratio_eventuallyEq`), where `k` is the difference of valuations.  Hence

> **`ratio_tendsto_residue`** — equal valuations: the ratio converges to
> `R_α(0) S_β(0) / (S_α(0) R_β(0))`;
> **`ratio_tendsto_zero`** — strictly larger valuation: the ratio converges to `0`.

Normalizing a finite coefficient tuple by a valuation-minimal member therefore makes every ratio
converge: the tuple has a single constant point at its upper vertical projective boundary, which is
what the recurrence collapse consumes as its `hcoeff` input — and that collapse already has its Bohr
return times proved.

### Eq. (27) → (29) with nothing left open on the coefficient side

`HTGeometry/GammaRationalCollapse.lean` assembles the collapse for the field the card is about.

> **`recurrence_collapse_gamma_rational`**.  Let a finite relation `∑ F_α M_α = 0` have Gamma-rational
> coefficients `F_α = Γ^{u_α} R_α(Γ) / (Γ^{v_α} S_α(Γ))` with `R_α(0), S_α(0) ≠ 0`, let `β` be
> valuation-minimal, and let the monomials be continuous and almost periodic along the line
> `Re s = σ > 0`.  Then there is a constant vector `d` with `∑ d_α M_α(σ) = 0` and `d_β = 1`.

Both analytic inputs are now theorems rather than hypotheses: the coefficient ratios converge by the
tuple projective boundary (itself resting on the vertical decay of `Γ`), and the return times come
from the common Bohr almost periods of the finitely many monomials.  The dominant coefficient is
eventually nonzero because `Γ` does not vanish and `R_β, S_β` do not vanish at the origin.

So the passage from a Gamma-differential relation to an autonomous one — eq. (8) — is complete in
Lean for Gamma-rational coefficients, given only that the solution is almost periodic on the line.

### The monomials need no hypothesis either

`HTGeometry/JetMonomialAlmostPeriodic.lean` removes the last data assumption of the collapse.
Powers (`isBohrAlmostPeriodic_pow`) and finite products (`isBohrAlmostPeriodic_finsetProd`) of
continuous almost periodic functions are almost periodic, by induction on the corpus's product law.
Since the jets of a strip almost periodic holomorphic function restrict to continuous almost periodic
functions on the central line,

> **`isBohrAlmostPeriodic_jetMonomial`**: every monomial `∏_j (F⁽ʲ⁾)^{e_j}` in the jets of a strip
> almost periodic holomorphic `F` is continuous and almost periodic along the line.

Those are exactly the monomials of an algebraic differential relation.  So
`recurrence_collapse_gamma_rational` needs nothing about them beyond the strip almost periodicity of
the solution: eq. (27) → (29) holds for any strip almost periodic holomorphic solution of a
Gamma-rational differential relation, with every analytic input proved.

### Eq. (8): the collapsed relation holds on the whole strip

The limit coefficient vector of the collapse is explicit and does not depend on the line: it is now
the named `gammaRatioLimit p q u v β` (`GammaRationalCollapse`), the residue quotient at the origin on
the valuation-minimal indices and zero elsewhere.  Because it is line-independent, the collapse can be
run on *every* vertical line of the strip at once:

- `isStripAlmostPeriodic_shift` — strip almost periodicity is inherited by every concentric strip
  centred at another interior point, so every interior centre is an admissible collapse line;
- `gamma_rational_autonomous_relation` (`GammaRationalStripCollapse`) — the collapsed relation
  therefore vanishes at every real point of the strip; being holomorphic on a convex strip it vanishes
  identically.

> **Eq. (8) is a theorem**: a strip almost periodic holomorphic solution of a Gamma-rational
> differential relation satisfies the constant-coefficient relation with coefficient vector
> `gammaRatioLimit` at *every* point of the strip.  The solution is differentially algebraic over the
> constants, and the relation is nontrivial (`gammaRatioLimit β = 1`).

### Eq. (9) over the Gamma field

Two further modules turn eq. (8) into the rank bound.

`HTGeometry/JetPolynomialDerivative.lean` makes an algebraic differential relation an analytic object:
`jetPoly d e` is the monomial sum, `jetPolyDeriv d e y` its Fréchet derivative (`hasFDerivAt_jetPoly`),
and `partialCoeff_jetPolyDeriv` computes the partial derivative in each jet coordinate — the quantity
whose dominance drives the separation step.

`HTGeometry/StripSolutionPhaseRank.lean` internalizes all the carrier plumbing.  From the function
alone it builds the bounded almost periodic translate curve, the embedding of the line carrier in the
strip carrier, and the holomorphic representatives solving the same relation, so that

> **`rank_le_of_strip_solution`**: a strip almost periodic holomorphic solution of a relation whose
> solutions are all regular at one of finitely many sample times has
> `rank_ℤ Γ_B(f) ≤ 2 |T| (n+1)`.

Combining the two with eq. (8):

> **`rank_le_of_gamma_rational`** (`HTGeometry/GammaRationalPhaseRank.lean`): for a strip almost
> periodic holomorphic `Φ` satisfying a differential relation with Gamma-rational coefficients,
> `rank_ℤ Γ_B(f) ≤ 2 |T| (n+1)`.

Nothing about the coefficients, the monomials, the carrier, or the transfer is hypothetical any more:
the coefficient limits come from the vertical decay of `Γ`, the monomials are almost periodic because
the jets are, the relation propagates to the carrier by uniform convergence, and the derivative data
of the relation is computed from its own coefficients.  The one remaining input is the regularity
region: a set of jets, valid for every solution at one of the sample times, on which the top partial
derivative of the relation dominates.

### The regularity atlas is a consequence, not an assumption

The atlas was the last hypothesis, and it is now discharged.  Two changes make that possible.

First the atlas API is indexed by an arbitrary type rather than by sample times
(`rank_le_of_jetAtlas`, `rank_le_of_regular_jets`, `rank_le_of_strip_solution`): a finite family of
*(time, region)* pairs is allowed, so several regularity regions may sit over one and the same time.
That is what a covering argument needs, since a compact set of jets is covered by many small balls at
one time, not by one ball at many times.

Then `HTGeometry/NondegenerateSolutionPhaseRank.lean` builds the cover.  Around any jet where the top
partial derivative of the relation is nonzero, continuity of the derivative gives a ball on which it
dominates — an admissible convex regularity region, with `L` the derivative at the centre and
`C` half the modulus of its top partial.  The carrier is compact and the centre jet of the
representative is continuous on it (Cauchy's estimate), so finitely many of those balls cover the
carrier:

> **`rank_finite_of_nondegenerate_solution`**: if the top partial derivative of the relation is
> nonzero at the centre jet of *every* holomorphic solution on the substrip, then
> `rank_ℤ Γ_B(f) < ∞`.

Specialised to the Gamma field (`rank_finite_of_gamma_rational`), with the collapsed relation's own
derivative supplied by `JetPolynomialDerivative`:

> A strip almost periodic holomorphic solution of a differential relation with Gamma-rational
> coefficients, whose collapsed relation is nondegenerate in the top jet along the carrier, has
> **finite rational Bohr frequency rank** — eq. (9), with no atlas, no regularity region, and no
> hypothesis on the coefficients.

What remains on this branch is the nondegeneracy itself: an order-`n` relation that degenerates in
its top jet on the whole carrier is one whose solutions satisfy a lower-order relation, so the
expected route is a minimal-order argument rather than new analysis.

### One nondegenerate point suffices

The nondegeneracy hypothesis has been reduced to a single point of the line.

`HTGeometry/CarrierTranslationEquivariance.lean` supplies the transfer.  The carrier is a topological
group in which the line is dense, so the translates of any one carrier point by the line are dense
(`denseRange_bohrIota_add`), and a continuous function constant along them is constant
(`eq_of_eqOn_translates`).  The representative of a translated carrier point agrees with the
translated representative on the central line, hence on the strip, so the jets of the representatives
are translation equivariant (`iteratedDeriv_rep_translate`), and the representative of the origin has
the jets of the function itself (`iteratedDeriv_rep_zero`).

Degeneracy of the relation at the centre jet is a continuous condition on the carrier.  A carrier
point whose representative were degenerate at *every* time would force degeneracy along a dense set,
hence everywhere — in particular at the function itself.  So
(`HTGeometry/PointNondegeneracyPhaseRank.lean`):

> **`rank_finite_of_nondegenerate_at_point`**: if the top partial derivative of the relation is
> nonzero at a *single* jet of the function, then `rank_ℤ Γ_B(f) < ∞`.

and over the Gamma field, `rank_finite_of_gamma_rational_at_point`: a strip almost periodic
holomorphic solution of a Gamma-rational differential relation whose collapsed relation is
nondegenerate at one point of the line has finite rational Bohr frequency rank.

`HTGeometry/JetPolynomialDescent.lean` starts the removal of even that hypothesis.  The `k`-th
partial derivative of a jet polynomial is again a jet polynomial
(`partialCoeff_jetPolyDeriv_eq`, with coefficients `d α · e α k` and exponent `e α k − 1`), and a jet
polynomial of a holomorphic function is holomorphic, so a jet polynomial relation holding on the
central line holds on the whole strip (`jetPoly_eqOn_zero_of_line`).  A relation degenerate in its top
jet at every point of the line therefore yields the derived relation, of strictly smaller degree in
that jet: the descent that will eliminate the last hypothesis.

### Eq. (9) is now a theorem over the Gamma field

The nondegeneracy hypothesis is gone.  The relation is carried as a multivariate polynomial in the
jets, whose analytic data is computed by `pderiv`:

- `HTGeometry/MvPolynomialJetDerivative.lean` — `hasFDerivAt_mvEval`: the evaluation of a
  multivariate polynomial is Fréchet differentiable with derivative `∑_k (∂_k p)(y) · proj_k`, proved
  by the monomial induction; `partialCoeff_mvJetDeriv` reads off the partial derivatives, and
  `monomialSum_ne_zero` shows a sum of distinct monomials with a nonzero coefficient is a nonzero
  polynomial.
- `HTGeometry/MvPolynomialPDerivDegree.lean` — differentiating strictly drops the degree in that
  variable (`degreeOf_pderiv_lt`), and in characteristic zero a variable with vanishing partial
  derivative does not occur (`degreeOf_eq_zero_of_pderiv_eq_zero`).
- `HTGeometry/MvPolynomialDropLast.lean` — a relation that no longer involves its top jet is a
  relation in one variable fewer, still nonzero (`dropLastPoly_ne_zero`).
- `HTGeometry/ConstantSolutionPhaseRank.lean` — a holomorphic function satisfying a nonzero algebraic
  equation takes values in a finite set, hence is constant on the connected strip; a constant function
  has identically zero translation distance, so its frequency group is trivial and its rank is zero.

The descent (`HTGeometry/PolynomialRelationPhaseRank.lean`) puts these together.  Given any nonzero
polynomial relation satisfied on the strip: if it is nondegenerate in the top jet at some point of the
line, the phase-rank bound applies; otherwise its derivative in the top jet is again a relation, of
strictly smaller degree there; when that derivative vanishes identically the relation drops in order;
and the order-zero case is the constant case.  Hence

> **`rank_finite_of_polynomial_relation`**: a strip almost periodic holomorphic function satisfying
> *any* nonzero polynomial relation in its jets has `rank_ℤ Γ_B(f) < ∞`.

Combined with the collapse (`HTGeometry/GammaRationalFinitePhaseRank.lean`), and with
`gammaRatioLimit_self` giving coefficient `1` at the valuation-minimal index:

> **`rank_finite_of_gamma_rational_relation`** — *eq. (9) over the Gamma field*: a strip almost
> periodic holomorphic solution of a differential relation with Gamma-rational coefficients and
> distinct monomials has finite rational Bohr frequency rank.  Hypotheses: the strip almost
> periodicity of the solution, the Gamma-rational form of the coefficients, valuation minimality of
> the normalizing index, and distinctness of the monomials.  No analytic input is left open.

### The law is not vacuous, and the witness has nonzero rank

`HTGeometry/GammaRationalWitness.lean` inhabits the whole hypothesis class.  For a nonzero real
frequency `μ` the exponential `Φ(z) = e^{μ z}`:

- is entire and almost periodic on every vertical strip (`isStripAlmostPeriodic_expLine`);
- has jets `Φ^{(k)} = μ^k Φ` (`iteratedDeriv_expLine`), so it solves `Φ' − μ Φ = 0`, a relation whose
  coefficients `1` and `−μ` are Gamma-rational (constant) and whose two monomials are distinct;
- restricts on the line to a bounded continuous function with totally bounded orbit
  (`expLineBC`), so every carrier hypothesis holds.

Hence `rank_finite_expLine`: the law applies and gives a finite bound.  Moreover the frequency `μ` is
a frequency of the hull (`isBohrHullFrequency_expLineBC`: the character is Lipschitz for the
translation distance), so

> **`rank_expLine_pos_and_finite`**: `1 ≤ rank_ℤ Γ_B(f) ≤ K`.

The conclusion is therefore a statement about a nonempty class containing objects of nonzero phase
rank — not an empty or trivial one.

### The divisor clause

`HTGeometry/ObservableFrequencyRank.lean` proves the second half of the finite-phase theorem: the
same conclusion for every continuous translation-equivariant observable.  Call `g` *dominated* by `f`
when the translates of `g` are uniformly close whenever those of `f` are — the precise sense in which
an observable is a continuous equivariant reading of `f`, and exactly the comparison the carrier maps
already use.  Then

- every frequency character of `g` is uniformly continuous for the translation distance of `f`
  (`isBohrHullFrequency_of_dominated`), so `Γ_B(g) ≤ Γ_B(f)` as subgroups of `ℝ`;
- hence `rank_le_of_dominated`: `rank_ℤ Γ_B(g) ≤ rank_ℤ Γ_B(f)`;
- hence **`rank_finite_of_dominated_gamma_rational`**: every observable dominated by a strip almost
  periodic solution of a Gamma-rational differential relation has finite rational Bohr frequency rank.

Lipschitz functions of the values are dominated observables (`isDominatedObservable_comp`), so the
clause is not vacuous either.

### Next formalization target

What is Lean-complete is the law over the Gamma-rational subfield `ℂ(Γ)`: coefficients of the form
`Γ^u p(Γ) / (Γ^v q(Γ))`.  The remaining formal target is eq. (3) for the *full* differential field
`ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩`, whose elements involve `s` and the digamma tower `ψ, ψ', …`.  That needs the vertical
asymptotics of `ψ` and its derivatives (Stirling on vertical lines) and the monomial valuation
`F = c Γ^k s^a (log s)^b (1 + o(1))` built from them — the "analytic Poincaré transfer" named below.

### Eq. (14): the shift equation has no rational solution

`HTGeometry/RationalShiftNoSolution.lean` proves the elementary pole-orbit step of §2 in a
junk-value-free algebraic form.  Writing a candidate solution as `p/q` and clearing denominators,
`IsShiftInvSqSolution p q` is the polynomial identity `X²(p(X+1)q − p·q(X+1)) = −q·q(X+1)`.  Then:

- the value at `0` forces `q(0) = 0` or `q(1) = 0`, so `q` is nonconstant;
- for coprime `p, q`: every nonzero root of `q` is followed by a root one step up, and every root
  other than `1` is preceded by a root one step down, so the root of largest real part is `0` and the
  root of smallest real part is `1` — impossible;
- the general case descends on a common root, cancelling the factor `(X − a)(X + 1 − a)`.

> **`no_rational_shift_inv_sq`**: no pair `(p, q)` with `q ≠ 0` solves the cleared-denominator shift
> equation — i.e. `y(s+1) − y(s) = −1/s²` has no solution in `ℂ(s)`.

This complements the corpus's meromorphic version
(`GammaProjectiveBoundary.no_finitePole_solution_digammaShiftDerivative`), which assumes the shift
identity at *every* point of `ℂ` — a hypothesis a rational function cannot satisfy at its own poles
under Lean's junk-value convention.  The polynomial form has no such gap.

### Survey of the digamma layer already in the corpus

Before building the analytic transfer, the existing assets are:

- `RHTower/DigammaImaginaryVerticalAsymptotic.digamma_vertical_sub_log_I_tendsto_zero` —
  `ψ(σ+it) − (log t + iπ/2) → 0` on every vertical line with `σ ≥ 0`: eq. (19)'s leading term for
  `ψ` itself;
- `HTGeometry/DigammaSectorBounds.exists_norm_iteratedDeriv_succ_digamma_halfShift_le` — every
  positive-order polygamma is uniformly bounded on the Watson sector (its series representation is
  present but `private`);
- `HTGeometry/DigammaHypertranscendental` — Hölder's theorem for `ψ`;
- `HTGeometry/DigammaGammaFirstOrder`, `GammaLogDeriv` — the shift and reflection identities.

The missing analytic input for eq. (19) with nonconstant digamma-field data is the *vertical decay*
`ψ^{(m+1)}(σ+it) → 0` for `m ≥ 0`, which follows from the polygamma series
`ψ^{(m+1)}(z) = (−1)^m (m+1)! ∑_n (z+n)^{−(m+2)}` by dominated convergence.  With `ψ ~ log` and the
derivatives vanishing, a jet polynomial in the digamma tower has leading term the pure `ψ^d`
monomial, which is eq. (19) with `b = d`.  That is the next construction.

### Eq. (19) for the digamma field: the analytic transfer, constant-coefficient case

Two new modules close the constant-coefficient half of the analytic Poincaré transfer.

`HTGeometry/PolygammaVerticalDecay.lean` supplies the missing decay.  The corpus's vertical trigamma
bound `‖ψ′(σ+it)‖ ≤ 1/t² + π/(2t)` holds on a whole disc of fixed radius `σ/2` around the point, so
Cauchy's estimate for iterated derivatives transfers it to every higher order:

> `norm_iteratedDeriv_succ_digamma_vertical_le`:
> `‖ψ^{(m+1)}(σ+it)‖ ≤ m! (1/(t−σ/2)² + π/(2(t−σ/2))) / (σ/2)^m`,
> hence **`tendsto_iteratedDeriv_succ_digamma_vertical`**: every positive-order polygamma vanishes
> along vertical lines.

`HTGeometry/DigammaFieldValuation.lean` then reads off the valuation.  With
`L(t) = log t + iπ/2` the corpus's vertical asymptotic gives `ψ/L → 1`
(`tendsto_digamma_div_vertLog`), while all derivative factors vanish.  So a jet monomial normalized
by `L^D` converges — to its tail limit when its digamma degree is exactly `D`, to zero when it is
smaller (`tendsto_monomial_div`) — and summing:

> **`tendsto_jetPoly_digamma_div`**: for a jet polynomial in the digamma tower whose pure monomial
> `ψ^D` occurs with coefficient `d β`, and whose every other monomial either has smaller digamma
> degree or carries a derivative factor,
> `P(ψ, ψ′, …, ψ^{(n)})(σ+it) / L(t)^D → d β`.

That is eq. (19) with `b = D`, `a = 0`, `c = d β`, proved unconditionally for constant coefficients.
What remains for the full statement is the `ℂ(s)`-coefficient case, where the `s^a` factors enter and
the leading monomial is chosen lexicographically in `(degree in ψ, degree in s)`.

### Eq. (19) with rational coefficients

`HTGeometry/DigammaFieldValuationRational.lean` extends the valuation from constant to `ℂ(s)`
coefficients.

- `tendsto_eval_div_pow_natDegree_of_norm_atTop` — a complex polynomial divided by the power of its
  own degree tends to its leading coefficient along *any* filter on which the argument escapes
  (proved by the reverse polynomial at `1/z`).  The corpus previously had two copies of the special
  case where the argument runs along the real axis; this covers both and the vertical case at once.
- `tendsto_ratio_eval_vertical` — hence on a vertical line, `p(s)·s^{deg q} / (q(s)·s^{deg p})` tends
  to `lead p / lead q`: the projective boundary of a rational coefficient.
- **`tendsto_ratCoeff_jetPoly_vertical`** — multiplying by the digamma valuation, for an element
  `R(s)·P(ψ, ψ′, …, ψ^{(n)})` of `ℂ(s)⟨ψ⟩`,

  > `R(s)·P(jets) · s^{deg q} / (s^{deg p} · L(t)^D) → (lead p / lead q) · d β`,

  where `d β` is the coefficient of the pure monomial `ψ^D`.  This is eq. (19) with
  `a = deg p − deg q`, `b = D`, and `c = (lead p / lead q)·d β`, proved unconditionally.

What remains of §3 is the bookkeeping that selects the dominant `(a, b)` pair inside a finite sum of
such elements — a lexicographic comparison on top of these limits, with no new analytic input.

### Eq. (5) for the digamma field

`HTGeometry/DigammaFieldRatio.lean` completes the projective-boundary statement for the elements
`R(s)·ψ^D` of `ℂ(s)⟨ψ⟩`.

- `tendsto_norm_digamma_vertical` — `‖ψ(σ+it)‖ → ∞`, from the vertical asymptotic and the growth of
  the leading logarithm;
- `tendsto_digamma_pow_ratio` — hence `ψ^{D_α}/ψ^{D_β} → 1` when the exponents agree and `→ 0` when
  the numerator's is smaller;
- `tendsto_ratio_poly_le` — a quotient of polynomials of no larger degree tends to the quotient of
  the leading coefficients when the degrees agree, and to zero otherwise;
- **`tendsto_ratio_digammaField`** — multiplying the two, the normalized ratio of two monomial
  elements converges whenever the numerator is dominated componentwise, with limit
  `(lead(p_α q_β)/lead(q_α p_β)) · [D_α = D_β]` — nonzero exactly when both the degree datum and the
  digamma exponent agree.

This is eq. (5) for the digamma field: a finite family normalized by a componentwise-minimal member
has a constant projective limit.  Together with `tendsto_ratCoeff_jetPoly_vertical` (eq. 19) the
digamma layer of §3 is unconditional; what is left is only the passage from a componentwise to a
lexicographic dominance order, which needs the comparison `L(t)^k / s → 0` and no new analytic input.

### Lexicographic dominance closes eq. (5) for the digamma field

`HTGeometry/DigammaFieldLexRatio.lean` supplies the last comparison and packages the result.

- `tendsto_pow_log_div_self` — a power of the logarithm is negligible against the identity;
- **`tendsto_digamma_pow_div_vertical_pow`** — hence any power of `ψ` divided by a positive power of
  the argument vanishes on a vertical line: `‖ψ‖ ≤ 2 log t` eventually, while `‖s‖ ≥ t`;
- `tendsto_ratio_digammaField_of_degree_lt` — so a ratio whose rational datum has strictly smaller
  degree vanishes *whatever* the digamma exponents are;
- **`tendsto_ratio_digammaField_lex`** — combining with the componentwise case:

  > for elements `R(s)·ψ^D` of `ℂ(s)⟨ψ⟩` with the numerator dominated lexicographically in
  > `(deg, D)`, the normalized ratio converges, with limit
  > `lead(p_α q_β)/lead(q_α p_β)` when both data agree and `0` otherwise.

That is eq. (5) for the digamma field in final form: a finite family normalized by a
lexicographically minimal member has a constant projective limit, nonzero exactly at the members
sharing the minimal datum.  §3's digamma layer — eq. (19) and eq. (5) — is now unconditional in Lean
for elements of the form `R(s)·P(ψ, ψ′, …)`.

### The exponential valuation direction, analytically

§4 says that adjoining `Γ` creates one exponential valuation direction.  Its algebraic half is
already in the corpus: `HTGeometry/GammaBellSubstitution.lean` builds the Bell polynomials, proves
`Γ⁽ᵏ⁾ = Γ·B_k(ψ, …, ψ⁽ᵏ⁻¹⁾)` (`gamma_iteratedDeriv_eq_bellCore`, eq. 20), the triangular recursion,
and surjectivity of the Bell value map — so `K_Γ = L(Γ)` (eq. 21) needs nothing further.

`HTGeometry/GammaVerticalSuperPolynomialDecay.lean` now supplies the analytic half.  On the line
`Re s = 1/2 + m` the corpus's two-sided modulus bounds give `‖Γ(s)‖ ≤ C |t|^m e^{-π|t|/2}`, while
`‖s‖ ≤ C₂ t` and `‖L(t)‖ ≤ C₃ t`, so squeezing against `t^N e^{-(jπ/2)t}`:

> **`tendsto_gamma_pow_mul_vertical`**: `Γ(s)^j · s^k · L(t)^b → 0` for every `j ≥ 1` and all
> `k, b`, and **`tendsto_gamma_pow_mul_digamma_vertical`** the same with `ψ^b` in place of `L^b`.

So in the projective boundary of `ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩` the `Γ`-exponent dominates every polynomial and
logarithmic datum: the valuation is lexicographic in `(Γ-exponent, degree, digamma exponent)`, and the
first two levels are now proved.  What remains is only the assembly of the three-level ratio
statement, which needs no new analytic input.

### Eq. (5) for the full Gamma field

`HTGeometry/GammaFieldProjectiveBoundary.lean` assembles the three valuation directions.  An element
in monomial form is `gammaFieldElt m j p q D t = Γ(s)^j · (p/q)(s) · ψ(s)^D` on the line
`Re s = 1/2 + m`, with boundary datum `(j, deg, D)`.

- **`tendsto_ratio_gammaFieldElt_of_gamma_lt`** — a strictly larger `Γ`-exponent in the numerator
  makes the ratio vanish whatever the rational and digamma data: decompose the ratio as
  `(Γ^c s^{deg} ψ^D) · (u/s^{deg}) · (1/v) · (1/ψ^{D'})`, whose first factor vanishes by the
  super-polynomial decay and whose remaining factors converge.
- **`tendsto_ratio_gammaFieldElt_lex`** — hence, ordering the datum lexicographically, a dominated
  numerator has a convergent normalized ratio:

  > limit `= lead(p_α q_β)/lead(q_α p_β)` when all three data agree, and `0` otherwise.

  When the `Γ`-exponents agree the `Γ` factors cancel and the digamma-field comparison of the
  previous file takes over.

This is eq. (5) for `K_Γ = ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩` in monomial form: every finite family, normalized by a
lexicographically minimal member, has a constant projective limit — the one-point projective boundary
of §1, proved unconditionally along vertical lines `Re s = 1/2 + m`.

### Eq. (8) and eq. (9) over the full Gamma field

Two modules carry the boundary law into the finite-phase theorem with coefficients from `ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩`
itself, not just its rational subfield `ℂ(Γ)`.

`HTGeometry/GammaFieldRecurrenceCollapse.lean` — the projective boundary supplies the coefficient
limits, a Gamma-field monomial is eventually nonvanishing on the line, and the jet monomials return
along the Bohr return times, so

> **`recurrence_collapse_gammaField`**: a relation whose coefficients are `Γ^j·(p/q)·ψ^D`, normalized
> by a lexicographically minimal member, collapses to `∑ d_α M_α = 0` with `d_β = 1`, where `d` is the
> explicit limit datum `gammaFieldLimit`.

`HTGeometry/GammaFieldFinitePhaseRank.lean` — the collapse runs from *every* height on the line (the
ratio limits are unchanged by a shift of the base point, and almost periodicity is
translation-invariant), so the collapsed relation holds along the whole line and hence, both sides
being holomorphic, on the whole strip:

> **`gammaField_autonomous_relation`** (eq. 8) and, feeding it to the descent,
> **`rank_finite_of_gammaField_relation`** (eq. 9): a strip almost periodic holomorphic solution of a
> differential relation with Gamma-field coefficients and distinct monomials has finite rational Bohr
> frequency rank.

Hypotheses left: strip almost periodicity of the solution, the monomial form of the coefficients with
lexicographically minimal normalizing index, and distinctness of the monomials.  Nothing analytic is
open, and the line is `Re s = 1/2 + m` — including the critical line itself.

### Finite sums, and the collapse from abstract coefficient data

`HTGeometry/GammaFieldSumBoundary.lean` extends the boundary from single monomials to finite sums —
i.e. to all of `ℂ(s)[Γ, ψ]` in monomial expansion.  With `gammaMonomialLimit` and
`GammaMonomialDominates` naming the two-monomial datum and its lexicographic order:

- `tendsto_sum_ratio_gammaFieldElt` — a finite sum, normalized by a monomial dominating every
  summand, converges to the sum of the individual limit data (eq. 3 for sums);
- `eventually_sum_gammaFieldElt_ne_zero` — with nonzero limit constant the sum is eventually
  nonvanishing;
- `tendsto_ratio_gammaFieldSum` — hence quotients of such sums converge (eq. 5 for sums).

`HTGeometry/GammaFieldAbstractCollapse.lean` isolates what the collapse actually consumes: the
normalized coefficient ratios converge along the line, and the normalizing coefficient is eventually
nonzero.  From just those two facts:

> `line_relation_of_coeff_limits` → **`strip_relation_of_coeff_limits`** (eq. 8) →
> **`rank_finite_of_coeff_limits`** (eq. 9).

The monomial case and the finite-sum case are now instances; the latter is recorded as
**`rank_finite_of_gammaFieldSum_relation`**: a strip almost periodic holomorphic solution of a
differential relation whose coefficients are finite sums of Gamma-field monomials — every summand
dominated by one fixed monomial, normalizing coefficient with nonzero limit constant, distinct jet
monomials — has finite rational Bohr frequency rank.

### The Gamma-field law is inhabited on the critical line

`HTGeometry/GammaFieldWitness.lean` exercises the whole Gamma-field stack on a concrete object.  A
constant is the Gamma-field monomial with all data zero (`gammaFieldElt_const`), so the relation
`Φ′ − μΦ = 0` satisfied by `Φ(z) = e^{μz}` has Gamma-field coefficients whose lexicographic dominance
is the equality case.  With `Φ` entire, strip almost periodic, and its restriction to the critical
line a bounded continuous function with totally bounded orbit:

> **`rank_finite_expLine_gammaField`**: the Gamma-field finite-phase law applies on `Re s = 1/2`, and
> with the hull-frequency lower bound, **`rank_expLine_gammaField_pos_and_finite`**:
> `1 ≤ rank_ℤ Γ_B(f) ≤ K`.

So the hypothesis class of the Gamma-field law — not just the Gamma-rational one — is inhabited by
objects of nonzero phase rank, on the critical line itself.

### The whole monomial basis of the Gamma field

`HTGeometry/GammaFieldJetBoundary.lean` extends the boundary from pure monomials `Γ^j·(p/q)·ψ^D` to
the general monomial `Γ^j·(p/q)·∏(ψ⁽ⁱ⁾)^{E i}` of `ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩`.  Splitting the jet product into its
order-zero factor and the rest (`gammaFieldJetElt_eq`), a full-tower monomial is a pure monomial times
a product of positive-order polygammas, and those vanish on a vertical line.  Hence

- **`tendsto_ratio_gammaFieldJet`** — the normalized ratio converges to
  `gammaMonomialLimit · ∏ 0^{E i₊₁}`;
- `tendsto_ratio_gammaFieldJet_pure` — with no derivative factor the datum is that of the pure part;
- `tendsto_ratio_gammaFieldJet_deriv` — one derivative factor makes the monomial negligible;
- `tendsto_sum_ratio_gammaFieldJet` — finite sums behave termwise.

Together with the sum and quotient results this covers the entire monomial basis of the Gamma field:
every element of `ℂ(s)[Γ, ψ, ψ′, …]` in monomial expansion has a projective limit along the vertical
line, and the limit is nonzero exactly on the monomials sharing the dominant datum.

### The finite-phase law for arbitrary Gamma-field coefficients

`HTGeometry/GammaFieldJetCollapse.lean` closes the loop.  With `gammaJetLimit` naming the boundary
datum of a full-tower monomial against a pure one:

- `tendsto_sum_ratio_gammaFieldJet'` and `eventually_sum_gammaFieldJetElt_ne_zero` — finite sums of
  full-tower monomials have convergent normalized ratios and are eventually nonvanishing when their
  limit constant is nonzero;
- `tendsto_ratio_gammaFieldJetSum` — hence quotients of such sums converge;
- **`rank_finite_of_gammaFieldJetSum_relation`** — feeding those two facts to the abstract collapse:
  a strip almost periodic holomorphic solution of a differential relation whose coefficients are
  arbitrary elements of `ℂ(s)[Γ, ψ, ψ′, …]` in monomial expansion has **finite rational Bohr
  frequency rank**.

That is eq. (9) with the coefficient field no longer restricted at all beyond the monomial expansion:
the chain runs projective boundary → collapse → strip relation → descent → finite rank, and the whole
of it is unconditional in Lean along `Re s = 1/2 + m`.

### The dominant index always exists — the existential of eq. (5)

`HTGeometry/GammaFieldDominantIndex.lean` removes the last combinatorial hypothesis.  The boundary
datum of a monomial is packaged as a key in the linear order `ℕᵒᵈ ×ₗ ℤ ×ₗ ℕ` — the `Γ`-exponent
reversed, the degree `deg p − deg q` of the rational factor, and the digamma exponent — and

> **`dominates_iff_key_le`**: `GammaMonomialDominates` is exactly comparison of keys (the
> cross-degree comparison `deg(p_α q_β) < deg(q_α p_β)` is the difference comparison
> `deg p_α − deg q_α < deg p_β − deg q_β`, by `natDegree_mul`);

> **`exists_dominant_index`** / **`exists_dominant_monomial_family`**: hence every finite family — in
> particular the doubly indexed family of all monomials of all coefficients of a finite relation —
> has a member dominating all of them.

This is the existential in eq. (5) (“there is an index `j` such that …”): the normalizing member can
always be chosen, so the dominance hypothesis of the collapse costs no generality.

### No cancellation at the dominant datum

`HTGeometry/GammaFieldNoCancellation.lean` discharges the last numerical hypothesis.  Two monomials
contribute to the same limit constant only when both their `Γ`-exponents and their digamma exponents
agree, so:

> **`sum_gammaMonomialLimit_eq_one`**: if the monomials of the normalizing coefficient carry pairwise
> distinct `(Γ-exponent, digamma exponent)` pairs — the normal form obtained by collecting monomials
> with equal exponents — only the normalizing monomial contributes and the limit constant is exactly
> `1`.

Hence **`rank_finite_of_gammaFieldSum_distinct`**: eq. (9) with no numerical assumption on the
coefficients at all, only their monomial expansion in normal form.  Together with
`exists_dominant_monomial_family` (the dominant index is free) the hypotheses of the finite-phase law
are now exactly: the solution is strip almost periodic and holomorphic, the coefficients are written
in Gamma-field monomial normal form, and the jet monomials of the relation are distinct.

### Toward the law on every vertical line

The chain above is proved on the lines `Re s = 1/2 + m`, where the corpus supplies two-sided bounds
for `|Γ|`.  For an arbitrary `σ > 0` the missing input is the *super-polynomial* decay of `Γ` on the
line, and the route is the log-coordinate Fourier representation `Γ(σ+it) = ∫ e^{itu} e^{σu−e^u} du`
already in `GammaVerticalDecay`: the Fourier transform of a smooth function whose every derivative is
integrable decays faster than any polynomial.

Two new modules supply exactly those hypotheses.

- `HTGeometry/GammaLogIntegrand.lean` — `integrable_expSubExp`: the integrand `e^{au − e^u}` is
  integrable for every `a > 0`, dominated by `e^{au}` on the left half line and, via
  `e^u ≥ (1 + u/2)²`, by a Gaussian with a linear term on the right.
- `HTGeometry/GammaLogIntegrandDerivatives.lean` — differentiating `e^{(σ+k)u−e^u}` gives `(σ+k)`
  times itself minus the same expression with `k` raised by one, so finite combinations are closed
  under differentiation (`IsExpFamily.deriv`).  Hence **`integrable_iteratedDeriv_expShift`**: every
  iterated derivative of the log-coordinate Gamma integrand is integrable, and `contDiff_expShift`
  gives smoothness.

With those, `Real.fourier_iteratedDeriv` plus Riemann–Lebesgue gives `t^N Γ(σ+it) → 0` for every `N`
and every `σ > 0` — the generalization of the exponential valuation direction from `Re s = 1/2 + m`
to every vertical line.  That assembly is the next step.

### Gamma beats every polynomial on every vertical line

`HTGeometry/GammaVerticalPolynomialDecay.lean` completes the route sketched above and removes the
restriction to half-integer lines.

- `iteratedDeriv_ofReal_comp` — iterated derivatives commute with the real-to-complex embedding, so
  the complexified log-coordinate integrand inherits smoothness and the integrability of all its
  derivatives;
- `fourier_expShiftC` — in logarithmic coordinates `Γ(σ+it)` *is* the Fourier transform of
  `e^{σu−e^u}` at `−t/2π`;
- **`tendsto_pow_mul_Gamma_vertical`** — `Real.fourier_iteratedDeriv` turns the `N`-th derivative into
  the factor `(2πix)^N`, and Riemann–Lebesgue kills the left side, so

  > `t^N · Γ(σ+it) → 0` for every `N` and **every** `σ > 0`.

So the exponential valuation direction — `Γ` dominating all polynomial and logarithmic data — is
available on every vertical line of the right half plane, not only on `Re s = 1/2 + m` where the
corpus's two-sided modulus bounds live.  Transporting the boundary and collapse theorems to a general
line is now a matter of replacing that one input.

### The domination results on an arbitrary vertical line

`HTGeometry/GammaVerticalDominationGeneral.lean` transports the exponential valuation direction off
the half-integer lines.  Using the super-polynomial decay together with the bound `‖Γ(σ+it)‖ ≤ Γ(σ)`,
splitting one factor of `Γ` off a positive power dominates every polynomial and logarithmic factor:

> **`tendsto_gamma_pow_mul_vertical_general`**: `Γ(σ+it)^j · s^k · L(t)^b → 0` for every `j ≥ 1`,
> every `k, b` and **every** `σ > 0`, and
> **`tendsto_gamma_pow_mul_digamma_vertical_general`** the same with `ψ^b` in place of `L^b`.

These are the general-`σ` forms of the two inputs the Gamma-field boundary and collapse theorems
consume; re-parametrizing those theorems from `Re s = 1/2 + m` to an arbitrary line is now purely
mechanical.

### The law on an arbitrary vertical line

Two changes free the whole chain from the half-integer lines.

The abstract collapse layer (`GammaFieldAbstractCollapse`) is now stated for an arbitrary line
`Re s = σ`: nothing in it used the value of `σ`, only that the coefficient ratios converge along the
line and the normalizing coefficient is eventually nonzero.  `line_relation_of_coeff_limits`,
`strip_relation_of_coeff_limits` and `rank_finite_of_coeff_limits` are therefore line-agnostic, and
the existing half-integer instances are unchanged.

`HTGeometry/GammaFieldGeneralLine.lean` then rebuilds the boundary on a general line, using the
general-`σ` domination results:

- `gammaEltG σ` — the Gamma-field monomial on `Re s = σ`, with `eventually_gammaEltG_ne_zero`;
- `tendsto_ratio_gammaEltG_of_gamma_lt` and **`tendsto_ratio_gammaEltG_lex`** — eq. (5) with the same
  lexicographic dominance, now for every `σ > 0`;
- **`rank_finite_of_gammaEltG_relation`** — eq. (9): a strip almost periodic holomorphic solution of a
  differential relation with Gamma-field monomial coefficients on **any** vertical line of the right
  half plane has finite rational Bohr frequency rank.

The restriction to `Re s = 1/2 + m`, which came only from the availability of two-sided modulus bounds
for `Γ`, is gone.

### Finite sums on an arbitrary line — the law in its stated generality

`HTGeometry/GammaFieldGeneralLineSums.lean` carries the sum and quotient results to a general line.
The limit datum `gammaMonomialLimit` and the order `GammaMonomialDominates` do not mention the line at
all, so once the monomial ratio is available for every `σ > 0` the rest is verbatim:

- `tendsto_sum_ratio_gammaEltG` — eq. (3) for finite sums;
- `eventually_sum_gammaEltG_ne_zero`, `tendsto_ratio_gammaEltG_sum` — eq. (5) for quotients of sums;
- **`rank_finite_of_gammaEltG_sum_distinct`** — eq. (9) on **any** vertical line `Re s = σ > 0`, for
  coefficients that are finite sums of Gamma-field monomials in normal form (nonzero rational parts,
  pairwise distinct exponent pairs in the normalizing coefficient), with the limit constant `1` coming
  from `sum_gammaMonomialLimit_eq_one` — itself line-independent.

The formalized statement now matches the card's: a holomorphic, vertically almost periodic solution of
a differential relation over the Gamma field, on an arbitrary vertical strip in the right half plane,
is differentially algebraic over the constants and has finite rational Bohr frequency rank.

### Eq. (9) in the generality of the card

`HTGeometry/GammaFieldGeneralLineJets.lean` puts the last two generalizations together — the full
digamma tower and the arbitrary line:

- `gammaJetEltG σ` and `gammaJetEltG_eq` — a general monomial `Γ^j·(p/q)·∏(ψ⁽ⁱ⁾)^{E i}` is a pure
  monomial times a product of positive-order polygammas;
- **`tendsto_ratio_gammaJetEltG`** — hence its normalized ratio converges to
  `gammaMonomialLimit · ∏ 0^{E i₊₁}`: the datum of the pure part, and `0` as soon as a derivative
  factor occurs;
- `tendsto_sum_ratio_gammaJetEltG`, `eventually_sum_gammaJetEltG_ne_zero`,
  `tendsto_ratio_gammaJetEltG_sum` — sums and quotients of such monomials;
- **`rank_finite_of_gammaJetEltG_sum_relation`** — eq. (9) with **no restriction left**: an arbitrary
  vertical line `Re s = σ > 0`, and coefficients that are arbitrary elements of
  `ℂ(s)[Γ, ψ, ψ′, …]` in monomial expansion.

Hypotheses of the final theorem: the solution is holomorphic and strip almost periodic on
`V_{σ,R}`, the coefficients are given in monomial expansion with nonzero rational parts, one monomial
dominates all of them lexicographically (always arrangeable, `exists_dominant_monomial_family`), the
normalizing coefficient has nonzero limit constant (automatic in normal form,
`sum_gammaMonomialLimit_eq_one`), and the jet monomials of the relation are distinct.

### The law in one theorem

`HTGeometry/GammaFieldFinitePhaseLaw.lean` states the card's §1 theorem in a single place:

> **`gammaField_finite_phase_law`**: a holomorphic function, almost periodic on a vertical strip of
> the right half plane, satisfying a differential relation whose coefficients are elements of
> `ℂ(s)[Γ, ψ, ψ′, …]` in monomial expansion, satisfies the collapsed constant-coefficient relation on
> the whole strip — eq. (8), it is differentially algebraic over `ℂ` — **and** has finite rational
> Bohr frequency rank — eq. (9).

> **`gammaField_finite_phase_law_observable`**: the same finiteness for every observable dominated by
> the solution — the divisor clause.

Both conclusions come from the same two facts about the coefficients, supplied by the projective
boundary: their normalized ratios converge along the line, and the normalizing coefficient is
eventually nonzero.

### The law is inhabited on every vertical line

`HTGeometry/GammaFieldLawWitness.lean` applies the master theorem to a concrete object on an
arbitrary line.  A constant is the full-tower monomial with all data zero
(`gammaJetEltG_const`), so `Φ′ − μΦ = 0` — the relation satisfied by `Φ(z) = e^{μz}` — has
coefficients in exactly the monomial normal form the law asks for, with the lexicographic dominance in
its equality case.  Hence

> **`gammaField_finite_phase_law_expLine`** for every `σ > 0`, and with the hull-frequency lower
> bound, **`gammaField_finite_phase_law_expLine_pos`**: `1 ≤ rank_ℤ Γ_B(f) ≤ K`.

So the master law is not vacuous on any vertical line of the right half plane, and the objects it
governs can have nonzero phase rank.

### Eq. (8) in its literal form, and where §2 stands

`gammaField_finite_phase_law` now returns three conclusions rather than two: besides the collapsed
relation on the strip and the finite rank, it returns that the collapsed relation is a **nonzero**
polynomial in the jets (`monomialSum_ne_zero` applied to the limit vector, whose `β`-coordinate is
`1`).  That is eq. (8) literally: the solution satisfies a nontrivial constant-coefficient algebraic
differential equation, i.e. it is differentially algebraic over `ℂ`.

On §2: the formalized route does not need the formal Stirling series at all.  The analytic inputs it
replaces are proved directly — the vertical asymptotic `ψ ∼ log`, the vanishing of every positive-order
polygamma, and the super-polynomial decay of `Γ` on every vertical line.  The two statements §2 uses
as devices are separately available in the corpus: the elementary pole-orbit obstruction
(`no_rational_shift_inv_sq`, eq. 14) and Hölder's theorem for the digamma
(`DigammaHypertranscendental.hypertranscendental_digamma`, the analytic content of eq. 16).

### §6: the hypertranscendence consequence, and uniform limits

`HTGeometry/StripAlmostPeriodicUniformLimit.lean` adds the two pieces §6 needs.

- **`isStripAlmostPeriodicOn_of_uniform_approx`** / `isStripAlmostPeriodic_of_uniform_approx` — strip
  almost periodicity passes to uniform limits on each closed substrip, by the `ε/3` transport of a
  relatively dense set of almost periods.  This is what puts uniformly convergent series —
  exponential sums, Dirichlet series (30) — inside the class the law governs.
- **`no_gammaField_relation_of_infinite_rank`** — eq. (31) in contrapositive form: a holomorphic strip
  almost periodic function whose rational Bohr frequency rank is infinite satisfies *no* differential
  relation with Gamma-field coefficients; it is hypertranscendental over `ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩`.

What remains of §6 is the Dirichlet-series input: that a uniformly convergent series (30) is strip
almost periodic (now a corollary of the uniform-limit theorem plus `isStripAlmostPeriodic_expSum`) and
that its Bohr spectrum is the exponent set — the latter identifies the rank and specializes to `ζ`
(eq. 32), a known theorem the card does not claim as new.

### Dirichlet series enter the class

`HTGeometry/DirichletStripAlmostPeriodic.lean` supplies the remaining input of §6's first half:

- `dirichletPartial_eq_expSum` — a partial sum of `∑ aₙ e^{−λₙ s}` is an exponential sum with real
  frequencies;
- **`isStripAlmostPeriodic_dirichlet`** — hence a Dirichlet series converging uniformly on the closed
  substrips is almost periodic on the strip, by the uniform-limit theorem.

So the series (30) of the card — in particular any `L`-series in its half plane of uniform
convergence — lies in the class the finite-phase law governs, and the contrapositive
`no_gammaField_relation_of_infinite_rank` applies to it: infinite rational Bohr rank forces
hypertranscendence over `ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩`, which is eq. (31).

What is still needed for the `ζ` specialization (32) is the spectrum identification — that the Bohr
spectrum of a uniformly convergent Dirichlet series is its exponent set — which converts "infinite
rank exponent group" into "infinite Bohr rank".  Eq. (32) itself is a known theorem (Wang–Yao) and is
not claimed as new on this card.

### The spectrum of a Dirichlet series

`HTGeometry/DirichletBohrSpectrum.lean` closes the second half of §6's mechanism.

- `dirichletPartial_line` — on a vertical line the partial sums are trigonometric polynomials with
  frequencies `−λₙ` and coefficients `aₙ e^{−λₙσ}`; `isBohrAlmostPeriodic_trigPoly` gives their almost
  periodicity on the line;
- **`bohrCoefficient_dirichlet_line`** — Bohr coefficients are `1`-Lipschitz for the uniform distance,
  so uniform convergence transfers the coefficients to the limit: the Bohr coefficient of the series
  at `−λₘ` is exactly `aₘ e^{−λₘσ}` (distinct exponents);
- **`isBohrHullFrequency_dirichlet`** — hence every exponent with nonzero coefficient is a frequency of
  the hull.

That is the step converting "exponent group of infinite rational rank" into "infinite Bohr rank", so
the contrapositive `no_gammaField_relation_of_infinite_rank` applies: such a series is
hypertranscendental over `ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩` — eq. (31), and with `{log p}` rationally independent by unique
factorization, the `ζ` specialization (32), which the card cites as known.

### Independent exponents force large rank — §6 complete

`HTGeometry/IndependentFrequencyRank.lean` supplies the last link of §6.

- **`cardinal_le_rank_of_independent_frequencies`** — a family of hull frequencies independent over
  `ℤ` lifts to a linearly independent family inside the frequency group, so its cardinality bounds the
  rational rank from below;
- `not_rank_le_of_independent_frequencies` — hence `K+1` independent frequencies rule out the bound
  `K`;
- **`not_rank_le_of_dirichlet_independent`** — for a uniformly convergent Dirichlet series, `K+1`
  independent exponents with nonzero coefficients rule out the bound `K`.

Chained with `no_gammaField_relation_of_infinite_rank`, that is eq. (31) in full: a Dirichlet series
whose exponent group has infinite rational rank satisfies no differential relation with Gamma-field
coefficients.  The `ζ` case (32) is the instance where the exponents `log p` are independent by unique
factorization — a known theorem the card does not claim as new.

### Eq. (31) packaged, and the `ζ` instance (32)

`HTGeometry/DirichletHypertranscendence.lean` states eq. (31) as one theorem:

- **`dirichlet_no_gammaField_relation`** — a holomorphic Dirichlet series converging uniformly on the
  closed substrips, whose exponents contain a `ℤ`-independent family of every finite size with nonzero
  coefficients, satisfies no nontrivial differential relation with Gamma-field coefficients.  The
  proof chains `isStripAlmostPeriodic_dirichlet` (a.p. on the strip), `not_rank_le_of_dirichlet_independent`
  (rank above every `K`) and `no_gammaField_relation_of_infinite_rank` (the finite phase law,
  contrapositive).

`HTGeometry/ZetaGammaFieldHypertranscendence.lean` discharges the `ζ` specialization (32)
unconditionally, with every analytic input built rather than assumed:

- `exp_neg_log_succ_mul` — the Dirichlet exponential of frequency `log(i+1)` is the `(i+1)`-st zeta
  term, so `ζ` is literally a series of the form (30) with `λ_i = log(i+1)`, `a_i = 1`;
- `norm_zeta_term`, `norm_zeta_term_le`, `summable_zeta_compare`, `summable_zeta_terms` — the
  comparison series on a closed half-plane `Re z ≥ c > 1`;
- **`zeta_uniform_approx`** — every partial sum beyond a threshold approximates `ζ` to within `ε`
  *uniformly* on that half-plane (tail of the comparison series), hence `zeta_dirichlet_conv`, the
  hypothesis (30) on any vertical strip inside `Re s > 1`;
- `norm_riemannZeta_le_halfPlane`, `continuous_zeta_line`, `zetaLine` — the restriction of `ζ` to a
  vertical line `σ > 1` as a bounded continuous function, the carrier the law needs;
- **`linearIndependent_neg_log_firstPrimes`** — the frequencies `−log p` of the first `K+1` primes are
  `ℤ`-independent, by unique factorization (`linearIndependent_log_primes` of `Core`), restricted from
  `ℚ` to `ℤ` and negated;
- `zetaFrequency_injective` — the exponents `log(i+1)` are pairwise distinct, so the Bohr coefficient
  identification applies;
- **`zeta_no_gammaField_relation`** — putting these into `dirichlet_no_gammaField_relation`: on any
  vertical strip inside `Re s > 1`, `ζ` satisfies no nontrivial differential relation whose
  coefficients are Gamma-field elements.  `#print axioms` clean.

This is eq. (32) as a Lean theorem.  It is a known result (Wang–Yao) which the card does not claim as
new; what is new here is that it now follows inside the corpus from the finite phase law, with no
hypothesis left open — the Bohr-rank mechanism, not a separate argument.

**Overlap, recorded.**  The corpus already contained eq. (32) in differential-algebra form:
`RHTower/MarkusConjecture.markus_conjecture : HypertranscendentalOver gammaBase zetaUpperEndMero`
(and `zeta_hypertranscendentalOver_digammaGamma`), proved by algebraic independence of the zeta and
digamma jets over the coordinate field, `#print axioms` clean.  So `zeta_no_gammaField_relation` is
a *second, independent* proof of the same fact along the card's own route — strip almost periodicity,
Bohr spectrum, frequency rank — not a new theorem for the corpus.  Its value is that it exercises the
finite phase law end to end on the canonical example and supplies the strip-level statement (relation
on `verticalStrip σ R` with explicit Gamma-field coefficient data) that the abstract germ-field
formulation does not give.

### Eq. (19) for the digamma field, not just its ring

`HTGeometry/DigammaFieldQuotientValuation.lean` carries eq. (19) from `ℂ(s)[ψ, ψ′, …]` to the field
`ℂ(s)⟨ψ⟩` it generates.  `digammaFieldElt` is the value of `p(s)·P(ψ, ψ′, …) / q(s)` on a vertical
line and `digammaFieldNorm` its normalization by its own valuation datum `(deg p − deg q, D)`;
`tendsto_digammaFieldNorm` is eq. (19) for that normalization.

- **`tendsto_ratio_digammaField_quotient`** — for two such elements with the denominator's leading
  constant nonzero, the quotient normalized by the *difference* of the valuation data converges to
  the quotient of the leading constants.  So a general element of the field — a quotient, not only a
  polynomial in the jets — has the asymptotic `c·s^a·L(t)^b` of eq. (19), with
  `a = (deg p₁ − deg q₁) − (deg p₂ − deg q₂)` and `b = D₁ − D₂`.

The proof is division of the two normalized limits; the eventual nonvanishing of the denominator
comes from its own limit being nonzero, so no new analytic input enters.

### The flat-cancellation gap, and the route that closes it

§2 justifies eq. (19) *in full generality* — every nonzero `A ∈ L` has a nonzero leading constant — by
the formal Stirling embedding (17), whose hypertranscendence input (15) the card imports from
Adamczewski–Dreyfus–Hardouin.  In Lean the corpus proves eq. (19) for elements presented with a
visible dominant monomial (`tendsto_ratCoeff_jetPoly_vertical`, and now
`tendsto_ratio_digammaField_quotient` for quotients).  The unrestricted statement is exactly the
exclusion of a *flat* value: a nonzero jet polynomial over `ℂ(s)` whose value decays faster than every
power on the vertical lines.

The corpus already owns the elimination that closes this, in a form that needs no formal field:

- `HolderShiftElimination.no_shift_stable_annihilatorWith` — a subset of the jet-polynomial algebra
  over `ℂ(s)`, closed under differences, `ℂ(s)`-multiples and the jet substitution
  `X_j ↦ X_j + r_j`, and containing no nonzero constant, contains no nonzero element at all;
- `HolderShiftElimination.ratShift_sub_polePart_combination` — its analytic hypothesis for the digamma
  increments `polePart j = (−1)ʲ j!/X^{j+1}` is a *theorem*: no rational function has a shift
  increment in their span.

So it suffices to check the four closure properties for the set of jet polynomials whose value at the
digamma jets is flat.  `HTGeometry/VerticalFlatness.lean` supplies the analysis:

- `IsVertFlat σ₀ A` — `t^N ‖A(σ+it)‖ → 0` for every `N` and every line `σ ≥ σ₀`;
- `IsVertFlat.sub` — differences;
- `eventually_norm_eval_le` / `eventually_norm_eval_ge` — a polynomial is squeezed between two powers
  of the height on a vertical line;
- **`IsVertFlat.mul_poly`** and **`IsVertFlat.div_poly`** — flatness is invariant under multiplication
  and division by a nonzero polynomial, hence under nonzero rational factors in both directions;
- **`IsVertFlat.shift`** — the unit translation moves the line right, so flatness is shift-stable;
- **`not_isVertFlat_polynomial`** — a nonzero polynomial is not flat, the "no nonzero constant"
  clause.

What remains is the bookkeeping that evaluates a jet polynomial over `ℂ(s)` at the digamma jets as an
honest function and matches the jet substitution with the unit translation of the argument — the
identity `eval (jetTranslate G) (s) = eval G (s+1)`, available abstractly as
`RationalShiftHypertranscendence.eval₂_jetTranslateWith` in any differential field with the shift.
With it, eq. (17) and the unrestricted eq. (19) become unconditional without the formal Stirling
field and without importing Adamczewski–Dreyfus–Hardouin.

### The coefficient side of the flat-cancellation route: germs on the half-plane

`HTGeometry/HalfPlaneFlatFilter.lean` moves flatness onto a single filter, which is what makes it an
algebraic notion:

- `halfPlaneUpper σ₀` — the filter of the sets `{s | σ₀ ≤ Re s, T ≤ Im s}`, with `‖s‖ → ∞` along it
  (`tendsto_norm_halfPlaneUpper`) and `NeBot`;
- `IsFlatOn σ₀ A` — `‖s‖^N ‖A s‖ → 0` along that filter, for every `N`;
- `eventually_norm_eval_le_filter` / `eventually_norm_eval_ge_filter` — a polynomial is squeezed
  between two multiples of `‖s‖^{deg}`, both directions from the same corpus limit
  `tendsto_eval_div_pow_natDegree_of_norm_atTop`;
- **`IsFlatOn.sub`, `IsFlatOn.mul_poly`, `IsFlatOn.div_poly`, `IsFlatOn.shift`** — the four closure
  properties, the last because `s ↦ s + 1` maps the filter into itself
  (`tendsto_add_one_halfPlaneUpper`);
- **`not_isFlatOn_polynomial`** — a nonzero polynomial is not flat.

`HTGeometry/HalfPlaneGermRing.lean` turns this into a ring statement.  Flatness depends only on the
germ (`IsFlatOn.congr_eventually`, `IsFlatGerm`), a nonzero polynomial is eventually nonzero on the
filter, so its germ is a unit (`isUnit_polyGerm`) — hence the polynomial germ map extends to

> **`ratGerm σ₀ : ℂ(s) →+* HalfPlaneGerm σ₀`**,

the coefficient action the elimination needs, with `ratGerm_eq_div` identifying it with the germ of
`num/denom`.  On top of it: `IsFlatGerm.sub`, **`IsFlatGerm.mul_ratGerm`** (flat germs absorb every
rational coefficient) and **`not_isFlatGerm_ratGerm`** (no nonzero rational germ is flat).

That is three of the four hypotheses of `no_shift_stable_annihilatorWith` — differences, `ℂ(s)`
multiples, no nonzero constants — for the set of jet polynomials with flat value.  What remains is
the jet substitution clause: evaluating the digamma jets in this germ ring and matching
`X_j ↦ X_j + polePart j` with the unit translation.

### Flat cancellation is excluded — eq. (17) without the formal Stirling field

The remaining clause is now proved, and with it the whole flat-cancellation route.

`HTGeometry/HalfPlaneGermShift.lean` makes the unit translation act on the germ ring:

- **`shiftGerm σ₀ : HalfPlaneGerm σ₀ →+* HalfPlaneGerm σ₀`** — precomposition with `s ↦ s + 1`,
  a ring endomorphism because the translation maps the filter into itself;
- `shiftGerm_polyGerm` — on polynomial germs it is the substitution `X ↦ X + 1`;
- **`shiftGerm_ratGerm`** — hence on the whole rational field it is `ratShift`
  (`IsLocalization.ringHom_ext` from the polynomial case), the coefficient compatibility the
  elimination requires;
- `IsFlatGerm.shiftGerm` — flat germs stay flat.

`HTGeometry/DigammaJetGerm.lean` puts the digamma tower in that ring:

- `eventually_off_poles` — the filter misses `−ℕ`, so every jet `ψ⁽ʲ⁾` has a germ;
- `ratGerm_polePart` — the germ of `polePart j` is the germ of `(−1)ʲ j!/s^{j+1}`;
- **`shiftGerm_digammaJetGerm`** — the jet substitution *is* the translation:
  `shift ψ⁽ʲ⁾ = ψ⁽ʲ⁾ + polePart j`, from the corpus's single-step law `digammaJet_shift`.

`HTGeometry/DigammaFlatElimination.lean` closes it:

- `digammaJetEval σ₀ m` — evaluation of a jet polynomial over `ℂ(s)` at the digamma jet germs, a
  ring homomorphism;
- `digammaJetEval_jetTranslate` — it intertwines `X_j ↦ X_j + polePart j` with `shiftGerm`;
- **`not_isFlatGerm_digammaJetEval`** — therefore the flat values form a shift-stable annihilator
  space with no nonzero constants, and `no_shift_stable_annihilatorWith` says no such space has a
  nonzero member:

  > **No nonzero differential polynomial in `ψ, ψ′, …` with `ℂ(s)` coefficients has a value that
  > beats every power of the modulus on the upper right half-plane.**

  `#print axioms` clean.

That is exactly the content §2 imports from Adamczewski–Dreyfus–Hardouin — the exclusion of a flat
Stirling cancellation, i.e. the injectivity of the expansion map on `ℂ(s)[ψ, ψ′, …]` — proved here
analytically, with no formal Laurent field, no shift automorphism of `ℂ((s^{-1}))`, and no imported
difference-Galois theorem.  The corpus's own `ratShift_sub_polePart_combination` (no rational
function has a shift increment in the span of the pole parts) is the only arithmetic input, and it
is a theorem.

### The line form, and eq. (22)–(23): Gamma is transcendental over the digamma field

`HTGeometry/VerticalFlatGermElimination.lean` repeats the elimination with the *line* notion of
flatness (`IsVertFlat`, decay faster than every power of the height on each vertical line), which is
weaker than half-plane flatness and therefore gives a stronger conclusion:

- `eventually_line_of_halfPlaneUpper` — what holds along the half-plane filter holds along each of
  its lines, so line flatness is a germ invariant (`IsVertFlatGerm`);
- the four closure properties again (`IsVertFlatGerm.sub`, `.mul_ratGerm`, `.shiftGerm`,
  `not_isVertFlatGerm_ratGerm`), so
- **`not_isVertFlatGerm_digammaJetEval`** — no nonzero jet polynomial over `ℂ(s)` decays faster than
  every power of the height on even one vertical line; and
- **`digammaJetEval_injective`** — evaluation at the digamma jets is injective, Hölder's theorem in
  germ form.

`HTGeometry/LinePolyGrowth.lean` supplies the growth side:

- `HasPolyGrowth σ₀ A` — on each line the modulus is eventually dominated by a power of the height;
  closed under sums and products;
- `hasPolyGrowth_polynomial`, `hasPolyGrowth_ratFunc` — rational coefficients;
- `hasPolyGrowth_digamma` (`ψ` is logarithmic, from `tendsto_digamma_div_vertLog`) and
  `hasPolyGrowth_iteratedDeriv_succ_digamma` (every higher jet is bounded, from
  `tendsto_iteratedDeriv_succ_digamma_vertical`), hence `hasPolyGrowth_digammaJet`;
- **`isVertFlat_gamma_mul`** — `Γ` times anything of polynomial growth is flat on a line, because
  `tendsto_pow_mul_Gamma_vertical` makes `Γ` beat every power downwards.

`HTGeometry/GammaTranscendentalOverDigamma.lean` closes §4's transcendence step:

- `gammaGerm`, `isUnit_gammaGerm` — `Γ` has no zeros, so its germ is a unit;
- `HasPolyGrowthGerm` with its closure lemmas and `hasPolyGrowthGerm_digammaJetEval` — every value of
  a jet polynomial at the digamma jets has polynomial growth;
- **`digammaJet_gamma_independent`** — if `∑_{i<d} P_i(ψ, ψ′, …)·Γ^i = 0` as germs then every `P_i`
  is the zero polynomial.

  The proof is the card's dichotomy with the *vertical decay* in place of the scaling automorphism:
  the constant term equals `−Γ·(rest)`, which is flat because the rest has polynomial growth; the
  flat elimination kills it; `Γ` is a unit, so the relation divides and the induction continues.

That is eq. (22)–(23) — `Γ` is transcendental over `L`, so every element of `K_Γ` is a rational
function of `Γ` over `L` — proved unconditionally and without the differential-automorphism argument,
which has no counterpart in a concrete function field.  `#print axioms` clean.

The same module also gives eq. (25):

- `IsVertFlat.mul_hasPolyGrowth` / `IsVertFlatGerm.mul_hasPolyGrowthGerm` — flat times polynomial
  growth is flat;
- `isVertFlatGerm_gammaSum_succ` — a Gamma polynomial with no constant term is flat;
- **`isVertFlatGerm_gammaSum_sub_const`** — hence a Gamma polynomial with digamma-jet coefficients
  differs from its constant term by a *flat* germ.  Normalizing by the lowest occurring power, the
  limit datum is exactly the coefficient of that power — `P(Γ) ~ A_u Γ^u`, with the error not merely
  smaller but asymptotically invisible.

### What is left for the unrestricted eq. (19), and the route to it

With the flat elimination and the Gamma layer in place, the remaining gap is precisely the *leading
term*.  The corpus now proves:

- no nonzero element of `ℂ(s)[ψ, ψ′, …]` is flat (`not_isVertFlatGerm_digammaJetEval`), so nothing
  cancels to invisibility; and
- eq. (19) whenever the element is presented with a visible dominant monomial
  (`tendsto_ratCoeff_jetPoly_vertical`, `tendsto_ratio_digammaField_quotient`).

What eq. (19) asserts in general — that *every* nonzero element has an asymptotic `c·s^a·(log s)^b`
with `c ≠ 0` — additionally needs the leading constant to be *computable* from the jet data, i.e. the
Poincaré expansion of each jet, because distinct monomials can share the pair (log-degree, s-degree):
`ψ″` and `(ψ′)²` both sit at `s^{-2}`.

The concrete route, with no external theorem needed:

1. each jet has an exact first-order asymptotic — `ψ ~ log s` (already in the corpus as
   `tendsto_digamma_div_vertLog`) and `ψ⁽ʲ⁾(s) ~ (−1)^{j−1}(j−1)!/s^j` for `j ≥ 1`, which is *not*
   yet in the corpus: the vertical estimates give only `O` bounds;
2. for `j = 1` it follows from the corpus's trigamma series
   (`RHTower/TrigammaVertical.deriv_digamma_partialSum_tendsto`, `ψ′(z) = Σ (z+n)^{−2}`) and the
   exact telescoping `Σ_n [1/(s+n) − 1/(s+n+1)] = 1/s`: the difference is
   `Σ_n 1/((s+n)²(s+n+1))`, which splits at `n ≈ t` into `O(log t / t²)` and `O(1/t²)`, so
   `s·ψ′(s) → 1`;
3. the higher orders follow from the same comparison applied to the polygamma series
   `ψ⁽ᵐ⁺¹⁾(z) = (−1)^m (m+1)! Σ (z+n)^{−(m+2)}` (present but `private` in `DigammaSectorBounds`);
4. with the exact jet asymptotics, a monomial's leading datum is explicit, and a finite sum's leading
   datum is the sum of the constants at the dominant `(log-degree, s-degree)` — nonzero exactly when
   that sum does not vanish, which is where the non-flatness theorem above closes the argument.

Steps 1–3 are the remaining analytic construction; step 4 is bookkeeping over the finite support.

**Step 2 is done.**  `HTGeometry/TrigammaLeadingAsymptotic.lean` proves the first jet's exact leading
datum unconditionally:

- `hasSum_one_div_shift_mul_succ` — the telescoping real majorant `Σ 1/((σ+n)(σ+n+1)) = 1/σ`,
  obtained from the partial sums and `summable_of_sum_range_le`;
- `norm_vertical_add_nat_ge_re` / `..._ge_im` — on a vertical line the modulus of `s+n` is bounded
  below by both the real part `σ+n` and the height `t`, hence `‖s+n‖² ≥ t(σ+n)`;
- **`hasSum_trigamma_tail`** — the trigamma series minus the telescoping series for `s⁻¹` sums
  termwise to `Σ ((s+n)²(s+n+1))⁻¹`, i.e. `ψ′(s) − 1/s` is that tail;
- **`tendsto_mul_deriv_digamma_vertical`** — multiplying the tail by `s` gives terms bounded by the
  fixed summable sequence `2/((σ+n)(σ+n+1))` and each tending to `0`, so dominated convergence for
  series gives

  > `s · ψ′(s) → 1` on every vertical line with `σ > 0`.

  This is the exact first-order law; the corpus's earlier vertical estimates gave only the order.
  `#print axioms` clean.

**Step 3's series input is done too.**  `HTGeometry/PolygammaSeries.lean` derives the polygamma
series publicly, from corpus assets only — no need to un-privatise `DigammaSectorBounds`:

- `summable_one_div_shift_pow` — a shifted `p`-series with exponent `≥ 2` is summable, by comparison
  with the telescoping majorant;
- `summable_polygamma_terms` — hence the terms `((s+n)^{m+2})⁻¹` are summable on a vertical line;
- **`hasSum_polygamma_series`** — the all-order shift law
  (`HTGeometry.iteratedDeriv_digamma_add_nat`) rearranges the partial sums into
  `c·(ψ⁽ᵐ⁺¹⁾(s+N) − ψ⁽ᵐ⁺¹⁾(s))`, and the far-field anchor
  (`iteratedDeriv_digamma_add_nat_tendsto`, already in `GammaProjectiveBoundary`) sends the shifted
  term to zero, so

  > `Σ_n (s+n)^{−(m+2)} = (−1)^m/(m+1)! · ψ⁽ᵐ⁺¹⁾(s)`,

  the trigamma series being the case `m = 0`.  `#print axioms` clean.

**Steps 1–3 are now complete.**  `HTGeometry/ReciprocalPowerTelescope.lean` proves the defect bound
algebraically, with no integral representation:

- `norm_pow_sub_pow_le_of_sub_eq_mul` — for reciprocals with `a − b = a·b`,
  `‖a^j − b^j‖ ≤ j‖a‖^{j+1}` (geometric factorization plus `‖b‖ ≤ ‖a‖`);
- `pow_sub_pow_eq_sum_of_sub_eq_mul` — the telescoping difference expands as
  `Σ_{i<m+1} a^{i+1} b^{m−i+1}`;
- **`norm_telescope_defect_le`** — each summand differs from `a^{m+2}` only by exchanging factors
  `a` for `b`, and each exchange costs another `a·b`, so
  `‖(a^{m+1} − b^{m+1}) − (m+1)a^{m+2}‖ ≤ (m+1)(m+2)‖a‖^{m+3}`.

`HTGeometry/PolygammaLeadingAsymptotic.lean` then closes the route:

- `norm_vertical_add_nat_le_succ`, `inv_sub_inv_eq_mul` — the two structural facts on a vertical
  line (`‖s+n‖ ≤ ‖s+n+1‖` and `a − b = a·b`);
- `hasSum_telescope_pow` — `Σ_n [(s+n)^{−(m+1)} − (s+n+1)^{−(m+1)}] = s^{−(m+1)}`;
- **`tendsto_pow_mul_polygamma_sum`** — multiplying the defect series by `s^{m+1}` gives terms
  dominated by `2^{m+1}(m+1)(m+2)/(σ+n)²` (from `‖s+n‖^{m+3} ≥ t^{m+1}(σ+n)²`) and each tending to
  zero, so dominated convergence gives `s^{m+1} Σ_n (s+n)^{−(m+2)} → 1/(m+1)`;
- **`tendsto_pow_mul_iteratedDeriv_digamma_vertical`** — with the polygamma series this is the exact
  leading law of every jet:

  > `s^{m+1} · ψ⁽ᵐ⁺¹⁾(s) → (−1)^m m!` on every vertical line with `σ > 0`.

  `#print axioms` clean.

So each digamma jet now has its exact first-order datum in the corpus: `ψ ~ log s` and
`ψ⁽ʲ⁾ ~ (−1)^{j−1}(j−1)!/s^j`.

**Step 4 is under way.**  `HTGeometry/DigammaJetLeadingConstants.lean` assembles the monomial datum:

- `normJet` — the `j`-th jet normalized by its own datum (`ψ/L` for `j = 0`, `s^j·ψ⁽ʲ⁾` otherwise);
- `jetLeadingConst` with `jetLeadingConst_ne_zero` — the limits `1` and `(−1)^{j−1}(j−1)!`;
- **`tendsto_normJet`**, **`tendsto_prod_normJet`** — every normalized jet, and hence every
  normalized monomial, converges to the product of those constants, and
  `prod_jetLeadingConst_ne_zero` says that product is never zero;
- `tendsto_sum_prod_normJet` — a finite combination at one dominance level converges to the
  corresponding combination of leading constants.

`HTGeometry/JetNormalizationDominance.lean` supplies the order between data:

- **`tendsto_vertLog_pow_div_vertical_pow`** — a power of the leading logarithm is negligible
  against any positive power of the argument;
- `jetNormFactor` and **`tendsto_jetNormFactor_ratio`** — hence for data ordered by argument degree
  first and log degree second, the dominated normalization factor over the dominant one vanishes.

Both `#print axioms` clean.

`HTGeometry/JetDominantClassLimit.lean` performs the split:

- `eventually_jetNormFactor_ne_zero` — the normalization factor is eventually nonzero on the line;
- **`tendsto_sum_normJet_dominant`** — in a finite combination of normalized jet monomials, each
  rescaled by its own datum relative to the dominant one, the members of the dominant class keep
  their leading constants and every other member vanishes:

  > the normalized combination converges to `Σ_{α : datum α = dominant} d_α · Π_j c_j^{e_α j}`.

  `#print axioms` clean.

`HTGeometry/JetMonomialNormalization.lean` finally states all of this about the *actual* monomials
rather than the normalized surrogates:

- `jetMonomial`, `jetSDeg` — the monomial `Π_j (ψ⁽ʲ⁾)^{e_j}` and its argument degree `Σ_j j·e_j`;
- `normJet_eq_mul`, `prod_normJet_mul_jetNormFactor` — the product of the normalized jets *is* the
  monomial divided by the normalization factor of its own datum `(e₀, Σ_j j·e_j)`;
- **`tendsto_jetMonomial_div_jetNormFactor`** — hence a jet monomial divided by that factor
  converges to the product of the jet leading constants, a nonzero number;
- **`tendsto_sum_jetMonomial_div_dominant`** — and a finite combination, divided by the factor of a
  dominant datum, converges to the sum of those constants over the members sharing the datum.

`HTGeometry/JetDominantExists.lean` removes the last hypothesis:

- **`exists_dominant_index`** — the jet data are ordered by argument degree first (smaller
  dominates) and log degree second (larger dominates), so a finite nonempty family always has a
  dominant member: minimize the argument degree, then maximize the log degree among the minimizers;
- **`exists_datum_tendsto_sum_jetMonomial`** — hence *every* finite nonempty combination of digamma
  jet monomials has a normalization datum for which it converges, with the explicit limit.

`HTGeometry/PolyCoeffJetResidue.lean` puts the coefficients back:

- `polyJetShape` — the asymptotic shape of a term `p_α(s)·M_α(s)`: log degree `e_α 0`, coefficient
  degree `deg p_α`, monomial argument degree `Σ_j j·e_{α j}`.  Comparing two such data needs no
  integers, since `deg p_α − b_α ≤ deg p_β − b_β` is `deg p_α + b_β ≤ deg p_β + b_α`;
- **`tendsto_sum_polyCoeff_jetMonomial`** — dividing by the shape of a dominant term, the
  combination converges to `Σ_{α dominant} lead p_α · Π_j c_j^{e_{α j}}`.

That is eq. (19) in the corpus's own terms, unconditionally: the algebraic-logarithmic residue of a
finite combination of digamma-jet monomials — now with polynomial coefficients — is *visible*: an
explicit finite sum of leading coefficients times products of the jet leading constants `1` and
`(−1)^{j−1}(j−1)!`, vanishing only when those cancel inside the dominant class.

`HTGeometry/RatCoeffJetResidue.lean` finishes the coefficient side:

- **`tendsto_sum_ratCoeff_jetMonomial`** — putting a finite family of rational coefficients over a
  common denominator `q`, the combination normalized by the dominant shape divided by `s^{deg q}`
  converges to the dominant residue divided by `lead q`.

So eq. (19) is unconditional in the corpus for finite combinations of digamma-jet monomials with
coefficients in `ℂ(s)` — that is, for the elements of `L = ℂ(s)⟨ψ⟩` written in normal form — with an
explicit residue.  `#print axioms` clean throughout.

### The Gamma layer of eq. (3), with the residue

`HTGeometry/GammaLowestPowerResidue.lean` lifts this to `K_Γ = L(Γ)`:

- `HasPolyGrowthLine` and `gammaLine`, with `gammaLine_ne_zero` (`Γ` has no zeros on a line to the
  right of the imaginary axis);
- **`tendsto_gammaLine_pow_mul`** — a positive power of `Γ` times any coefficient of polynomial
  growth tends to zero, since `Γ` beats every power of the height downwards;
- **`tendsto_gammaSum_div_pow`** — hence a finite Gamma polynomial whose coefficients have
  polynomial growth, divided by its lowest occurring power `Γ^u`, converges to the limit of the
  `u`-th coefficient.

That is eq. (25) → (3) on the Gamma layer, with the limit *explicit*.

`HTGeometry/GammaFieldProjectiveResidue.lean` composes the two layers:

- `HasPolyGrowthLine.of_tendsto` — a convergent coefficient has polynomial growth, so the growth
  hypotheses of the Gamma layer are discharged by the eq. (19) limits themselves;
- **`tendsto_gammaField_projective`** — a finite Gamma polynomial whose coefficients converge after
  division by a common shape, and whose coefficients below the lowest index vanish, converges after
  division by that power of `Γ` times the shape, to the residue of the lowest coefficient.

That is eq. (3) with an explicit limit: the projective boundary of an element of `ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩` is the
algebraic-logarithmic residue of the coefficient of its lowest Gamma power — with the residue itself
computed by the eq. (19) chain as a finite sum of leading coefficients times products of the jet
constants `1` and `(−1)^{j−1}(j−1)!`.  `#print axioms` clean.

### Eq. (5): the ratio law

`HTGeometry/NormalizedRatioLaw.lean` turns the boundary law into the tuple statement:

- **`tendsto_ratio_of_normalized`** — two functions presented by a shape and their limit after
  dividing by it have a convergent ratio as soon as the shape ratio converges, with limit
  `c_F·r/c_G`; it vanishes exactly when the shape ratio does, that is when the numerator is strictly
  dominated;
- **`tendsto_gammaLine_pow_ratio`** — a strictly larger Gamma power is negligible.

Combined with the shape-ratio limits (`tendsto_jetNormFactor_ratio` on the digamma side) this is
eq. (5): a finite tuple of Gamma-field elements, normalized by a dominant member, has a constant
projective limit — nonzero exactly on the members sharing the dominant datum, and the limit is the
quotient of the explicit residues.  `#print axioms` clean.

### Eq. (27) → (29) from the residues

`HTGeometry/NormalizedFamilyRatios.lean` packages the coefficient data:

- `eventually_ne_zero_of_normalized` — a nonzero normalized limit makes the function eventually
  nonzero;
- **`tendsto_family_ratio_of_normalized`** — normalizing a family by a member with nonzero limit
  gives exactly the collapse input: that member is eventually nonzero, its own shape ratio is `1`,
  and every ratio converges to `c_α·r_α/c_β`.

`HTGeometry/GammaFieldCollapseFromResidues.lean` feeds it to the Bohr-return collapse:

- **`recurrence_collapse_of_normalized`** — a finite differential relation whose coefficients
  converge after division by their shapes, with convergent shape ratios and a nonzero normalizing
  residue, collapses along the return times to the autonomous relation
  `Σ_α (c_α r_α / c_β)·M_α(σ) = 0` with the coefficient at `β` equal to `1`.

So eq. (27) → (29) now holds with the coefficient vector *explicit* — the quotients of the residues
computed by the eq. (19)/(3) chain — and with no hypothesis on the coefficient data beyond its
normalized presentation.  `#print axioms` clean.

`HTGeometry/GammaJetShapeRatios.lean` supplies the shape ratios that presentation needs:

- `gammaJetShape` — a power of `Γ` times the shape of the lowest coefficient, with
  `eventually_gammaJetShape_ne_zero`;
- `hasPolyGrowthLine_polyJetShape_ratio` — a ratio of jet shapes has at most polynomial growth,
  since `‖L‖` and `‖s‖` are between `1` and a multiple of the height;
- **`tendsto_gammaJetShape_ratio_of_gamma_lt`** — hence a strictly larger Gamma power makes the whole
  shape ratio vanish, whatever the jet data;
- **`tendsto_gammaJetShape_ratio_of_gamma_eq`** — and at equal Gamma powers the jet dominance decides.

That is the `(Γ-exponent, s-degree, log-degree)` lexicographic order of eq. (26), with each level
proved on the line.  `#print axioms` clean.

`HTGeometry/GammaJetDominantExists.lean` completes eq. (26) with the existence statement:

- **`exists_dominant_gammaJet_index`** — a finite nonempty family of Gamma-jet data has a dominant
  member: minimize the Gamma exponent, maximize the argument degree among the minimizers, maximize
  the log degree among those.  The argument-degree comparison stays in `ℕ` in the statement
  (`deg p_α + b_β ≤ deg p_β + b_α`), with the intermediate maximization run over `ℤ`.

So "choose a component with dominant triple and divide the tuple by it" — the sentence that opens
eq. (5)'s proof on the card — is now a Lean theorem, together with all three dominance limits.
`#print axioms` clean.

`HTGeometry/GammaJetRatioFamily.lean` assembles the tuple statement:

- `polyJetShape_ratio_eq` — a ratio of jet shapes *is* a ratio of normalization factors with shifted
  argument degrees, so the digamma-layer dominance limits transfer verbatim
  (`tendsto_polyJetShape_ratio_one`, `..._zero_of_deg`, `..._zero_of_log`);
- **`exists_dominant_with_shape_ratios`** — hence a finite nonempty family of Gamma-jet data has a
  dominant member together with the full family of shape-ratio limits: `1` at the dominant member,
