  `0` at every strictly dominated one.

That is the input `hr` of `recurrence_collapse_of_normalized`, so the collapse now runs on concrete
Gamma-field shape data with no hypothesis chosen by hand.  `#print axioms` clean.

`HTGeometry/GammaJetCollapse.lean` is the capstone of the chain:

> **`exists_collapse_of_gammaJet_data`** — a finite differential relation whose coefficients converge
> after division by their Gamma-jet shapes, with nonzero residues, collapses along the Bohr return
> times to an autonomous relation `Σ_α d_α·M_α(σ) = 0` with `d` explicit and `d_β = 1` at the
> dominant datum.

The dominance is *produced*, not assumed: the `(Γ-exponent, argument degree, log degree)` order gives
the dominant member, the shape ratios follow, the residues come from the eq. (19)/(3) chain, and the
Bohr-return collapse turns them into the autonomous relation.  `#print axioms` clean.

`HTGeometry/GammaFieldShapeConvergence.lean` closes the loop back to concrete elements:

- `eventually_polyJetShape_ne_zero`;
- **`tendsto_gammaField_div_gammaJetShape`** — a Gamma polynomial whose coefficients converge
  against a common jet shape converges against the *Gamma-jet shape* of its lowest index, to the
  residue of that coefficient.

That is exactly the `hF` hypothesis of `exists_collapse_of_gammaJet_data`, so an element of
`ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩` presented in normal form — a Gamma polynomial with jet-monomial coefficients over `ℂ(s)` —
feeds the collapse directly.  The chain runs end to end:

`polygamma series → jet asymptotics → jet leading constants → dominance → residue (19) → Gamma layer
(3) → shape ratios (26) → dominant datum → ratio family (5) → Bohr-return collapse (27)→(29)`,

every step unconditional and `#print axioms` clean.

`HTGeometry/GammaFieldQuotientBoundary.lean` extends the boundary from the ring `L[Γ]` to the field
`L(Γ)` of eq. (23):

- **`tendsto_gammaFieldQuotient`** — with numerator and denominator each presented by their Gamma-jet
  data, and the denominator's residue nonzero, the quotient converges against the ratio of the two
  shapes, to `c_A·r/c_B`.

So the boundary law covers every element of `K_Γ = L(Γ)` written as a quotient of Gamma polynomials
over `ℂ(s)⟨ψ⟩`, not only the polynomial case.  `#print axioms` clean.  The non-flatness theorem `not_isVertFlatGerm_digammaJetEval` closes the
degenerate branch: a nonzero jet polynomial cannot decay faster than every power, so a normalization
whose visible residue cancels still leaves a nonzero element behind, to be found one dominance level
down.  Iterating the split over the finitely many levels is the remaining bookkeeping, and it needs
no further analytic input.

*(b) The Euclidean side reduces to fine covers of small order.*  For a compact metric space, a
Lebesgue number turns any finite open cover into a mesh condition, so `dim X ≤ n` follows from: for
every `δ > 0` there is a finite open cover of mesh `< δ` and order `≤ n + 1`.  For a subset of `ℝ^M`
that is the classical brick decomposition into `M + 1` families of disjoint boxes.  This is the only
genuinely geometric input left on the Euclidean side.

2. **The dimension identity** `dim X = rank_ℚ X̂` for compact abelian groups, in the direction
   needed here: an embedding `X ↪ ℂ^N` forbids a surjection onto `𝕋^r` for `r > 2N`.  Note that
   plain continuity is *not* enough for the dimension step (Peano curves; and open maps can raise
   dimension, by Keldysh's example): the group structure of the fibres is load-bearing.

   **This step is closed.**  `HTGeometry/JetSampleAtlas.rank_bohrHullFrequencies_le` bounds the
   frequency rank by the real dimension of any finite-dimensional space the carrier injects into
   continuously, and `rank_le_of_jetAtlas` consumes the finite jet-sample embedding to give
   `rank_ℤ Γ_B(f) ≤ 2 |T| N` outright; `PhaseRankCapstone.rank_le_of_regular_jets` and
   `NondegenerateSolutionPhaseRank.rank_finite_of_nondegenerate_solution` are its analytic form.
   Both are `#print axioms` clean, so the whole eq. (9) chain — surjection, character injection,
   dimension bound, jet embedding — is unconditional in Lean.

Formerly open and now closed as well: the analytic Poincaré transfer of eq. (19) beyond the ring
`ℂ(s)[ψ, ψ′, …]` (see the quotient section below), and the classical eq. (32), which no longer
needs relative differential Galois theory — it is proved here from the finite phase law
(`zeta_no_gammaField_relation`).

`formal/GaloisForLFunctions/HTGeometry/GammaProjectivePrimeTorusCarrier.lean` proves the §7
finite-aperture prime-torus carrier law (eq. 34) unconditionally:

- `primesLE N` (the primes `≤ N`) has cardinality `Nat.primeCounting N = π(N)`;
- the aperture-`N` character lattice `primeApertureCharacterModule N = span_ℚ {log p : p ≤ N}` is
  `ℚ`-linearly independent (unique factorization) of rank exactly `π(N)`;
- each coordinate character's diagonal-flow frequency `log p` lies in that module
  (`primeLogFrequency (single p 1)`), grounding the lattice in the Kronecker spectrum; at the
  canonical aperture `N = p` this is hypothesis-free
  (`primeLogFrequency_single_mem_selfAperture`);
- **carrier lower bound**: any rational carrier character space receiving an injection of the
  aperture lattice has `Module.finrank ℚ ≥ π(N)` — the covering-dimension `≥ π(N)` bound, with the
  injection standing for the dual of the carrier's factor map onto the aperture hull;
- **dimension divergence**: `∀ M, ∃ N, M ≤ π(N)`, so no fixed finite-dimensional carrier serves all
  apertures;
- **fixed-carrier exclusion** (`primeAperture_no_fixed_finiteCarrier`): for every finite rational
  character space `A`, some aperture module admits no linear injection into `A`.

That list of remaining formal work — full Laurent shift field, Poincaré asymptotic field, general
Gamma-field monomial valuation, holomorphic almost-periodic hull, Bohr-frequency rank infrastructure
— is now out of date on every item except the first, which the corpus *bypasses*:

- **Laurent shift field**: not built, and not needed.  Its purpose was eq. (17), the injectivity of
  the formal Stirling expansion; the corpus proves that content analytically instead
  (`not_isVertFlatGerm_digammaJetEval`: no nonzero jet polynomial over `ℂ(s)` decays faster than
  every power on a vertical line), using the corpus's own shift elimination rather than
  Adamczewski–Dreyfus–Hardouin.
- **Poincaré asymptotic field**: built, in the exact form the law needs — the jet leading constants
  `1` and `(−1)^{j−1}(j−1)!` (`tendsto_pow_mul_iteratedDeriv_digamma_vertical`), the residue of a
  finite combination (`tendsto_sum_ratCoeff_jetMonomial`), and its Gamma layer
  (`tendsto_gammaField_projective`).
- **General Gamma-field monomial valuation**: built — the three-level order with each dominance limit
  proved (`tendsto_gammaJetShape_ratio_of_gamma_lt`, `..._of_gamma_eq`,
  `tendsto_polyJetShape_ratio_zero_of_deg`, `..._of_log`) and the dominant datum produced
  (`exists_dominant_gammaJet_index`).
- **Holomorphic almost-periodic hull** and **Bohr-frequency rank**: built earlier in the session
  (`AlmostPeriodicHull`, `PhaseRankCapstone.rank_le_of_regular_jets`,
  `NondegenerateSolutionPhaseRank.rank_finite_of_nondegenerate_solution`).

**That bridge is now built.**  `HTGeometry/FinitePhaseLawFromRatios.lean` isolates the law's
coefficient interface and re-enters it from the new presentation:

- **`finite_phase_law_of_coeff_ratios`** — the law needs only three facts about the coefficients: the
  normalizing one is eventually nonzero, the ratios converge, and the limit at the normalizing index
  is nonzero.  Stated that way it is independent of how the coefficients are written
  (`strip_relation_of_coeff_limits`, `monomialSum_ne_zero`, `rank_finite_of_coeff_limits` are the
  three pieces it assembles);
- **`finite_phase_law_of_gammaJet_data`** — feeding it the Gamma-jet presentation: each coefficient
  given by its datum `(u, a, deg p, b)` and residue `c α ≠ 0`.  The datum order produces the
  normalizing index, `exists_dominant_with_shape_ratios` gives the shape-ratio limits,
  `tendsto_family_ratio_of_normalized` turns them into coefficient ratios, and the conclusion is the
  law's own: the collapsed relation holds on the strip, the collapsed polynomial is nonzero, and the
  rational Bohr frequency rank is finite — with `d β = 1`.

So the law now runs on either coefficient format, and on the new one *no dominance hypothesis is
chosen by hand*.  `#print axioms` clean.

`HTGeometry/FinitePhaseLawGammaJetWitness.lean` checks that the new hypothesis class is inhabited —
a law whose hypotheses were never satisfiable would assert nothing:

- `gammaJetShape_zero` — the trivial Gamma-jet datum has shape `1`, so a constant coefficient is
  presented by it with its own value as residue;
- **`finite_phase_law_gammaJet_expLine`** — the exponential line satisfies the hypotheses of
  `finite_phase_law_of_gammaJet_data` on every vertical line, with the relation `Φ′ − μΦ = 0` and
  constant coefficients, and its rational Bohr frequency rank is finite;
- **`finite_phase_law_gammaJet_expLine_of_data`** — the general form: multiplying the relation
  `Φ′ − μΦ = 0` by *any* coefficient that converges against its Gamma-jet shape keeps every
  hypothesis, so each datum level can be exercised by a one-line corollary;
- the three levels are then all inhabited: `finite_phase_law_gammaJet_expLine_coordCoeff` (argument
  degree, shape `s`), `..._digammaCoeff` (log degree, shape the leading logarithm, residue `1` by
  `tendsto_digamma_div_vertLog`), `..._gammaCoeff` (Gamma exponent, shape `Γ`), with the shape
  identities `gammaJetShape_zero`, `..._arg_one`, `..._log_one`, `..._gamma_one`.

So the hypothesis class is not confined to constants: every level of the datum is realized by a
genuine Gamma-field coefficient.

`#print axioms` clean.  The card remains at stage 2 until the two non-Lean gates — the literature
comparison and the independent audit — are closed.

`HTGeometry/BohrProductUniformLimit.lean` and `HTGeometry/BohrFrequencyModuleProduct.lean` close
the last branch that the multiplicative half of eq. (9) was recorded as still needing.

- `bohrFrequencyModule_mul_le_of_uniform_approx` — the transfer principle: if `g` is uniformly
  approximable by trigonometric polynomials whose frequencies stay in a set `S`, then
  `Γ_B(f·g) ≤ Γ_B(f) ⊔ span_ℚ S` for every bounded almost-periodic `f`.  The proof is the
  trigonometric-polynomial case `bohrFrequencyModule_mul_trigPoly_le` plus the `1`-Lipschitz bound
  `norm_bohrCoefficient_sub_le`: an off-module frequency has coefficient `0` for every approximant,
  hence norm `≤ Cε` for every `ε`, hence `0`.
- `bohrSpectrum_mul_subset` and **`bohrFrequencyModule_mul_le`** — the general product rule with no
  approximation hypothesis at all:

  `Γ_B(f·g) ≤ Γ_B(f) ⊔ Γ_B(g)`  for continuous almost-periodic `f, g`.

  This does *not* need Bochner–Fejér.  The corpus's own `bohrCoefficient_mul_eq_zero`
  (`BohrProductRule.lean`) already routes around it through the *mean-square* spectral approximation
  `exists_spectral_mean_square_approx`, whose frequencies are known to lie in the spectrum by
  construction; contraposing it gives `spec(f·g) ⊆ spec f + spec g` and spanning gives the module
  statement.  The earlier note on this branch — that the general rule waits on the Bochner–Fejér
  approximation theorem — was therefore stale: the mean-square route is strictly cheaper and is
  already complete.

`#print axioms` clean for all three.

`HTGeometry/GammaJetDatumUniqueness.lean` supplies the clause of eq. (3) and eq. (19) that the
existence theorems left open: *uniqueness* of the datum and of the residue.  §4 argues it by
"the exponential separation of distinct Gamma powers, the power separation of distinct `s`
exponents, and the logarithmic separation of distinct `log s` exponents"; that is exactly the
three-level dominance already proved, read in the contrapositive.

- `gammaJetShape_ratio_mul_symm` — the two shape ratios multiply to `1` on the line, so neither can
  tend to `0` while the other has a nonzero limit;
- **`gammaJetShape_datum_unique`** — if the ratio of two Gamma-jet shapes has a *nonzero* limit `c`,
  then `u₁ = u₂`, `a₁ = a₂`, `dP₁ − b₁ = dP₂ − b₂` and `c = 1`.  Each of the six strict cases is
  killed by one of the dominance limits (`..._of_gamma_lt`, `..._ratio_zero_of_deg`,
  `..._ratio_zero_of_log`), and the diagonal case is `tendsto_polyJetShape_ratio_one`;
- **`gammaJet_datum_residue_unique`** — the element-level form: if `F` converges against two
  Gamma-jet shapes with nonzero residues `c₁, c₂`, the two data agree and `c₁ = c₂`.  So the
  triple `(k, a, b)` and the constant `c(F)` of eq. (3) are functions of `F`, not of the
  presentation;
- **`polyJetShape_datum_unique`** and **`polyJet_datum_residue_unique`** — the same at Gamma
  exponent `0`, i.e. the uniqueness clause of eq. (19) inside the digamma field `L`
  (`gammaJetShape_gamma_zero` identifies the two shapes).

`#print axioms` clean.  With `tendsto_gammaField_projective` (existence) this closes eq. (3) in the
form §1 states it — *unique* integers and a *unique* nonzero constant.

`HTGeometry/GammaLineBoundaryCases.lean` formalizes the two boundary cases of §8 that are
statements about the vertical Gamma law itself rather than about the almost-periodicity hypothesis.

- `tendsto_gammaLine_zero`, `gammaLine_shift_one` — Gamma decays on every vertical line
  `Re s = σ > 0`, and the one-step shift multiplies it by the argument;
- **`tendsto_norm_gammaLine_shift_ratio_atTop`** (§8.1) — `‖Γ(s+1)/Γ(s)‖ = ‖s‖ → ∞` on a vertical
  line.  So point normalization really is false: dividing by the value at one base point leaves a
  factor that diverges, and the law's normalization by a *component of the tuple, as a function*, is
  not a convenience;
- **`tendsto_one_div_gammaLine_sub`** and `tendsto_one_div_gammaLine_sub_one` (§8.3) — for `c ≠ 0`
  the `c`-point function `1/(Γ − c)` converges to `−1/c` on every vertical line, and in particular
  `1/(Γ − 1) → −1`.  A nontrivial global pole set is no obstruction: the law is vertical.

`#print axioms` clean.

`HTGeometry/EulerFactorRankOne.lean` builds the witness for §8.6 — *finite support is not claimed*.
The card's example is `exp(2^{-s})`; the formalized witness is the Euler factor at an arbitrary base
`b > 1`, `E(s) = (1 − b^{-s})^{-1}`, which has the same three features and a geometric rather than an
exponential tail, and which is the single-prime case of the finite aperture `(34)` of §7.

- `basePowerLine_eq`, `basePowerLine_pow` — on the line `Re s = σ` the Dirichlet term `b^{-ns}` is
  the constant `b^{-σn}` times the character of frequency `−n log b`;
- `norm_eulerFactorLine_sub_dirichletSum_le`, `tendsto_dirichlet_error` — the partial sums converge
  to `E` *uniformly on the line*, with geometric error `(b^{-σ})^N/(1 − b^{-σ})`.  Hence
  `isBohrAlmostPeriodic_eulerFactorLine`;
- **`bohrCoefficient_eulerFactorLine`** — the Bohr coefficient at `−m log b` is exactly the
  Dirichlet coefficient `b^{-σm}`, by the `1`-Lipschitz bound against the partial sums;
- **`infinite_bohrSpectrum_eulerFactorLine`** — the spectrum is therefore infinite;
- **`rank_bohrFrequencyModule_eulerFactorLine_le_one`** — yet every frequency is an integer multiple
  of `log b`, so `rank_ℚ Γ_B(E) ≤ 1`, through the new
  `bohrFrequencyModule_le_of_uniform_approx_nat`;
- **`eulerFactor_autonomous`** — and `E` is autonomous differentially algebraic: on the
  half-plane `Re s > 0`,

  `E′ + (log b)(E² − E) = 0`,

  a first-order polynomial relation with *constant* coefficients (`hasDerivAt_eulerFactor`).

So infinite Dirichlet support coexists with rank-one frequency module: the law's finite-rank
conclusion is sharp, and cannot be strengthened to finite support.  `#print axioms` clean.

`bohrFrequencyModule_le_of_uniform_approx_nat` (in `BohrProductUniformLimit.lean`) is the general
tool behind that: a function uniformly approximated by the partial sums of an exponential series has
its frequency module inside the `ℚ`-span of the series' frequencies.

`HTGeometry/PrimeDirichletApertureSpectrum.lean` grounds §7's aperture lattice in an actual Bohr
spectrum.  Until now `primeApertureCharacterModule N = span_ℚ{log p : p ≤ N}` was an abstract
lattice, and `primeAperture_carrier_covering_dim_ge` *assumed* an injection of it into the carrier's
character space.  The finite prime Dirichlet polynomial supplies the missing object:

`D_{N,σ}(t) = ∑_{p ≤ N} p^{-σ-it}`

is a trigonometric polynomial on the line whose frequencies are exactly `−log p`, `p ≤ N`.

- `bohrCoefficient_primeDirichletLine_prime` — its Bohr coefficient at `−log p` is `p^{-σ} ≠ 0`, by
  injectivity of `p ↦ log p` (`primeLineFrequency_injective`, from `Real.log_injOn_pos`);
- `bohrSpectrum_primeDirichletLine_subset` — and there are no other frequencies;
- **`bohrFrequencyModule_primeDirichletLine`** — hence `Γ_B(D_{N,σ}) = primeApertureCharacterModule N`
  *on the nose*, for every `σ`;
- **`finrank_bohrFrequencyModule_primeDirichletLine`** — so its rational Bohr rank is exactly `π(N)`;
- **`primeDirichlet_carrier_covering_dim_ge`** — the §7 carrier bound restated on the concrete
  observable: any finite rational character space receiving an injection of `Γ_B(D_{N,σ})` has
  dimension at least `π(N)`.

The aperture lattice is therefore not a modelling choice: it is the frequency module of a genuine
analytic object on the line, and the finite-phase law's rank invariant attains `π(N)` on it.
`#print axioms` clean.

`HTGeometry/PrimeEulerProductAperture.lean` then does the same for the cutoff Euler product `(34)`
itself, `Z_{N,σ}(t) = ∏_{p ≤ N} (1 − p^{-σ-it})^{-1}`, which is the object §7 actually names.

- `norm_prod_sub_prod_le` — a uniform factor bound turns a per-factor error `δ` into the product
  error `|s|·C^{|s|}·δ`;
- `eulerProductApprox_eq` — expanding the product of the factors' geometric partial sums
  (`Finset.prod_univ_sum`, `prod_expChar`) gives a trigonometric polynomial indexed by exponent
  vectors `e : {p ≤ N} → ℕ`, with frequency `smoothFreq = −∑ e_p log p` and coefficient
  `smoothCoeff = ∏ p^{-σ e_p}`;
- `norm_eulerProductLine_sub_approx_le`, `tendsto_apertureError` — those trigonometric polynomials
  converge to `Z_{N,σ}` uniformly on the line, since `p^{-σ} ≤ 2^{-σ} < 1` for every `p ≤ N`.  Hence
  `isBohrAlmostPeriodic_eulerProductLine`;
- **`prime_rpow_le_smoothTrig_coeff`** — the no-cancellation step, and the reason this needs no
  smooth-number bookkeeping: *every* exponent-vector coefficient is a positive real, so the
  coefficient at `−log p` is bounded below by the single vector `e = δ_p`, i.e. by `p^{-σ}`;
- **`prime_rpow_le_norm_bohrCoefficient_eulerProductLine`** — passing to the limit,
  `‖c_{−log p}(Z_{N,σ})‖ ≥ p^{-σ} > 0`, so every aperture prime really is a frequency;
- **`bohrFrequencyModule_eulerProductLine`** — with the converse inclusion from
  `smoothFreq_mem_aperture` and the new `bohrFrequencyModule_le_of_uniform_approx_index`, the
  frequency module of the cutoff Euler product is *exactly* `primeApertureCharacterModule N`;
- **`finrank_bohrFrequencyModule_eulerProductLine`** — so `rank_ℚ Γ_B(Z_{N,σ}) = π(N)`;
- **`eulerProduct_carrier_covering_dim_ge`** — §7's carrier bound now applies to `(34)` itself: any
  finite rational character space receiving an injection of `Γ_B(Z_{N,σ})` has dimension at least
  `π(N)`.

That closes the `[PROVED; BRIDGE]` clause of §7 in Lean: the finite-aperture Euler product does
transport `π(N)` independent phase dimensions, and the aperture lattice is its Bohr frequency
module, not a modelling choice.  `#print axioms` clean.

The literal hull statement of §7 — *its translation hull is the full prime torus `T^{π(N)}`* — is
now a theorem about the observable too.  `eulerProductBCF` bundles `Z_{N,σ}` as a bounded continuous
function (`norm_eulerProductLine_le`), `hasTotallyBoundedOrbit_eulerProductBCF` gives Bochner
compactness of its orbit, and `isBohrHullFrequency_primeLog` promotes each spectral frequency
`−log p` to a hull frequency `log p`.  Feeding those, with the rational independence
`primeAperture_linearIndependent`, to the corpus's `bohrHull_torus_surjective_hom` yields
**`eulerProduct_bohrHull_torus_surjective`**: the joint character map of the packet
`{log p : p ≤ N}` is multiplicative, unimodular in every coordinate, and *surjective onto the whole
torus* — every point of `T^{π(N)}` is attained by a point of the hull.  `#print axioms` clean.

`HTGeometry/ScaledGammaLine.lean` closes §8.8 in the form the card claims it — *scaled archimedean
factors admit the same argument after an affine change of variable*, and nothing more.

- `scaledGammaLine_eq` — `Γ((σ+μ+it)/2)` is `Γ` on the line `Re s = (σ+μ)/2` at parameter `t/2`;
- **`tendsto_scaledGammaLine_pow_mul`** — hence a positive power of the scaled factor still kills
  every coefficient of polynomial growth (`HasPolyGrowthLine.comp_mul_two` carries the growth bound
  across the substitution), and `tendsto_scaledGammaLine_zero`, `tendsto_scaledGammaLine_pow_ratio`
  give the decay and the dominance of a higher scaled power.

The transfer is one affine substitution, so it says nothing about *simultaneous incommensurable*
Gamma scales — which is exactly the limitation §8.8 states.  `#print axioms` clean.

`HTGeometry/BoundedNonAlmostPeriodic.lean` supplies the §8.5 separation — *compact-open normality is
insufficient*.  The witness is `1/(s+2)`:

- `norm_shiftedInverse_le`, `differentiableOn_shiftedInverse` — bounded by `1/2` and holomorphic on
  every vertical strip inside the right half plane, so Montel makes its translate family normal for
  the compact-open topology;
- `tendsto_shiftedInverse_line` — on each vertical line it tends to `0` at `+∞`;
- **`not_isBohrAlmostPeriodic_shiftedInverse_line`** — hence it is *not* Bohr almost periodic on any
  such line: the corpus's vanishing lemma would force it to vanish identically, and its value at
  `t = 0` is `(σ+2)^{-1} ≠ 0`.

So the return property is strictly stronger than boundedness plus holomorphy, exactly as §8.5 says;
the recurrence hypothesis of the law is not decorative.  `#print axioms` clean.

`HTGeometry/SinExpNotRecurrent.lean` closes §8.4 — *arbitrary DA functions*.  The card's example is
`sin(s e^s)`, and both halves of its claim are now theorems.

- **`sinExp_differentialAlgebraic`** — eliminating `e^s` and `cos(s e^s)` from the first two
  derivatives leaves the second-order polynomial relation with rational coefficients

  `(1+s)(1 − y²) y″ − (2+s)(1 − y²) y′ + (1+s) y (y′)² = 0`,

  valid at *every* point: the elimination needs no division, because substituting `1 − y² = cos²`
  makes the whole expression `cos³·((1+s)u″ − (2+s)u′)` with `u = s e^s`, and that vanishes
  identically.  So `sin(s e^s)` is differentially algebraic over `ℂ(s)`;
- `norm_sin_ge`, `exp_line_sample` — at the sample parameters `t = 2πn` the exponential returns to
  `e^σ`, so the argument `s e^s` has imaginary part `2πn e^σ`, and `‖sin‖ ≥ (e^y − e^{-y})/2`;
- **`not_isBohrAlmostPeriodic_sinExpLine`** — hence the restriction to *any* vertical line is
  unbounded (`exists_norm_sinExpLine_gt`), and an almost-periodic continuous function is bounded, so
  it is not Bohr almost periodic there.

Differential algebraicity over `ℂ(s)` — with finite order and whatever zero density — therefore does
not give recurrence.  `#print axioms` clean.

### The generality gap is closed: nonvanishing alone gives the datum

The route described below is now a Lean theorem.  Three modules do it.

`StdRatLineRigidity.lean` proves the rigidity that makes line data half-plane data.
`isStdRat_of_line_decay`: a standard rational function whose values on **one** vertical line decay
faster than its recorded defect has the larger defect — if the defect were smaller, the datum limit
`s^p·P/∏(s+j) → lead P ≠ 0` of `tendsto_stdRat_datum` would contradict the bound.  Hence
`isVertFlat_of_isLineBigO_all`: a function carrying standard rational approximations of every depth
that is `O(s^{-n})` for every `n` on one line is flat on **every** line.  The point is that the
approximating rational function does not depend on the line, so the line that supplies the decay
and the line that consumes it need not be the same.

`JetPolyLineDatum.lean` couples that to Hölder.  A jet polynomial with complex constant coefficients
is `jetPolyMv N M a e`, an element of `MvPolynomial (Fin (N+1)) (ℂ(s))`; `jetPolyMv_ne_zero` shows it
is nonzero as soon as one coefficient is (the exponent vectors `jetExp` of distinct monomials are
distinct), and `digammaJetEval_jetPolyMv` identifies its value at the digamma jets with the germ of
the function on the line.  Then **`exists_not_isLineBigO_jetPoly`**: for a nonzero jet polynomial and
any `σ > 0` there is an `n` with `¬ IsLineBigO σ n` — because flatness on that one line would, by the
rigidity above, be flatness on the half-plane, which `not_isVertFlatGerm_digammaJetEval` forbids.

Feeding that into the residue chain gives **`exists_jetPoly_datum_of_ne_zero`**:

> for every `σ > 0` and every jet polynomial `A = ∑_α a_α ∏_i (ψ^{(i+1)})^{e_{α i}}` with complex
> constant coefficients that is nonzero as a differential polynomial, there are `p ∈ ℕ` and `c ≠ 0`
> with `s^p·A(s) → c` along the line `Re s = σ`.

No presentation is supplied and no datum is assumed: nonvanishing alone produces the residue.  That
is the existential half of eq. (19) for the positive-order layer, `#print axioms` clean.

### The existential now holds for the field, not only for jet polynomials

Four further modules lift the datum from a single jet polynomial to an arbitrary element of the
digamma differential field.

`FullJetPolyNonzeroDatum.lean` adds the logarithmic level.  A monomial in *all* jets is
`psi^{E_0}` times a monomial in the positive-order jets; grouping the monomials by their `psi`-degree
produces finitely many groups with pairwise distinct logarithmic degrees, each a nonzero polynomial
in the positive-order jets, hence each with a datum by the previous section.  Distinct logarithmic
degrees cannot cancel, so **`exists_full_jetPoly_datum`** gives the residue of the dominant group:
any nonzero differential polynomial in `psi, psi', …, psi^{(K)}` with complex constant coefficients
has a residue `A(t)/polyJetShape σ a 0 b → r ≠ 0`.

`DifferentialJetAlgebra.lean` supplies the algebra step: `exists_jetAlgebra_denominator` and
`exists_common_denominator` clear denominators — one nonzero element turns coefficients in the field
into coefficients in the subalgebra generated by the jets — and `exists_cleared_relation` records
that this changes neither the relation nor which coefficients vanish.

`SupportReindex.lean` (`exists_reindex_support`, `exists_fin_reindex_of_finset`) drops the vanishing
coefficients of a presentation, and the injectivity strengthenings
`exists_fin_monomial_sum_inj` / `exists_canonical_exponents_family_inj` show the surviving monomials
stay pairwise distinct — the two hypotheses the datum theorem needs and that a raw presentation does
not promise.

Assembling in `DigammaFieldJetPresentation.lean`: `exists_digamma_algebra_datum` gives the datum of a
nonzero element of the digamma jet algebra, and **`exists_digamma_field_datum`**:

> for every `σ > 0` and every nonzero `x` in the differential field generated over `ℂ` by the digamma
> germ, there are jet polynomials `P, Q` in the polygammas with `x = ⟦P⟧/⟦Q⟧`, naturals
> `(a₁,b₁,a₂,b₂)` and `r ≠ 0` with
> `(P/polyJetShape σ a₁ 0 b₁) / (Q/polyJetShape σ a₂ 0 b₂) → r` along the line.

Numerator and denominator each carry their own shape, so the quotient needs no negative exponent:
this is eq. (19) in its existential form for the digamma field, `#print axioms` clean.

### Coefficients in `ℂ(s)`: the datum holds over the rational base

The digamma layer of eq. (19) is now proved over `ℂ(s)`, not only over `ℂ`.

`RatCoeffJetPoly.lean` repeats the line criterion with rational coefficients.  `ratGerm_div_polyPow`
identifies the germ of `p(s)/s^d` with the rational function `p/X^d`, so a jet polynomial with such
coefficients is again a value of `digammaJetEval`; `jetPolyMvCoeff_ne_zero` gives its nonvanishing
from one nonzero coefficient, and the shared criterion `exists_not_isLineBigO_of_germ` — factored out
of the constant-coefficient proof — yields `exists_jetPolyCoeff_datum_of_ne_zero` and, after the
`psi`-degree grouping, `exists_full_jetPolyCoeff_datum`.

`RatCoeffClearing.lean` supplies the two conversions.  `exists_common_denom_poly`: a finite family of
rational functions has a common polynomial denominator, so one nonzero polynomial turns all
coefficients of a jet polynomial into polynomials.  `tendsto_div_polyJetShape_num_pow`: the numerator
slot `dP` of `polyJetShape` absorbs a power of `s`, so bounded coefficients `p(s)/s^d` and plain
polynomial coefficients `p(s)` describe the same residue, read against different shapes.

`DigammaRationalFieldDatum.lean` assembles them.  `exists_ratAlgebra_analytic` presents an element of
the jet algebra over `ℂ(s)`, after multiplication by one nonzero polynomial, as an analytic germ whose
values are a jet polynomial with polynomial coefficients; `exists_polyCoeff_jet_datum` gives the datum
of such a germ when it is nonzero; and **`exists_digamma_ratField_datum`**:

> for every `σ > 0` and every nonzero `x` in the differential field generated over `ℂ(s)` by the
> digamma germ, there are jet polynomials `P, Q` with polynomial coefficients and analytic germs
> `U, V` with `x = ⟦U⟧/⟦V⟧`, `U ↦ P`, `V ↦ Q`, and
> `(P/polyJetShape σ a₁ d₁ b₁) / (Q/polyJetShape σ a₂ d₂ b₂) → r ≠ 0` along the line.

Both denominators — the field denominator and the coefficient denominators — are cleared by the same
device, and the same polynomial multiplier cancels between numerator and denominator, so no extra
datum is needed for it.  `#print axioms` clean.

### The Gamma level: Gamma enters the carrier and its jets factor

`UpperEndGammaElement.lean` puts `Γ` itself into the upper-end carrier.  `Γ` is analytic and zero-free
on every upper half-plane, so `gammaUpperAnalytic` is an element of the analytic subring and
`gammaUpperMero` of the meromorphic field, with

> **`upperEndAnalyticDeriv_gammaUpperAnalytic`** / **`deriv_gammaUpperMero`**: `Γ′ = Γ·ψ`,

the logarithmic-derivative identity as a single germ identity.  Iterating it with the Leibniz rule
gives **`exists_gammaJet_factor`**: for every `n`, `δⁿΓ = Γ·y` with `y` in the digamma jet algebra —
one factor of `Γ` per jet, all remaining content polynomial in the polygammas.  The induction uses
`deriv_mem_differentialJetAlgebra` (new, in `DifferentialJetAlgebra.lean`): the jet algebra is closed
under the derivation, because its generators are jets and the base constants are constant.

Consequently **`gammaJetAlgebra_le_ratAdjoin`**: the differential algebra generated over `ℂ(s)` by
`Γ` is contained in the ordinary algebra generated by `Γ` together with the digamma jets.  That is
the structural statement that turns an element of `ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩` into a polynomial in `Γ` whose
coefficients are jet polynomials in the polygammas — the form the `Γ`-power dominance of
`GammaJetDominantExists`/`GammaJetRatioFamily` consumes.

### Eq. (3) for the full field `ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩`

The card's stated remaining target is now a Lean theorem.

`GammaShapeDominantResidue.lean` supplies the analytic half.
`tendsto_div_gammaJetShape_of_polyDatum`: a term `Γ^u·g` whose digamma part has a residue against
`polyJetShape σ a dP b` has the same residue against `gammaJetShape σ u a dP b`.
`exists_dominant_gammaShape_residue` (and its `Function.Injective u` corollary): a finite family of
such terms with pairwise distinct data has a dominant member — on a vertical line the *smallest*
power of `Γ` wins, since `Γ` decays faster than any power of `s` — and the sum inherits its residue.

`GammaFieldRatDatum.lean` assembles the field.  `exists_gammaAlgebra_clearing` turns an element of
the `Γ` jet algebra over `ℂ(s)`, after multiplication by one nonzero polynomial, into an analytic
germ `∑_j Γ^{u_j}·C_j` with distinct `u_j` and each `C_j` a jet polynomial with polynomial
coefficients (via `exists_gamma_expansion` and the per-coefficient clearing).
`exists_gammaAlgebra_datum` gives that germ's residue: coefficients whose germ vanishes are
eventually zero on the line (`eventually_line_of_upperEnd`), the survivors each have a digamma datum,
and the `Γ`-dominance selects one.  Finally **`exists_gammaField_datum`**:

> for every `σ > 0` and every nonzero `x` in the differential field generated over `ℂ(s)` by `Γ`,
> there are nonzero polynomials `q₁, q₂`, analytic germs `U, V` represented by functions `G, H`, and
> exponents with
> `x = (q₂·U)/(q₁·V)` and
> `(G/gammaJetShape σ u₁ a₁ d₁ b₁) / (H/gammaJetShape σ u₂ a₂ d₂ b₂) → r ≠ 0` along the line.

That is eq. (3)/(19) in existential form for `ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩`: no presentation supplied, no datum assumed —
nonvanishing alone produces the residue.  `#print axioms` clean.

### The law itself, with the coefficient data discharged

`GammaShapeDominantResidue.lean` gains `gammaJetShape_denom_add` and
`tendsto_div_poly_gammaJetShape`: dividing a residue by a polynomial moves the polynomial's degree
into the shape's denominator slot and divides the limit by the leading coefficient.  With it,
`exists_gammaAlgebra_line_datum` gives the element *itself* — not only its cleared multiple — a
single-shape residue, which is exactly the form of the law's coefficient hypothesis.

`GammaFieldCoefficientLaw.lean` closes the loop.  `eventually_polyLine_ne_zero` and
**`eventually_line_eq_of_representatives`** show that any two cleared meromorphic representatives of
the same field element agree far up the line (the two clearings cross-multiply to an identity of
analytic germs, and `IsFractionRing.injective` moves it back to the analytic subring).  So the
residue attaches to whichever representative appears in the relation, giving

> **`finite_phase_law_of_gammaField_coefficients`**: the finite phase law for a relation whose
> coefficients are nonzero elements of the `Γ` jet algebra over `ℂ(s)`, represented by the functions
> in the relation — with no residue hypothesis at all.

Every coefficient hypothesis of the law is now produced from nonvanishing alone.  `#print axioms`
clean.

### The relation hypothesis weakened to what a germ relation gives

The collapse argument samples the relation along a Bohr return sequence, which tends to `+∞`, so the
relation is only ever *used* far up the vertical line.  `EventualRelationFinitePhaseLaw.lean` records
that: `recurrence_collapse_to_autonomous_of_eventual`,
`recurrence_collapse_of_bohr_return_eventual`, `line_relation_of_coeff_limits_eventual`,
`strip_relation_of_coeff_limits_eventual`, `rank_finite_of_coeff_limits_eventual`,
`finite_phase_law_of_coeff_ratios_eventual` and `finite_phase_law_of_gammaJet_data_eventual` are the
whole chain with `∀ s : ℂ` replaced by `∀ᶠ t in atTop` on the line.  Nothing is lost, and the
conclusion is unchanged: the collapsed constant-coefficient relation still holds on the *whole* strip
(the line-to-strip step is `jetPoly_eqOn_zero_of_line`, which needs the collapsed relation only on the
line).

This matters because a relation between elements of the differential field is a relation between
germs at the upper end: it determines the representing functions only there.  Combining with the
coefficient work above gives the final form

> **`finite_phase_law_of_gammaField_coefficients_eventual`**: for a relation that holds far up the
> line, whose coefficients are nonzero elements of the `Γ` jet algebra over `ℂ(s)` represented by the
> functions appearing in it, the collapsed constant-coefficient relation holds on the whole strip, is
> nontrivial, and the Bohr frequency rank is finite.

No residue hypothesis, no presentation, no relation off the line.  `#print axioms` clean.

### The law from a relation stated in the carrier

The last link is the one that makes the law's input a statement *about the field* rather than about
functions.  `eventual_line_relation_of_analytic_relation` applies the germ representation to an
identity between analytic elements of the upper-end carrier — the coefficients `W α` and the jets
`upperEndAnalyticDeriv^[i] Ψ` of the solution's germ — and returns the pointwise identity far up
every vertical line.  Combined with everything above:

> **`finite_phase_law_of_gammaField_carrier_relation`**: given a relation
> `∑_α W_α ∏_i (δ^i Ψ)^{e_{αi}} = 0` in the upper-end analytic carrier, whose coefficients are
> nonzero elements of the `Γ` jet algebra over `ℂ(s)` and whose solution germ `Ψ` represents a
> strip-almost-periodic holomorphic `Φ`, the collapsed constant-coefficient relation holds on the
> whole strip, is nontrivial, and the Bohr frequency rank of `Φ` on the line is finite.

No residue hypothesis, no datum, no presentation, no relation off the line: the only inputs are the
relation itself, the nonvanishing of its coefficients, and the almost periodicity of `Φ`.
`#print axioms` clean.

### The law for a relation with arbitrary field coefficients

**`finite_phase_law_of_gammaField_relation`** removes the last restriction.  The coefficients are
arbitrary *nonzero elements of the field* `ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩` — denominators allowed — and the relation is
stated in the field itself, between the jets `δⁱ Ψ` of the solution's germ:

> if `∑_α x_α ∏_i (δⁱΨ)^{e_{αi}} = 0` with `x_α ∈ ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩ \ {0}`, `Ψ` the germ of a
> strip-almost-periodic holomorphic `Φ`, and the jet exponents distinct, then the collapsed
> constant-coefficient relation holds on the whole strip, is nontrivial, and `Φ`'s Bohr frequency
> rank is finite.

The proof clears twice — `exists_common_denominator` for the field denominator, then the per
coefficient clearing polynomials `q_α`, whose product multiplies the relation — lands in the analytic
carrier by `IsFractionRing.injective`, and applies the carrier form.  Since the multiplier is a
single nonzero element, no coefficient is lost and none is created.

This is eq. (3), eq. (19) and eq. (5) of the card in one statement, with every hypothesis discharged
except those about `Φ` itself.  `#print axioms` clean.

`HTGeometry/GammaFieldLawInstanceFree.lean` removes the last two *instance* hypotheses as well.  The
rank half of the law is proved through Peter–Weyl on the hull, whose Haar-measure argument carries
`[MeasurableSpace (BohrHull f)]` and `[BorelSpace (BohrHull f)]` along as instance arguments — never
an assumption about the problem, since every topological space carries its Borel σ-algebra.  Choosing
it inside the proof (`letI := borel _`, `haveI : BorelSpace _ := ⟨rfl⟩`) yields

> **`finite_phase_law_of_gammaField_relation'`** and
> **`finite_phase_law_of_gammaField_carrier_relation'`**,

whose hypotheses are about `Φ`, the coefficients and the relation, and nothing else.  Both
`#print axioms` clean.

### The one remaining generality gap, and the route to it

*(Historical: this section describes the gap as it stood before the existential was proved; it is
recorded because the obstruction it identifies is what the new modules had to overcome.)*

With §8 closed, every numbered claim of §1–§8 had Lean except one *quantifier*: eq. (3) and eq. (19)
were proved for field elements **presented with their residue data** (a datum `(u,a,dP,b)` and a
nonzero limit `c`), and — since `GammaJetDatumUniqueness.lean` — that presentation is unique when it
exists.  What is not yet Lean is the existential half in the card's own phrasing: *every* nonzero
`A ∈ L` has such a datum, with no presentation supplied.

The obstruction is real and is exactly the one §2–§3 address on paper.  The corpus knows the
*leading* asymptotic of every jet (`tendsto_pow_mul_iteratedDeriv_digamma_vertical`) and knows that
no nonzero jet polynomial over `ℂ(s)` is flat (`not_isVertFlatGerm_digammaJetEval`).  But two
distinct monomials can share a datum — `ψ′ψ‴` and `(ψ″)²` both have `s`-degree `6` and log-degree
`0`, with leading constants `2` and `1` — so a combination such as `ψ′ψ‴ − 2(ψ″)²` cancels at
leading order, and its true datum lies deeper.  Deciding the datum of an *arbitrary* element
therefore needs the asymptotic expansion to arbitrary depth, which is what the paper gets from the
formal Stirling field.

Route (nothing here is blocked, only unbuilt; mathlib has no Euler–Maclaurin and no asymptotic
expansion of the polygamma functions):

1. **Asymptotic scale on a vertical line.**  A predicate for `‖f(t)‖ = O(t^{-k})` on the line, closed
   under sums, products and rational coefficients; the leading-coefficient extraction map.
2. **One order at a time, algebraically.**  For `T_k(s) = ∑_{n≥0}(s+n)^{-k}` (which is the corpus's
   `hasSum_polygamma_series` up to a factorial) the identity
   `(k−1)^{-1}[(s+n)^{-(k-1)} − (s+n+1)^{-(k-1)}] = (s+n)^{-k} + O((s+n)^{-k-1})`
   telescopes: `T_k(s) = ((k−1)s^{k−1})^{-1} + (a sum of the same type one order higher)`.  This is
   Euler–Maclaurin done by rational algebra, with no integrals.
3. **All orders.**  Iterating (2) with the coefficients carried as data gives, for every `K`, an
   expansion `ψ^{(m)}(s) = ∑_{j<K} c_{m,j} s^{-m-j} + O(s^{-m-K})`; differentiation transfers
   expansions between levels by Cauchy's estimate on discs of radius `|s|/2`.
4. **`ψ` itself** carries the `log s` term: integrate the expansion of `ψ′`.
5. **Products and sums.**  The scale is a ring, so every jet polynomial over `ℂ(s)` has an expansion
   to every depth; if *all* coefficients vanished the function would be flat, which
   `not_isVertFlatGerm_digammaJetEval` forbids — so a first nonzero coefficient exists, and that is
   the datum.
6. **Eq. (19) and eq. (3) unquantified**, then the field version by the existing quotient lemmas.

Step 5 is the point of the design: the corpus's flat-elimination theorem replaces the
Adamczewski–Dreyfus–Hardouin input as the injectivity of the expansion map, so no formal shift field
is needed even in the general case.

**Step 1 is built.**  `HTGeometry/LineAsymptoticScale.lean` defines the scale and proves it is a
graded ring: `IsLineBigO σ k f` (`‖f(t)‖·‖σ+it‖^k` bounded) is closed under `add`, `sub`, `mul`
(orders add), `const_mul`, `pow`, and `mono` (a smaller order absorbs a larger one); a positive order
forces `f → 0`.  The two bridges to the corpus's `Tendsto` idiom are
**`tendsto_pow_mul_of_isLineBigO`** — if `f − c·s^{-k}` is one order smaller then `s^k f(s) → c`,
so an expansion coefficient *is* a residue — and its converse `isLineBigO_of_tendsto`.
`isLineBigO_coeff_unique` records that the coefficient is determined.

**Step 1 applied.**  `HTGeometry/PolygammaLineOrders.lean` reads the corpus's leading asymptotics
through the scale: `isLineBigO_iteratedDeriv_succ_digamma` (`ψ^{(m+1)} = O(s^{-(m+1)})`),
`isLineBigO_jet_pow`, and **`isLineBigO_jet_monomial`** — an arbitrary finite monomial
`∏ (ψ^{(i+1)})^{e_i}` decays at its total weight `∑ (i+1)e_i`, which is the grading the datum order
uses.  `#print axioms` clean.

**Step 2 is built, in the form that avoids Euler–Maclaurin entirely.**  Two modules:

- `HTGeometry/ShiftOrbitSumBounds.lean` — the analytic half.  Every orbit point `s+n` has modulus at
  least the ordinate `t` *and* at least its own abscissa `σ+n` (both already in the corpus).
  Splitting the orbit at `n ≈ t` — the near part contributes `≤ (t+1)/t³`, the far part telescopes
  through the corpus's `hasSum_one_div_shift_mul_succ` — gives
  **`tsum_norm_shift_inv_cube_le`** (`∑_n ‖s+n‖^{-3} ≤ 4/t²`) and
  **`tsum_norm_shift_inv_pow_le`** (`∑_n ‖s+n‖^{-(q+3)} ≤ 4/t^{q+2}`).  Each is *one full order
  better* than the trivial term bound, and that gain is what an expansion step consumes.
- `HTGeometry/RisingFactorialTelescope.lean` — the algebraic half.  With
  `risingProd s m = s(s+1)⋯(s+m−1)`, the identity
  **`inv_risingProd_sub`** (`(risingProd s m)^{-1} − (risingProd (s+1) m)^{-1} = m·(risingProd s (m+1))^{-1}`)
  telescopes to the closed form
  **`hasSum_inv_risingProd`**: `∑_{n≥0} 1/((s+n)⋯(s+n+m)) = 1/((m+1)·s(s+1)⋯(s+m))` exactly, for
  every `s` in the right half plane.  These reciprocal rising factorials are the exactly summable
  basis: `(s+n)^{-k}` differs from the rising factorial of the same length by one order, so the
  orbit bound converts the difference into a gained power of `t`.

`#print axioms` clean for both.

**The two halves now meet.**  `HTGeometry/PolygammaFactorialComparison.lean` runs one expansion step
end to end:

- `norm_le_norm_add_nat`, `norm_pow_le_norm_risingProd` — in the right half plane a shift to the
  right never decreases the modulus, so `‖s(s+1)⋯(s+m−1)‖ ≥ ‖s‖^m`;
- **`exists_norm_risingProd_sub_pow_le`** — the leading terms of `x(x+1)⋯(x+m)` and `x^{m+1}` cancel:
  the difference is `O(‖x‖^m)`, by induction on `m` with an explicit constant;
- **`exists_norm_inv_pow_sub_inv_risingProd_le`** — hence the reciprocals differ by *one order*:
  `‖x^{-(m+2)} − (x⋯(x+m+1))^{-1}‖ ≤ C‖x‖^{-(m+3)}`;
- **`exists_norm_polygammaSeries_sub_factorial_le`** — summing over the orbit and applying the orbit
  bound at exponent `m+3`, the polygamma series and the (exactly summable) factorial series differ
  by `C/t^{m+2}`;
- **`exists_norm_iteratedDeriv_digamma_sub_rising_le`** — in the corpus's own normalization,

  `‖ψ^{(m+1)}(σ+it) − (−1)^m (m+1)!/((m+1)·s(s+1)⋯(s+m))‖ ≤ C/t^{m+2}`,

  a *quantitative* leading asymptotic: the jet equals an explicit rational function up to an error
  one full order smaller than the jet itself.  The corpus previously had only the qualitative limit.

`#print axioms` clean.

**The depth induction now has its two engines.**  Iterating the step needs the remainder to be
carried *algebraically*, since only its degree controls the next order.  Two modules do that:

- `HTGeometry/RationalOrderBound.lean` — `exists_norm_polynomial_le` (a polynomial is bounded by its
  degree power outside the unit disc) and **`exists_norm_div_risingProd_le`**: a numerator of degree
  `d` over `x^a·x(x+1)⋯(x+b−1)` decays at order `a+b−d`.  This converts an algebraic degree count
  into an analytic decay order with no further estimates — the workhorse of the induction.
- `HTGeometry/RisingFactorialPolynomial.lean` — the rising factorial as a monic polynomial
  (`risingPoly`, `risingPoly_monic`, `risingPoly_natDegree`) and the crux,
  **`expansion_step`**: for `P` of degree `≤ d` and `a + b = d + p`,

  `P(x)/(x^a·x(x+1)⋯(x+b−1)) − P_d·(x(x+1)⋯(x+p−1))^{-1} = Q(x)/(x^a·x(x+1)⋯(x+b−1)·x(x+1)⋯(x+p−1))`

  with an *explicit* `Q` of degree `< a+b`, i.e. one order better.  The degree drop is proved by
  splitting `Q` into `(P − P_d X^d)·risingPoly p`, whose degree is below `d+p` because the `d`-th
  coefficient cancels, and `P_d·(X^d·risingPoly p − X^a·risingPoly b)`, a difference of two *monic*
  polynomials of the same degree `a+b`.

So each expansion step is now a closed loop: subtract the leading reciprocal rising factorial (whose
orbit sum is exact by `hasSum_inv_risingProd`), and the remainder is again a rational function of the
same shape with one more order — which `exists_norm_div_risingProd_le` converts into the next
estimate.  `#print axioms` clean.

**Step 3 is complete: the jets now expand to arbitrary depth.**  Two more modules run the loop.

`HTGeometry/StandardRationalExpansion.lean` gives the loop a shape it is closed under.  A *standard*
rational function is `P(x)/∏_j(x+j)` — denominator a product of factors shifted by naturals
(`shiftProd`, `shiftPoly`, monic of the multiset's cardinality) — with degree defect at least `p`;
subtracting a reciprocal rising factorial appends `p` more factors, so the shape is preserved.

- `IsStdRat.exists_norm_le` — the defect *is* the decay order;
- `shift_expansion_step`, **`IsStdRat.step`** — one step: split off the leading reciprocal rising
  factorial, the remainder is standard with defect `p+1`;
- **`IsStdRat.expansion`** — iterating: for every depth `K`,
  `f(x) = ∑_{i<K} c_i·(x(x+1)⋯(x+p+i−1))^{-1} + g(x)` with `g` standard of defect `p+K`;
- `isStdRat_inv_pow` — a reciprocal power `x^{-k}` is standard of defect `k`, which is the input.

`HTGeometry/PolygammaDepthExpansion.lean` sums that over the shift orbit.  Each reciprocal rising
factorial sums in closed form (`hasSum_inv_risingProd`), the remainder is standard of defect
`m+K+3` and the orbit bound converts it into `4C/t^{m+K+2}`, and the corpus's polygamma series turns
the total into the jet.  The result is **`exists_jet_expansion`**: for every `m` and every depth `K`,

`‖ψ^{(m+1)}(σ+it) − ∑_{i≤K} b_i·(s(s+1)⋯(s+m+i))^{-1}‖ ≤ C/t^{m+2+K}`

for all `σ > 0` and `t ≥ 2`, with explicit coefficients `b_i`.  The error is smaller than every term
kept, so the expansion is genuinely asymptotic — and it is available to *arbitrary* depth, which is
exactly what deciding the datum of a jet polynomial with leading-order cancellation needs.  mathlib
has neither Euler–Maclaurin nor any asymptotic expansion of the polygamma functions; this is built
from the corpus's own series and the elementary orbit and degree estimates.  `#print axioms` clean.

**The algebra of the assembly is in place too.**  `StandardRationalExpansion.lean` now closes the
class under the operations a jet polynomial performs: `IsStdRat.mul` (defects add), `IsStdRat.add`
(common denominator), `IsStdRat.const_mul`, `IsStdRat.pow`, `IsStdRat.mono`.  So *any* polynomial in
the jets, with the jets replaced by their depth-`K` rising-factorial approximations, is again a
single standard rational function whose defect is the monomial weight.

`HTGeometry/WeightedProductBound.lean` supplies the matching error estimate, which a uniform product
bound would lose: with `u = 1/t`, if each factor has size `A·u^{w_i}` and is approximated to depth
`K` with error `A·u^{w_i+K}`, then **`norm_prod_sub_prod_mul_pow_le`** gives

`‖∏ a_i − ∏ b_i‖ ≤ (|s|·A^{|s|})·u^{(∑ w_i) + K}`,

i.e. the product is approximated to the *total weight plus the depth* — each factor's error is
multiplied by the others, which are themselves small.  `norm_prod_le_mul_pow` is the companion size
bound.  `#print axioms` clean.

**The per-factor packages are built.**  `IsStdRat.sum`, `IsStdRat.prod`, `isStdRat_zero` and
`isStdRat_inv_risingProd` complete the closure list, and `PolygammaDepthExpansion.lean` now packages
each factor in exactly the shape the weighted product estimate consumes:

- **`exists_jet_stdRat_approx`** — the depth-`K` approximation of `ψ^{(m+1)}` *is* a standard
  rational function of defect `m+1` (a finite combination of reciprocal rising factorials);
- **`exists_jet_package`** — the jet, its approximation and the error, all measured against powers of
  `u = 1/t`: sizes `A·u^{m+1}`, error `A·u^{m+1+K}`;
- **`exists_jet_pow_package`** — the same for a *power* `(ψ^{(m+1)})^e`, obtained by feeding the
  constant family into `norm_prod_sub_prod_mul_pow_le`: size and error at weight `(m+1)·e`, with the
  approximation still standard (`IsStdRat.pow`).

**The assembly is now built, and so is the extraction.**

- **`exists_monomial_package`** — multiplying the power packages over the finitely many jets (outer
  weighted product, uniform constant `B = 1 + ∑ A_i`), a jet *monomial* is approximated to its own
  weight plus the depth by a standard rational function of that weight;
- **`exists_jetPoly_package`** — summing monomials of weight at least `W` (each approximant
  `mono`'d down to defect `W`, each error absorbed since `u ≤ 1`), a *jet polynomial* with constant
  coefficients is approximated to depth `K` by a single standard rational function of defect `W`;
- `HTGeometry/StdRatDatum.lean` — the extraction.  `tendsto_inv_vertical`,
  `tendsto_polynomial_mul_inv_pow` (a polynomial against its degree power tends to its leading
  coefficient) and `tendsto_shiftProd_mul_inv_pow` (a shift product against its length power tends to
  `1`) combine into **`tendsto_stdRat_datum`**: for `card l = deg P + p`,

  `s^p · P(s)/∏_j(s+j) → leadingCoeff P` on every vertical line with `σ > 0`.

  So a nonzero standard rational function *has* a datum, read off its numerator.

`#print axioms` clean.

**The dichotomy, and eq. (19) for constant coefficients.**  `HTGeometry/JetDatumFromNonFlat.lean`
proves the step that turns approximation into a residue.  `isLineBigO_of_bound` converts a bound in
powers of `1/t` into the line scale (through the corpus's `eventually_norm_vertical_le`), and then
**`exists_datum_of_not_flat`**: if `f` is approximated to *every* depth by standard rational
functions of a fixed defect `W`, and `f` is not flat (`¬ IsLineBigO σ n f` for some `n`), then

`∃ p c, c ≠ 0 ∧ s^p·f(s) → c`.

The proof runs the dichotomy at the single depth `K = n`.  If that approximant's numerator is zero
then `f` is `O(u^{W+n})`, hence `IsLineBigO σ n f`, contradiction.  Otherwise the numerator is
nonzero, its own datum is `tendsto_stdRat_datum`, and either the datum order `p` is below `W+n` — in
which case the error is of strictly higher order and passes to `f` — or `p ≥ W+n`, which again makes
`f` small enough to contradict non-flatness.

`HTGeometry/JetPolynomialDatum.lean` then reads this on the object of interest:
**`exists_jetPoly_datum`** — a polynomial in the positive-order digamma jets with *constant*
coefficients, if it is not flat on the line, satisfies `s^p·P(ψ′,ψ″,…)(s) → c ≠ 0`.  That is eq. (19)
for the constant-coefficient case with **no presentation supplied**: only the polynomial and its
non-flatness go in, and the corpus's `not_isVertFlatGerm_digammaJetEval` is exactly what supplies
non-flatness for a nonzero polynomial — which exists because a standard rational function whose
every coefficient vanished to every depth would make the jet polynomial flat, contradicting
`not_isVertFlatGerm_digammaJetEval`.  After that, the `log s` level for `ψ` itself (integrating the
`ψ′` expansion) and the `ℂ(s)`-coefficient case.

The coefficient case has a clean route that needs no new analysis, only the closure already proved.
Clearing denominators reduces `ℂ(s)` coefficients to *polynomial* ones, and for a polynomial
coefficient `a ∈ ℂ[X]` of degree at most `d`, the function `a(s)/s^d` is itself standard of defect
`0` (numerator `a`, denominator `Multiset.replicate d 0`), hence bounded.  So

`f(s)/s^d = ∑_α (a_α(s)/s^d)·(monomial_α)`

is approximated to every depth by standard rational functions of the minimal weight — the main parts
multiply by `IsStdRat.mul`, and the errors are multiplied by a *bounded* factor — and
`exists_datum_of_not_flat` applies to it verbatim.  Transporting back multiplies the datum exponent
by `s^{-d}`, i.e. gives the integer exponent `p − d` that a growing coefficient requires.

That route is now built.  `isStdRat_poly_div_pow` gives the bounded normalized coefficient, and
`HTGeometry/JetPolyCoeffDatum.lean` carries it through:

- **`exists_jetPolyCoeff_package`** — the monomial packages multiplied by the bounded coefficients:
  the normalized jet polynomial `∑_α (a_α(s)/s^d)·∏_i(ψ^{(i+1)})^{e_{αi}}` is approximated to every
  depth `K` by a standard rational function of the minimal weight `W`, with error `A·u^{W+K}` (main
  parts by `IsStdRat.mul` and `IsStdRat.sum`, errors multiplied by the bounded factor);
- **`exists_jetPolyCoeff_datum`** — hence, if that normalized polynomial is not flat, it has a datum
  `s^p·f(s) → c ≠ 0`.

Since clearing denominators turns `ℂ(s)` coefficients into polynomial ones and the normalization by
`s^d` only shifts the datum exponent by `−d`, this is eq. (19) for the whole coefficient field, with
no presentation supplied.

The whole `HTGeometry` umbrella rebuilds with all of this wired in (12754 jobs, no warnings).

**The logarithmic level, first case.**  `HTGeometry/DigammaLogPowers.lean` proves
**`tendsto_digamma_pow_div_vertLog_pow`**: `ψ(σ+it)^e/(log t + iπ/2)^e → 1`, the datum of a pure
power of `ψ` — the case in which no cancellation between monomials is possible.  It rests on the
corpus's `digamma_vertical_sub_log_I_tendsto_zero`, which is a *difference* statement
(`ψ(s) − vertLog t → 0`), not merely a ratio.

**`tendsto_datum_mul_digamma_pow`** carries this into the datum calculus: if `g` has the datum
`s^p·g(s) → c`, then `ψ^e·g` has the datum `s^p·(ψ^e·g)(s)/vertLog^e → c` — multiplying by a power of
`ψ` multiplies the datum by the corresponding power of the vertical logarithm, which is exactly the
`b`-component of the card's `(a, b, c)`.  Composed with `exists_jetPolyCoeff_datum`, this gives the
datum of any monomial `a(s)·ψ^{e₀}·∏(ψ^{(i+1)})^{e_i}` in the full `(a, b, c)` form.

**The loop is closed against the law's own interface.**  `HTGeometry/DatumToJetShape.lean` proves
**`tendsto_div_polyJetShape_of_datum`**: a datum in the form the expansion machinery produces —
`s^p·F(s)/(log t + iπ/2)^a → c` — *is* a residue against the corpus's jet shape,
`F/polyJetShape σ a 0 p → c`, since that shape is exactly `L^a/s^p`.  So the unquantified datum of a
jet polynomial is an admissible coefficient datum for `finite_phase_law_of_gammaJet_data`: the new
machinery feeds the law directly, with no presentation chosen by hand.

**The general logarithmic level needs no expansion of `ψ − log`.**  Group a jet polynomial by its
`ψ`-degree: `F = ∑_{e₀} ψ^{e₀}·P_{e₀}(ψ′,ψ″,…)` with `P_{e₀}` having `ℂ(s)` coefficients.  Each group
has a datum `(p_{e₀}, c_{e₀})` from `exists_jetPolyCoeff_datum`, and `ψ^{e₀} = L^{e₀}(1+o(1))` by
`tendsto_digamma_pow_div_vertLog_pow`, so the group behaves like `c_{e₀}·L^{e₀}·s^{-p_{e₀}}`.

Cancellation between *different* groups is impossible: at a fixed `s`-order the groups carry
*distinct* powers of `L`, and `L^{e}/L^{e'} → ∞` for `e > e'`, so the lexicographic pair
`(p, −e₀)` selects a unique dominant group whose coefficient is already known to be nonzero.  The
ε-mixing that seemed to require an expansion of `ψ − log` only moves weight *downward* in `L`, where
the ordering has already decided the winner.

**The assembly step is built.**  `HTGeometry/DominantSumDatum.lean`:
**`tendsto_sum_div_shape`** — a finite sum of functions, each with a residue `c_α` against its own
shape `S_α`, has the residue `∑ c_α r_α` against any chosen `S_β`, where `r_α` are the shape ratios
(dividing termwise, `F_α/S_β = (F_α/S_α)·(S_α/S_β)`); and **`tendsto_sum_div_dominant`** — when `β`
is dominant, i.e. every other shape ratio tends to `0`, the residue of the sum is exactly `c_β`.
Together with `tendsto_vertLog_pow_ratio` (distinct `ψ`-degrees are separated) and the corpus's
`polyJetShape` ratio lemmas (distinct `s`-orders are separated), this is the whole assembly; the
criterion itself is packaged as **`tendsto_polyJetShape_ratio_zero_of_pair`** — a shape `L^{a₁}/s^{b₁}`
is subordinate to `L^{a₂}/s^{b₂}` exactly when `b₂ < b₁`, or `b₂ = b₁` and `a₁ < a₂`.

The bookkeeping is done too: **`exists_dominant_pair_index`** takes the lexicographic minimum of the
pairs `(b, M − a)` over the family (`Finset.exists_min_image` on `ℕ ×ₗ ℕ`) and returns an index to
which every other member is subordinate, using injectivity of the log-degrees to break the second
component.  Feeding that into the two previous lemmas gives

**`exists_dominant_shape_residue`** — *a finite sum of functions, each with a residue against its own
shape `L^a/s^b` and with the log-degrees pairwise distinct, has the residue of its dominant member.*

Since grouping a jet polynomial by `ψ`-degree produces exactly such a family — distinct log-degrees
by construction, each group's residue supplied by `exists_jetPolyCoeff_datum` through
`tendsto_div_polyJetShape_of_datum` — this completes the general logarithmic level: no cancellation
can occur between groups, and the datum of the whole polynomial is the dominant group's.

`HTGeometry/FullJetPolyDatum.lean` states that assembly as one theorem,
**`exists_grouped_jetPoly_datum`**: given groups `G_α` with data `s^{p_α}·G_α → c_α` and pairwise
distinct `ψ`-degrees `e_α`, the grouped polynomial `∑_α ψ^{e_α}·G_α` has the residue `c_β` against
the shape `L^{e_β}/s^{p_β}` of its dominant group.  That is eq. (19) for the digamma field in the
card's own `(a, b, c)` form, assembled from data that the earlier modules produce for each group.

**The Gamma level closes the same way.**  `tendsto_div_gammaJetShape_of_datum` — multiplying a
residue by `Γ^u` multiplies the shape by `Γ^u`, so the residue against `gammaJetShape σ u a 0 p` is
unchanged.  Combined with the corpus's `tendsto_gammaField_div_gammaJetShape` (sums over `Γ`-powers)
and `tendsto_gammaFieldQuotient` (quotients), every element of `ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩` now obtains its eq. (3)
datum from data the new machinery produces unconditionally — the `(k, a, b, c)` of §1, with
uniqueness already supplied by `gammaJet_datum_residue_unique`.

So the remaining step is not analysis but the dominance plumbing the corpus already owns: each group
is a residue against `polyJetShape σ e₀ 0 p_{e₀}` (by `tendsto_div_polyJetShape_of_datum`), and
`exists_dominant_with_shape_ratios` together with `tendsto_family_ratio_of_normalized` turns a finite
family of such residues into a dominant index with convergent ratios — which is exactly the input
`finite_phase_law_of_coeff_ratios` consumes.

### The law now runs on data

`HTGeometry/FinitePhaseLawFromDatum.lean` closes the circuit: **`finite_phase_law_of_coefficient_datum`**
takes the coefficients in the form the residue machinery produces —
`s^{b_α}·F_α(s)/(log t + iπ/2)^{a_α} → c_α` with `c_α ≠ 0` — and returns the law's three conclusions
(the collapsed relation on the strip, the nonzero constant-coefficient polynomial, and the finite
rational Bohr rank).  The conversion is `tendsto_div_polyJetShape_of_datum` followed by
`gammaJetShape_gamma_zero`; nothing else is supplied by hand.

So the chain runs end to end: a relation with `ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩` coefficients, each coefficient's datum
obtained unconditionally from the expansion machinery, feeds the finite-phase law directly.

The hypothesis class of the datum form is inhabited: `datum_of_const` (a constant coefficient has the
trivial datum `(0,0)`) and `datum_of_pow_datum` (a coefficient with a pure `s`-power datum has log
degree zero), so the entry point is not vacuous at any level of the family.

And **`exists_datum_hypothesis_of_jetPolyCoeff`** supplies the hypothesis from the object itself: a
jet polynomial with `ℂ(s)` coefficients, *given only that it is not flat*, produces a `(b, c)` with
`c ≠ 0` in exactly the form `finite_phase_law_of_coefficient_datum` consumes.  Nothing about the
coefficient is chosen by hand — non-flatness is the only input, and for a nonzero polynomial the
corpus's `not_isVertFlatGerm_digammaJetEval` provides it.

**The transport, instantiated.**  `upperEndAnalyticDeriv_iterate_repr` (a germ's `n`-th jet is
represented by `iteratedDeriv n` of any representative) and **`digammaJetMero_eq_algebraMap`** (the
carrier's digamma jets are the images of the iterated analytic derivatives) put the abstract jets of
`differentialAdjoin` presentations on the analytic footing the residue chain uses.  With
`exists_fin_jetPoly_quotient` on the algebraic side, an element of `L` or `K_Γ` is a quotient of
`Fin`-indexed polynomials in `iteratedDeriv n digammaFun`.

### Map of the unquantified-residue chain

For navigation, the modules built for the general form of eq. (19)/(3), in dependency order:

| module | content |
|---|---|
| `LineAsymptoticScale` | `IsLineBigO`, graded ring, extraction `s^k f → c`, uniqueness |
| `PolygammaLineOrders` | jets and jet monomials in that scale |
| `ShiftOrbitSumBounds` | `∑_n ‖s+n‖^{-(q+3)} ≤ 4/t^{q+2}` — one gained order |
| `RisingFactorialTelescope` | exact factorial series `∑ 1/((s+n)⋯(s+n+m))` |
| `PolygammaFactorialComparison` | one expansion step, quantitative leading asymptotic |
| `RationalOrderBound` | degree defect ⟹ decay order |
| `RisingFactorialPolynomial` | `risingPoly`, the degree-dropping `expansion_step` |
| `StandardRationalExpansion` | `IsStdRat`, closure (`mul/add/pow/sum/prod/mono`), depth expansion |
| `PolygammaDepthExpansion` | `exists_jet_expansion` (arbitrary depth) and the jet/monomial/polynomial packages |
| `WeightedProductBound` | product error at total weight plus depth |
| `StdRatDatum` | `s^p·P/∏(s+j) → leadingCoeff P` |
| `JetDatumFromNonFlat` | the dichotomy: non-flat ⟹ datum |
| `JetPolynomialDatum`, `JetPolyCoeffDatum` | constant and `ℂ(s)` coefficients |
| `DigammaLogPowers` | `ψ^e ∼ L^e`, log-power separation, datum times `ψ^e` |
| `DatumToJetShape` | datum ⟹ residue against `polyJetShape` |
| `DominantSumDatum` | dominant member of a family, separation criterion, dominant index |
| `FullJetPolyDatum` | the grouped assembly, and the `Γ^u` level |

Every one is `#print axioms` clean and reachable from the `HTGeometry` umbrella.

### Where the next session starts

The analytic content of eq. (3)/(19) is now formalized without presentation hypotheses, and the law
runs on the resulting data.  What is *not* formalized is the field-theoretic packaging: `K_Γ` is not
defined in the corpus as a differential subfield object, so "every `F ∈ K_Γ`" is expressed through
the presentations of §4 (`(21)` `K_Γ = L(Γ)` and `(23)` `F = P(Γ)/Q(Γ)`) rather than quantified over
an abstract carrier.  Building that packaging is the remaining item on this branch, and it is differential-algebra plumbing
rather than analysis — and it should *reuse*, not rebuild: the corpus already owns a
`differentialAdjoin` carrier over meromorphic germs
(`HTGeometry/MeromorphicRationalCoordinateDifferentialAdjoin.lean`,
`differentialAdjoin_meromorphicRationalCoordinate_eq`, and the elliptic-gamma modules'
`differentialAdjoin` covariance theorems).  The carrier is `differentialAdjoin F s = IntermediateField.adjoin F (differentialJetSet s)`
(`HTGeometry/DifferentialIndependence.lean`), so `L` and `K_Γ` are `differentialAdjoin` of the
digamma and Gamma germs over the rational-coordinate field, and the presentation lemma is a statement
about `IntermediateField.adjoin`: every element of the adjoin of a jet set lies in the adjoin of a
*finite* subset, and over a finite generating set the adjoin is the set of quotients of polynomial
expressions in the generators.  Both halves are mathlib API rather than new mathematics, and the first is now built:
`HTGeometry/DifferentialAdjoinPresentation.lean` proves **`mem_differentialAdjoin_iff`** — an element
of `differentialAdjoin F s` is exactly a quotient of two polynomial expressions in the jets of the
generators (`IntermediateField.mem_adjoin_iff` applied to `differentialJetSet`), with
`exists_jetPoly_presentation` as the usable form.  That is §4's `(23)` in formal dress.

**`exists_fin_jetPoly_quotient`** is the capstone: every element of a differential adjoin is a
quotient of two `Fin`-indexed jet polynomials whose variables are jets of the generators — §4's
`(23)` in exactly the form the residue theorems accept.  Underneath,
**`exists_fin_monomial_sum`** completes the bookkeeping: numerator and denominator of a presentation
are `∑_{α : Fin M} a_α ∏_{i : Fin N} h_i^{e_{α i}}` — literally the shape the residue theorems
consume.  Its ingredient **`aeval_eq_sum_monomials_vars`** writes a presentation as a sum of
monomials over the *single*
index set `r.vars` (padding each monomial by `prod_pow_eq_prod_pow_of_subset`, whose hypothesis is
`monomial_support_subset_vars`) — the exponent-family shape the residue machinery requires.
Underneath, `aeval_eq_sum_monomials` puts a presentation in the monomial-sum shape
`∑_d coeff_d · ∏_i x_i^{d i}`, and `exists_fin_sum_eq` / `exists_fin_prod_eq` reindex any finite sum
or product over `Fin N` — the bookkeeping steps between a presentation and the `Fin N` families the
residue theorems take.

**`exists_finite_jetPoly_presentation`** states the two halves as one: every element of a
differential adjoin is a quotient of two polynomials in *finitely many* jets of the generators —
eq. `(23)` of the card in formal form.  Its ingredient
**`exists_finset_jets_of_mem_differentialAdjoin`** is the finiteness half: every element already
lies in the field generated by a *finite* set of jets, so a presentation is a jet polynomial in
finitely many variables — the `Fin N` form the residue machinery accepts.

`differentialJetSet_singleton` and `differentialJetSet_pair` give the two shapes the card needs —
`L = ℂ(s)⟨ψ⟩` presents over the jets of one generator, `K_Γ = ℂ(s)⟨ψ, Γ⟩` over the union of two jet
ranges — and `mem_differentialAdjoin_singleton_iff` states the presentation in that form.

What is left is the transport, and its primitive is already in the corpus: the carrier is
`upperEndMeroField` with `upperEndMeroFieldDifferential`
(`HTGeometry/UpperEndDifferentialField.lean`), and **`upperEndGermDeriv_coe`** states that the germ
derivative of a function's germ is the germ of its derivative.  Iterating that is now proved:
`HTGeometry/UpperEndJetTransport.lean` gives **`upperEndGermDeriv_iterate_coe`** —
`(upperEndGermDeriv)^[n] ⟦f⟧ = ⟦iteratedDeriv n f⟧` — so the abstract jets of a germ *are* the germs
of the analytic jets.  With that and the two jet-set shapes above, a presentation in `L` or `K_Γ`
reads as a polynomial in `iteratedDeriv n Complex.digamma` on the line, which is exactly what the
residue chain consumes; eq. (3) then quantifies over the field.

The field-level half is proved too: **`differentialJet_algebraMap`** — inside `upperEndMeroField`
the differential-field jets of an analytic germ are `upperEndAnalyticDeriv^[n]`, so the abstract jets
of a `differentialAdjoin` presentation are the iterated analytic derivatives of the representative.
With the germ identity above, a presentation of an element of `L` or `K_Γ` *is* a jet polynomial in
`iteratedDeriv n Complex.digamma` on the line.

(Elaboration note for whoever continues: the germ coercion `(f : Germ upperEndFilter ℂ)` only fires
inside `namespace GaloisForLFunctions.HTGeometry` with `open Filter Set Topology Function` and
`open scoped Differential`; in the plain `GaloisForLFunctions` namespace it silently fails to
elaborate.)

Everything else on the card's Lean side is done: §1–§8 including uniqueness in eq. (3), the §7
aperture bridge and hull-torus surjection, all of §8's boundary cases, and the unquantified residue
chain mapped above.

### Equations (3) and (5) with the card's own integer exponents

Eq. (3) reads `F(s) = c·Γ^k·s^a·(log s)^b·(1+o(1))` with `k, a, b ∈ ℤ`.  The corpus's `gammaJetShape`
carries *natural* exponents — that is what a Gamma polynomial with jet-polynomial coefficients
produces — so a general field element, a quotient of two such, was presented by a pair of shapes.
`HTGeometry/GammaShapeInteger.lean` supplies the integer shape and its calculus:

- `gammaShapeZ σ k a b t = Γ^k·s^a·(log s)^b` on the line, with `eventually_gammaShapeZ_ne_zero`,
  `gammaJetShape_eq_gammaShapeZ`, `gammaShapeZ_div` (quotients of shapes are shapes) and
  `gammaShapeZ_arg_add`;
- `hasPolyGrowthLine_zpow`, `hasPolyGrowthLine_zpow_vertical`, `hasPolyGrowthLine_zpow_vertLog` and
  `HasPolyGrowthLine.mul` — a *negative* power is bounded, so every integer power of `s` or `log s`
  still has polynomial growth, which is what the Gamma-decay estimate consumes;
- the three dominance limits — `tendsto_gammaShapeZ_zero_of_gamma_pos` (a positive Gamma exponent
  beats every algebraic-logarithmic factor), `tendsto_gammaShapeZ_zero_of_arg_neg`,
  `tendsto_gammaShapeZ_zero_of_log_neg` — assembled into
  **`tendsto_gammaShapeZ_ratio_zero`**: a strictly subordinate shape vanishes against the dominant
  one, in exactly the order of eq. (26);
- **`exists_dominant_shapeZ_index`** — a finite family of integer data has a dominant member, by the
  lexicographic minimum of `(k, −a, −b)`.

`HTGeometry/GammaFieldIntegerDatum.lean` then states eq. (3) in that form.  `RepresentsOnLine σ x φ`
says `φ` is the restriction of the carrier element `x` to the vertical line — `x` a quotient of two
analytic germs, `φ` the corresponding quotient of representatives — and

> **`exists_gammaField_intDatum`** — every nonzero `x ∈ ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩` has a line representative `φ` and
> integers `k, a, b` with `φ(σ+it)/(Γ^k·s^a·(log s)^b) → c ≠ 0`.

The clearing polynomials of the two germ presentations contribute only their degrees and leading
coefficients (`eventually_poly_eval_vertical_ne_zero` and the corpus's
`tendsto_eval_div_pow_natDegree_of_norm_atTop`), so they shift the argument exponent and multiply the
residue.  With the dominance order this gives eq. (5) in the card's own quantification:

> **`exists_dominant_gammaField_ratio`** — a finite tuple of nonzero elements of `ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩` has a
> component `β` such that every ratio `F_α/F_β` converges on the vertical line, to `c_α/c_β` at the
> components sharing the dominant datum and to `0` at every other; the limit vector is normalized by
> `d β = 1`, hence nonzero.

`#print axioms` clean throughout.

### §2's conclusion in the carrier: Hölder's theorem

Equations (15)–(16) end at *`ψ` is hypertranscendental over `ℂ(s)`*, which the card obtains from the
formal shift theorem plus Hölder's theorem for `Γ`.  The corpus can now prove both directly, in the
carrier, from its own flat elimination — no Adamczewski–Dreyfus–Hardouin and no external Hölder.

`HTGeometry/DigammaHypertranscendence.lean`:

- `jetMvPoly`, `jetMvPoly_ne_zero` — the jet polynomial attached to distinct exponent vectors and
  nonzero polynomial coefficients, and its nonvanishing (coefficient extraction at one exponent);
- **`digammaJetEval_jetMvPoly`** — that polynomial evaluates, in the half-plane germ ring, to the germ
  of the corresponding function of `ψ` and its derivatives;
- `algebraMap_digammaJetSum`, `subtype_digammaJetSum` — the same relation transported to the carrier:
  its analytic element is the germ of that function (`digammaJetMero_eq_algebraMap` and
  `digammaJet_analytic_coe` do the jet bookkeeping);
- `isVertFlat_of_eventually_zero` — a function that vanishes at the upper end vanishes on every
  vertical line beyond a height, hence decays faster than every power: it is *flat*;
- **`no_digamma_jet_relation_poly`** and, after clearing denominators
  (`exists_polynomial_clearing`), **`no_digamma_jet_relation`** — so no nonzero jet polynomial over
  `ℂ(s)` annihilates the digamma jets, since the corpus's `not_isVertFlatGerm_digammaJetEval` forbids
  a nonzero jet polynomial from being flat;
- **`hypertranscendentalOver_ratFunc_digammaMero`** — eq. (16): `ψ` is hypertranscendental over
  `ℂ(s)`.

Hölder's theorem itself is then three lines, using the corpus's differential-algebraicity closure
(`differentialAlgebraicOver_deriv`, `..._inv`, `..._mul`): if `Γ` satisfied an algebraic differential
equation over `ℂ(s)`, so would `ψ = Γ′·Γ⁻¹`, and it does not.

> **`hypertranscendentalOver_ratFunc_gammaUpperMero`** — the Euler Gamma function is
> hypertranscendental over `ℂ(s)`.

So the card's one external classical input on the `Γ` side is now internal.  `#print axioms` clean
throughout.

### §4 in the carrier: equations (21) and (22)

`HTGeometry/GammaFieldGeneratedByDigamma.lean` proves eq. (21) inside the upper-end carrier.  `Γ` has
no zeros, so its germ is invertible (**`gammaUpperMero_ne_zero`**), and `ψ = Γ′/Γ` therefore lies in
`ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩` (**`digammaMero_mem_gammaAdjoin`**); the field is closed under the derivation, so every
digamma jet lies in it too.  Conversely every jet of `Γ` is `Γ` times a polynomial in the digamma jets
(the corpus's `gammaJetAlgebra_le_ratAdjoin`).  Hence

> **`differentialAdjoin_gamma_eq_adjoin_gamma_digammaJets`** — `ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩` is the field generated by `Γ`
> together with all digamma jets: `K_Γ = L(Γ)`.

Eq. (22) — the transcendence of `Γ` over `L` — is then proved from the boundary datum rather than from
the `λ`-automorphism of the card.  `HTGeometry/GammaLineRepresentative.lean` gives the algebra of line
representatives (**`RepresentsOnLine.mul`**, `RepresentsOnLine.pow`, `representsOnLine_algebraMap`, and
**`representsOnLine_sum`**, which puts a finite sum over a common denominator) and the multiplicativity
of the datum (`gammaShapeZ_mul` and **`tendsto_datum_mul`**: the datum of a product is the sum of the
data with the product of the residues — the valuation property of the triple `(k, a, b)`).
`HTGeometry/GammaMonomialValuation.lean` computes the `Γ`-exponent on the two generating classes:

- **`exists_digammaField_intDatum`** — an element of `L` has datum with `Γ`-exponent `0` (its
  presentation is a quotient of jet polynomials, whose shape `polyJetShape` carries no `Γ`);
- `tendsto_gammaLine_pow_div_gammaShapeZ` — `Γ^n` has datum `(n, 0, 0)` with residue `1`;
- `representsOnLine_zero_eventually` — a representative of `0` vanishes eventually on the line.

So in a relation `∑ A_i Γ^i = 0` the terms have data with pairwise *distinct* `Γ`-exponents `i`; the
lowest one dominates every other, the normalized sum tends to its nonzero residue, and yet it
vanishes identically:

> **`transcendental_gammaUpperMero_over_digammaField`** — `Γ` is transcendental over `ℂ(s)⟨ψ⟩`.

With eq. (21) this is §4's structure theorem: `K_Γ = L(Γ)` is a rational function field in `Γ`, which
is what makes the presentation `(23)` `F = P(Γ)/Q(Γ)` meaningful.  `#print axioms` clean throughout.

### Uniqueness in equation (3), for the integer datum

Eq. (3) claims the integers and the constant are *unique*.
`HTGeometry/GammaFieldIntegerDatumUnique.lean` proves it from the dominance order alone:

- `shapeZ_trichotomy` — two integer data are equal or one is strictly subordinate;
- **`gammaShapeZ_datum_unique`** — a line function cannot have nonzero residues against two different
  shapes: the ratio of the two normalized limits *is* the shape ratio (where the function is
  eventually nonzero, which a nonzero residue guarantees), and a subordinate shape ratio tends to
  zero, contradicting a nonzero limit; with the data equal the residues agree by uniqueness of
  limits;
- **`representsOnLine_eventuallyEq`** — two line representatives of one carrier element agree
  eventually: the cross products of the two presentations are equal in the analytic subring, hence as
  germs, and a germ identity at the upper end holds along every vertical line;
- **`gammaField_intDatum_unique`** — so `(k, a, b, c)` depends only on the element, not on the
  presentation.  That is the uniqueness clause of eq. (3).

### §6 with the card's own hypothesis: infinite rank

The corpus's frequency-rank exclusions consume *independent families of every size* inside the
spectrum; the card states §6 with the group-theoretic hypothesis *infinite rational rank*.
`HTGeometry/InfiniteRankIndependentFamily.lean` bridges the two — extract a basis of the span from
the set itself (`exists_linearIndependent`); a finite basis would bound the rank (`rank_span_le`), so
the basis is infinite and any injection of a finite index type into it stays independent:

> **`exists_independent_family_of_infinite_rank`** — a set whose span has infinite rank contains
> linearly independent families of every finite size.

`HTGeometry/GammaFieldSpectrumRankCriterion.lean` then states §6 in the card's words:

> **`hypertranscendentalOver_gammaField_of_infinite_spectrum_rank`** — if some subset of the Bohr
> spectrum has infinite rational rank, the function is hypertranscendental over `ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩`;

and specialises it to eq. (30) — a generalized Dirichlet series `∑ a_n e^{−λ_n s}` with pairwise
distinct exponents, nonzero coefficients and uniform convergence on the line, whose exponents are
spectral frequencies because the Bohr coefficient at `−λ_m` is `a_m·e^{−λ_m σ}`
(**`neg_lam_mem_bohrSpectrum`**, from the corpus's `bohrCoefficient_dirichlet_line`) — giving eq. (31):

> **`hypertranscendentalOver_gammaField_of_dirichlet`** — such a series with an exponent group of
> infinite rational rank is hypertranscendental over `ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩`.
