`#print axioms` clean throughout.

### The law in the card's own quantification

Up to now every Lean form of the finite-phase theorem took the relation (27) as a hypothesis.  The
card states it about a *function*: `f` differentially algebraic over `K_Γ` implies (8) and (9).  That
gap is now closed, and it closes by definition-chasing rather than analysis.  Differential
algebraicity over a base is the failure of algebraic independence of the jet family, and a failure of
algebraic independence is a vanishing nonzero polynomial — whose support supplies exactly the distinct
exponent vectors and nonzero coefficients the law consumes.  `JetHypertranscendenceBridge.lean` states
that extraction (**`exists_jet_relation_of_not_algebraicIndependent`**, and its packaged form
**`exists_jet_relation_of_differentialAlgebraic`**), and
`HTGeometry/GammaFieldDifferentialAlgebraicLaw.lean` runs the law on it:

> **`finite_phase_law_of_differentialAlgebraic`** — a holomorphic, vertically uniformly almost
> periodic `Φ` whose germ is differentially algebraic over `ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩` satisfies an autonomous algebraic
> differential equation on the strip, with a nonzero constant-coefficient polynomial, and its vertical
> Bohr frequency group has finite rank.

That is eq. (8) and eq. (9) with the hypothesis in the card's own words — no relation supplied, no
coefficient datum chosen by hand.  Its contrapositive is §6's criterion, also now a theorem:

> **`hypertranscendentalOver_gammaField_of_infinite_rank`** — a holomorphic vertically uniformly
> almost periodic function whose Bohr frequency group has infinite rank is hypertranscendental over
> `ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩`.

`#print axioms` clean for both.

The last hypotheses that were not the card's own are gone too.  The law's earlier Lean forms carried
the central-line restriction `f` as data, together with its uniform continuity and the total
boundedness of its translation orbit.  All three are consequences of hypotheses (6) and (7): the
corpus's `verticalRestriction` packages the line restriction as a bounded continuous function, and
`isBohrAlmostPeriodic_line` plus `uniformContinuous_of_isBohrAlmostPeriodic` and
`hasTotallyBoundedOrbit_of_isBohrAlmostPeriodic` supply the rest.  So

> **`finite_phase_law_of_differentialAlgebraic_strip`** — holomorphic on the strip, uniformly almost
> periodic there, differentially algebraic over `ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩` ⟹ an autonomous algebraic differential
> equation on the strip and finite rank of the vertical Bohr frequency group,

with the four hypotheses of §1 and nothing else, and its contrapositive
**`hypertranscendentalOver_gammaField_of_infinite_rank_strip`**.  `#print axioms` clean.

(Correction to the note above on where a session should start: the field-theoretic packaging it
describes as remaining is in fact built — **`exists_gammaField_datum`** in
`HTGeometry/GammaFieldRatDatum.lean` quantifies over `x ∈ differentialAdjoin (RatFunc ℂ) {Γ}`, i.e.
over the abstract carrier `K_Γ`, and produces the eq. (3) datum for it.  The note predates that
module.)

### The tube uniformity: first step

The `o(1)` of eq. (3) is claimed uniform for a variable in a compact subset of a vertical tube.  The
corpus's depth expansion already carries that uniformity in its statement — `exists_jet_expansion`
produces one constant `C` valid for **every** `σ > 0` — so the leading law of each jet upgrades from
one line to a tube.  `HTGeometry/PolygammaUniformLeadingAsymptotic.lean`:

- `norm_vertical_ge_im` — on a vertical line the modulus dominates the ordinate;
- **`exists_norm_pow_div_risingProd_sub_one_le`** — a rising product agrees with the corresponding
  power up to a relative error `C/‖s‖`, the constant depending only on the length (from
  `exists_norm_risingProd_sub_pow_le` and `norm_pow_le_norm_risingProd`);
- **`exists_uniform_height_pow_mul_iteratedDeriv_digamma`** — for every `ε` there is a height beyond
  which `s^{m+1}·ψ⁽ᵐ⁺¹⁾(s)` is within `ε` of `(−1)^m m!` *simultaneously for all* `0 < σ ≤ M`.  The
  expansion's constant is `σ`-free and `‖s‖ ≤ (1+M)t`, so both the shape defect and the expansion
  error are `O(1/t)` with a constant depending only on `M` and `m`; the value of the constant is then
  identified by the known limit on the single line `σ = M`;
- **`tendstoUniformlyOn_pow_mul_iteratedDeriv_digamma`** — the same as `TendstoUniformlyOn … (Ioc 0 M)`.

That is the first jet-level step of the tube form of eq. (3).

The *extraction* step is uniform too, and with no upper bound on the abscissa at all.
`HTGeometry/StdRatDatumUniform.lean`:

- **`exists_uniform_bound_polynomial_mul_inv_pow`** — `‖P(s)·s^{−deg P} − lead P‖ ≤ B/t` with
  `B = ∑_{i<deg P} ‖P_i‖`: the subleading terms each carry at least one factor `s^{-1}`, and
  `‖s‖ ≥ t` on every vertical line;
- **`exists_uniform_bound_shiftProd_mul_inv_pow`** — `‖∏_j(s+j)·s^{−|l|} − 1‖ ≤ B/t`, by induction on
  the multiset: one factor costs `j/t` and the rest are bounded;
- **`exists_uniform_height_stdRat_datum`** — hence for every `ε` a height beyond which
  `s^p·P(s)/∏_j(s+j)` is within `ε` of `lead P` *simultaneously for all `σ > 0`*.  The denominator
  factor is `≥ 1/2` beyond `2B₂`, so the quotient splits into `(X − lead)/Y` and `lead·(1 − Y)/Y`,
  both `O(1/t)`.

Both the jet packages (`exists_jet_package`, `exists_monomial_package`, `exists_jetPoly_package`) and
this extraction are stated with constants that do not see `σ`, and the approximating standard rational
function is itself chosen independently of `σ`.

**The dichotomy is uniform too**, and that closes eq. (19) for constant coefficients in the tube form.
`HTGeometry/JetDatumUniform.lean`:

- `norm_vertical_le_of_le` — on a tube `0 < σ ≤ M` the modulus is at most `(1+M)t`;
- **`exists_uniform_datum_of_not_flat`** — if a family `f σ` is approximated to every depth by
  standard rational functions *chosen once for all abscissas*, and `f` fails to be flat on **one**
  line, then there are `p` and `c ≠ 0` such that `s^p·f(σ+it) → c` uniformly on every tube.  The
  proof is the per-line dichotomy with the two branches read correctly: a vanishing numerator makes
  `f` small on the one line where non-flatness fails, while a nonzero numerator gives a datum that the
  uniform extraction transports to every line at once;
- **`exists_uniform_jetPoly_datum`** — instantiated at a jet polynomial with constant coefficients:
  the tube form of eq. (19), with non-flatness needed on a single line only (which is exactly what the
  corpus's flat elimination produces).

The coefficient field costs nothing more: `exists_jetPolyCoeff_package` is stated with the same
abscissa-free constants, so **`exists_uniform_jetPolyCoeff_datum`** gives the tube form of eq. (19)
for jet polynomials with `ℂ(s)` coefficients as well.

**The `Γ` layer is uniform too.**  The corpus's `t^N·Γ(σ+it) → 0` goes through the Fourier transform
and fixes the line, but two facts the corpus already owns give the tube version directly:
`norm_Gamma_le_Gamma_re` (the modulus is at most the real Gamma of the abscissa, from the integral
representation) and `norm_Gamma_add_nat` (`‖Γ(z+m)‖ = (∏_{i<m}‖z+i‖)·‖Γ(z)‖`).  On a vertical line
every factor `‖z+i‖` is at least the ordinate, so shifting `k+2` steps right costs `t^{k+2}` and lands
where the real Gamma is monotone:

> **`exists_uniform_bound_norm_Gamma_vertical`** (`HTGeometry/GammaVerticalUniformDecay.lean`) — for
> every `k` one constant `C` with `‖Γ(σ+it)‖ ≤ C/t^k` for all `0 < σ ≤ M` and `t ≥ 1`.

**The logarithmic level is uniform as well.**  The per-line statement `ψ(σ+it) − (log t + iπ/2) → 0`
rests on two bounds that never see the abscissa — the corpus's
`RHTower.re_digamma_vertical_bound_uncond` (`|Re ψ − ½log(σ²+t²)| ≤ 1/t² + π/(2t)`) and
`RHTower.im_digamma_vertical_sandwich` (`π/2 − arctan(σ/t) ≤ Im ψ ≤ 1/t + π/2 − arctan(σ/t)`) — so with
`½log(1+σ²/t²) ≤ σ²/(2t²)` and `arctan x ≤ x` the whole difference is `O(1/t)` with one constant:

> **`exists_uniform_bound_digamma_sub_vertLog`** (`HTGeometry/DigammaVerticalUniformLog.lean`) —
> `‖ψ(σ+it) − (log t + iπ/2)‖ ≤ C/t` for all `0 ≤ σ ≤ M`, `t ≥ 1`.

`HTGeometry/DigammaLogPowersUniform.lean` carries that to the `ψ`-degree itself:
`norm_pow_sub_one_le_of_norm_sub_one_le` (a number within `1` of `1` has modulus at most `2`, so a
power costs only `2^e`), **`exists_uniform_height_digamma_div_vertLog`** (`ψ/L → 1` uniformly, since
`‖ψ − L‖ = O(1/t)` while `‖L‖ ≥ log t ≥ 1`) and **`exists_uniform_height_digamma_pow_div_vertLog_pow`**
(`ψ^e/L^e → 1` uniformly).

`HTGeometry/UniformTubeLimit.lean` gives the assembly its algebra.  `TubeLimit M f c` names the shape
every uniform statement above has — *for every `ε` a height beyond which `f σ t` is within `ε` of `c`
for all `0 < σ ≤ M` at once* — and then:

- **`tubeLimit_of_bound`** — any `O(1/t)` bound with an abscissa-free constant is a tube limit;
- `TubeLimit.exists_bound`, **`TubeLimit.add`**, **`TubeLimit.mul`**, `TubeLimit.congr` — tube limits
  are eventually bounded, and add, multiply and transfer along pointwise identities;
- `tubeLimit_pow_mul_iteratedDeriv_digamma`, `tubeLimit_digamma_pow_div_vertLog_pow`,
  **`tubeLimit_jetPolyCoeff_datum`** — the jets, the powers of the logarithm and the datum of a
  non-flat jet polynomial with rational coefficients, restated in that language;
- **`TubeLimit.mul_digamma_pow_div_vertLog_pow`** — the logarithmic level of a datum: multiplying by
  `ψ^e` and dividing by `L^e` preserves the limit, the tube form of `tendsto_datum_mul_digamma_pow`.

`HTGeometry/UniformShapeBounds.lean` adds the shape factors and one more calculus rule:

- **`tubeLimit_of_squeeze`** — a quantity dominated on the tube by an abscissa-free null sequence has
  tube limit zero (the form the dominance limits of eq. (26) take);
- **`norm_zpow_vertical_le`** — `‖s^a‖ ≤ (1+M)^{a⁺}·t^a` on the tube, for *integer* `a`: positive
  exponents through `‖s‖ ≤ (1+M)t`, negative ones through `‖s‖ ≥ t ≥ 1`;
- **`norm_vertLog_zpow_le`** — `‖L^b‖ ≤ (log t + π/2)^{b⁺}`, with no abscissa in sight.

`HTGeometry/UniformShapeDominance.lean` runs the three cases of eq. (26) in that language:

- `zpow_le_zpow_of_le_of_neg`, `tendsto_log_zpow_neg` — a negative integer power is antitone in the
  base above `1`, and the logarithm's negative powers vanish;
- **`tubeLimit_gammaShapeZ_zero_of_log_neg`**, **`tubeLimit_gammaShapeZ_zero_of_arg_neg`**,
  **`tubeLimit_gammaShapeZ_zero_of_gamma_pos`** — the logarithmic, argument and `Γ` cases, each a
  squeeze against an abscissa-free null sequence (`(log t)^b`, `(2 log t)^{b⁺}/t`, and
  `D^K·C·(1+M)^{a⁺}·2^{b⁺}/t` respectively; the last uses the uniform `Γ` decay at order
  `a⁺ + b⁺ + 2`);
- `gammaShapeZ_div_of_le` — beyond `t = 3` the quotient of two shapes *is* the shape at the
  difference, with the nonvanishing checked explicitly rather than eventually;
- **`tubeLimit_gammaShapeZ_ratio_zero`** — hence a strictly subordinate shape vanishes against the
  dominant one uniformly on the tube.  That is eq. (26) in tube form.

`HTGeometry/UniformDominantSum.lean` closes the assembly step:

- **`TubeLimit.finsetSum`** (and its `Fin N` form `TubeLimit.sum`) — tube limits add over a finite
  index;
- `gammaShapeZ_ne_zero_of_le` — the shape is nonzero beyond `t = 3`, at an explicit threshold rather
  than eventually;
- **`tubeLimit_sum_div_dominant`** — a finite sum of quantities, each with a residue against its own
  shape, has the residue of its dominant member against that member's shape, uniformly on the tube.
  Each subordinate term factors as `(F_α/S_α)·(S_α/S_β)` — a product of tube limits whose second
  factor vanishes by eq. (26) — so only the dominant residue survives.

Ties are allowed too: **`tubeLimit_sum_div_dominant_class`** keeps the residues of every member
sharing the dominant datum and kills the rest, and **`exists_tubeLimit_dominant_class`** produces the
dominant index itself from `exists_dominant_shapeZ_index`, so a finite family of data needs no
dominance hypothesis chosen by hand:

> a finite nonempty family, each member with a residue against its own shape, has — uniformly on the
> tube — the residue `∑_{α : datum α = datum β} c_α` against the shape of a dominant member `β`.

`HTGeometry/UniformGammaJetTerm.lean` runs a whole presentation through that machinery:

- `tubeLimit_div_gammaShapeZ_of_datum` — a datum `s^p·g → c` *is* a residue against the shape `s^{-p}`;
- **`tubeLimit_gammaJet_term`** — multiplying by `Γ^u` and `ψ^e` multiplies the shape by `Γ^u` and
  `(log s)^e`: dividing the term by its own shape leaves exactly `ψ^e/(log s)^e` times `s^p·g`, a
  product of two tube limits;
- **`exists_tubeLimit_gammaJet_residue`** — hence a finite family of Gamma-jet terms, each with a
  uniform datum, has the residue of its dominant class against that member's shape, uniformly on the
  tube.

That is the tube form of eq. (3) for a Gamma-field element in normal form, with the dominant datum
produced rather than assumed.

**Equation (5) follows on tubes as well.**  `HTGeometry/UniformDominantRatio.lean` adds the two
missing calculus rules and the tuple law:

- `TubeLimit.exists_lower_bound` — a tube limit with nonzero value keeps its quantity `≥ ‖c‖/2`
  uniformly; **`TubeLimit.inv`** — hence tube limits invert away from zero, with the estimate
  `‖f⁻¹ − c⁻¹‖ ≤ (2/‖c‖²)‖f − c‖`;
- **`exists_tubeLimit_dominant_ratio`** — dividing a finite family by a dominant member, *every* ratio
  converges uniformly on the tube: to `c_α/c_β` on the dominant class and to `0` elsewhere, with the
  normalizing member's ratio equal to `1`.  The proof is the factorisation
  `F_α/F_β = (F_α/S_α)·(S_α/S_β)·(S_β/F_β)`, three tube limits multiplied.

The last hypothesis comes off too: **`exists_uniform_jetPolyCoeff_datum_of_ne_zero`** supplies the
non-flatness from the flat elimination at a single line (`exists_not_isLineBigO_jetPolyCoeff` at
`σ = 1`), so a jet polynomial with rational coefficients that is nonzero *as a polynomial* has a
uniform datum on every tube, with nothing assumed about the line.

A presentation's monomials involve *all* jets, `ψ` included, while the datum machinery is stated for
the positive-order ones.  `HTGeometry/UniformJetMonomialDatum.lean` bridges that: `prod_jets_split`
peels the `ψ`-slot off the product (`Fin.prod_univ_succ`), and

> **`exists_tubeLimit_jetMonomial_datum`** — a single jet monomial with a nonzero polynomial
> coefficient has a residue against `s^{-p}·(log s)^{E 0}`, uniformly on every tube,

with the positive part supplying the datum and the `ψ`-power supplying the logarithm.

Summing the monomials closes eq. (19) on tubes for an element of `ℂ(s)⟨ψ⟩` in normal form:

> **`exists_tubeLimit_jetPoly_full_residue`** — every monomial (all jets, nonzero polynomial
> coefficient) has its own datum, and the sum has the residue of the dominant class, uniformly on the
> tube.

And the `Γ` layer on top of it: `TubeLimit.mul_gammaPow` (multiplying a residue by `Γ^u` multiplies
its shape by the same power, so the quotient is untouched) gives

> **`exists_tubeLimit_gammaJetPoly_residue`** — a Gamma-field presentation
> `∑_α Γ^{u_α}·a_α(s)/s^d·∏_i (ψ^{(i)})^{E_{α i}}` has the residue of its dominant class, uniformly on
> the tube.

That is eq. (3) in tube form for a presentation, with the dominant datum produced rather than assumed.

A presentation's terms carry their own jet ranges, so `HTGeometry/UniformJetPadding.lean` supplies
the last piece of plumbing: `padExp` extends an exponent vector by zeros, **`prod_jets_pad`** shows a
jet monomial is unchanged by that padding, and **`exists_tubeLimit_gammaJetPoly_residue_pad`** states
the residue theorem for a family whose terms have *different* jet ranges — the shape the clearing data
actually produces.

A presentation's natural index is a *sigma* type — a `Γ`-power together with a monomial of the
corresponding coefficient — not an interval, and the dominance argument never used the interval
structure.  `HTGeometry/UniformDominantFintype.lean` restates it over an arbitrary finite index:
**`exists_dominant_shapeZ_index_fintype`**, `tubeLimit_sum_div_dominant_class_fintype` and
**`exists_tubeLimit_dominant_class_fintype`** need only `Fintype` and `Nonempty`, so the presentation
data feeds the residue theorem with no flattening step at all.

> **`exists_tubeLimit_gammaPresentation_residue`** (`HTGeometry/UniformGammaPresentation.lean`) — a
> presentation in the *nested* shape the clearing theorem produces — finitely many `Γ`-powers, each
> with its own jet polynomial, each with its own number of monomials and its own jet range — has the
> residue of its dominant class, uniformly on the tube.

Indexed by the sigma type `Σ j, Fin (M j)` and padded to a common jet range, the tube residue theorem
applies with no reindexing: that is eq. (3) in tube form stated on the data the algebra hands over.

The clearing theorem hands over *plain* polynomial coefficients, not coefficients over a common
power of the argument, and that last normalization comes off with `TubeLimit.mul_pow_arg`
(multiplying a residue by `s^d` shifts its argument exponent by `d`):

> **`exists_tubeLimit_gammaPresentation_residue_raw`** — the residue, uniformly on the tube, of a
> presentation `∑_j Γ^{u_j}·(∑_α a_{jα}(s)·∏_i (ψ^{(i)})^{E_{jα i}})` with plain polynomial
> coefficients, per-power monomial counts and per-power jet ranges.

That is eq. (3) in tube form on exactly the data `exists_gammaAlgebra_clearing` produces.

A cleared presentation need not have all its coefficients nonzero, and a monomial with zero
coefficient has no datum.  Those terms are the zero function, so dropping them changes nothing:

> **`exists_tubeLimit_gammaPresentation_residue_drop`** — the residue, uniformly on the tube, of a
> presentation with arbitrary polynomial coefficients, indexed by the *surviving* subfamily
> `{x // a_x ≠ 0}`.  The only hypothesis left is that some coefficient is nonzero.

**A correction to the term-by-term route.**  Taking each monomial's residue separately and summing is
*not* enough for a carrier element: monomials sharing a `ψ`-degree can cancel at leading order, so the
dominant class residue `∑_{class} c_α` could vanish even with every `c_α ≠ 0`.  The per-line chain
avoids this by sending a whole `ψ`-degree group through the non-flatness dichotomy at once, and the
tube form must do the same.  `HTGeometry/UniformJetPolyGroup.lean` does:

> **`exists_tubeLimit_jetPolyGroup_datum`** — a group of monomials sharing a `ψ`-degree, with distinct
> exponent vectors and some nonzero coefficient, has a datum with *nonzero* residue against
> `s^{-p}·(log s)^e`, uniformly on the tube.

Factoring the shared `ψ`-power out turns the group into a jet polynomial in the positive-order jets —
whose exponent vectors stay distinct precisely because the `ψ`-slot is common — and the
hypothesis-free datum handles it as a whole, whatever cancellation occurs inside.  Across groups the
`log`-degrees differ, so no cancellation can occur there.

Across groups nothing can tie, and `HTGeometry/UniformDistinctKeyResidue.lean` turns that into the
nonvanishing of the total residue:

- **`exists_tubeLimit_distinctKey_residue`** — if the members of a family are separated by their
  `Γ`-exponent together with their `log`-degree, the dominant class is a *singleton*, so the residue
  of the sum is a single member's residue;
- **`exists_tubeLimit_logSeparated_residue`** — the `ψ`-degree groups of a jet polynomial, which have
  distinct log degrees by construction.

Together with the group datum this is the architecture the per-line chain uses: cancellation is
possible only *inside* a `ψ`-degree group, where the dichotomy absorbs it, and never *between* groups,
where the shapes differ.

The groups of a jet polynomial are *subsets* of its monomial index, not intervals, so
**`exists_tubeLimit_jetPolyGroup_datum_fintype`** (`HTGeometry/UniformJetPolyGroupFintype.lean`)
restates the group datum over an arbitrary finite index, transporting along the canonical equivalence
with an interval — sums move by `Fintype.sum_equiv` and injectivity is preserved.

So both clauses of §1 that the earlier audit flagged as line-restricted — the `o(1)` of eq. (3) and
the convergence of eq. (5) — now have their tube forms in the corpus.

With this, every analytic input of the residue chain is available in tube form: the jets
(`exists_uniform_height_pow_mul_iteratedDeriv_digamma`), the extraction
(`exists_uniform_height_stdRat_datum`), the dichotomy (`exists_uniform_datum_of_not_flat`), the
coefficient field (`exists_uniform_jetPolyCoeff_datum`), the logarithm (above) and the `Γ` layer
(`exists_uniform_bound_norm_Gamma_vertical`).  What is left is assembly — running the same grouping and
dominance bookkeeping the per-line chain performs, with the uniform bounds in place of the pointwise
limits.
`#print axioms` clean.

The assembly is now complete, at the carrier level.  A jet polynomial whose monomials carry *mixed*
`ψ`-degrees splits into the fibres of `α ↦ E α 0`; each fibre is a group, so
**`exists_tubeLimit_jetPolyGroup_datum_fintype`** gives it a datum with nonzero residue, and distinct
fibres carry distinct `log`-degrees, so **`exists_tubeLimit_logSeparated_residue`** picks out a single
one.  That is **`exists_tubeLimit_jetPoly_mixed_residue`** (`HTGeometry/UniformJetPolyMixed.lean`):
every jet polynomial with distinct exponent vectors and nonzero coefficients has a nonzero residue
against an integer Gamma shape, uniformly on `0 < σ ≤ M`.

One level up, a presentation `∑_j Γ^{u_j}·P_j` is grouped by its `Γ`-power instead.  Each `P_j` is a
jet polynomial and contributes a nonzero residue by the previous paragraph; distinct `u_j` cannot tie,
so **`exists_tubeLimit_distinctKeyZ_residue`** again selects one group.  This is
**`exists_tubeLimit_gammaPresentation_residue_nonzero`**
(`HTGeometry/UniformGammaPresentationNonzero.lean`), stated over arbitrary finite index types so that
the pruning of vanishing coefficients — and of `Γ`-powers all of whose coefficients vanish — can be
done by passing to subtypes: **`exists_tubeLimit_gammaPresentation_residue_prune`** asks only that
*some* coefficient is nonzero.  The bookkeeping lemma **`padExp_injective`** carries distinctness of
exponent vectors through the padding to a common jet range.

Feeding the clearing theorem's output into that gives the carrier statement:
**`exists_tubeLimit_gammaAlgebra_datum`** (`HTGeometry/UniformGammaAlgebraDatum.lean`) — a nonzero
element of the differential algebra `ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩_jet` has, after clearing one polynomial denominator, an
analytic germ with a *nonzero* residue against one integer Gamma shape, with a single height threshold
valid for every abscissa in `(0, M]`.  The nonvanishing hypothesis is discharged internally: if every
coefficient polynomial vanished, every `C_j` would be the zero germ and the cleared element would be
zero.  A common jet range and a common degree bound come from `Finset.sup` over the finitely many
coefficients.

Dividing two such data gives the field statement **`exists_tubeLimit_gammaField_datum`**
(`HTGeometry/UniformGammaFieldDatum.lean`), the tube analogue of **`exists_gammaField_datum`**: every
nonzero element of `K_Γ` is a quotient of two cleared germs whose residues exist uniformly on the tube
and whose ratio is nonzero.  The quotient step is `TubeLimit.div`, from `TubeLimit.mul` and
`TubeLimit.inv` — the denominator's residue being nonzero is what makes the ratio uniform, and that is
exactly the nonvanishing the group architecture was built to preserve.

So eq. (3) and eq. (5) now hold in the corpus in both forms, on the line and on the tube, and the tube
form is proved for the same carrier objects as the line form, not for a model of them.
`#print axioms` clean.

The datum of the *element* — not of its cleared germ — is one step further: the two clearing
polynomials contribute their leading coefficients and shift the argument exponent by
`deg q₂ − deg q₁`.  Both contributions are uniform, the first by `tubeLimit_poly_div_pow` (which reads
the tube bound `exists_uniform_bound_polynomial_mul_inv_pow` through `tubeLimit_of_bound`), the second
by the pointwise shape identities `gammaShapeZ_div_of_le` and `gammaShapeZ_arg_add_of_le`.  So
**`exists_tubeLimit_gammaField_intDatum`** (`HTGeometry/UniformGammaFieldIntDatum.lean`) gives every
nonzero `x ∈ K_Γ` a triple `(k, A, B)` and a nonzero `c` with
`φ(σ, t)/gammaShapeZ σ k A B t → c` uniformly for `0 < σ ≤ M` — the tube form of eq. (3).

`RepresentsOnTube` is the representation datum of that statement: the same quotient of analytic germs
as `RepresentsOnLine`, but read on every vertical line at once, and
`representsOnLine_of_representsOnTube` restricts it to one abscissa.  Feeding the tube data into
**`exists_tubeLimit_dominant_ratio`** gives **`exists_tubeLimit_dominant_gammaField_ratio`**, the tube
form of eqs. (4)–(5): a finite tuple of nonzero elements of `K_Γ` has a dominant member, and every
ratio to it converges with a single height threshold for the whole tube.

### The datum is an invariant of the element, not of the line

The tube form has a consequence the line form cannot see.  `TubeLimit.tendsto_line` restricts a tube
limit to every vertical line the tube covers, so a tube datum is the datum of each of those lines; and
the line datum is unique (`hasGammaDatum_unique`).  Since every abscissa lies in some tube — take
`M = max σ σ'` — the two data must agree:

> **`hasGammaDatum_abscissa_independent`** (`HTGeometry/UniformGammaDatumAbscissa.lean`): for a nonzero
> `x ∈ K_Γ`, the `Γ`-exponent `k`, the argument exponent `a`, the logarithm exponent `b` and the
> residue `c` are the same on every vertical line `Re s = σ > 0`.

So `(k, a, b, c)` is an invariant of `x`, not a feature of the line on which it is read.  The tube
datum itself is packaged as `HasGammaTubeDatum` with **`exists_hasGammaTubeDatum`**,
**`hasGammaTubeDatum_unique`** and **`existsUnique_gammaTube_datum`** — the tube analogue of
**`existsUnique_gammaField_datum`**, and the reading of eq. (3) the prose intends.

This also settles a question the line theory leaves open: the map `x ↦ (k, a, b)` is a well-defined
map on `K_Γ \ {0}` — it is the exponential valuation of §4 together with the algebraic-logarithmic
residue of §3, and the tube statement is what makes it independent of the presentation *and* of the
line.

### The tube datum is a valuation, and §4's "one exponential direction" is a theorem

`HTGeometry/UniformGammaDatumAlgebra.lean` gives the tube datum the laws of the line datum:
**`HasGammaTubeDatum.mul`** (the three exponents add, the residues multiply), **`.inv`**,
`hasGammaTubeDatum_gammaUpperMero` (`Γ` has datum `(1, 0, 0)` with residue `1`), and
`HasGammaTubeDatum.ne_zero`.  The shape identities behind them are the pointwise
`gammaShapeZ_mul_of_le` and `tubeLimit_const`.  On the digamma side, `digammaAdjoin_le_gammaAdjoin`
(from `digammaMero_mem_gammaAdjoin` and derivation-closure) lets the tube datum be read there too, and
the `Γ`-exponent is `0`: **`exists_hasGammaTubeDatum_digammaField`**,
**`existsUnique_digammaTube_datum`** — eq. (19) on a tube — and
`gammaUpperMero_not_mem_digammaAdjoin_tube`.

`HTGeometry/UniformGammaDatumHom.lean` turns this into the structural statement of §4.  Every unit of
`K_Γ` has exactly one datum, so choosing it defines **`gammaUnitDatum`**, and the laws above say the
choice is multiplicative:

> **`gammaDatumHom : K_Γˣ →* Multiplicative (ℤ × ℤ × ℤ) × ℂˣ`** — the boundary datum is a group
> homomorphism.

Its first component is **`gammaExponentHom : K_Γˣ →* Multiplicative ℤ`**, and the two facts that make
it *the* exponential valuation direction are:

> **`gammaExponentHom_gammaUnit`** — `Γ` is a uniformizer, `v(Γ) = 1`;
> **`gammaExponentHom_surjective`** — every integer occurs, on the corresponding power of `Γ`;
> **`gammaExponentHom_eq_one_of_mem_digammaAdjoin`** — every unit lying in `ℂ(s)⟨ψ⟩` is in the kernel.

So `K_Γ = L(Γ)` carries a surjective `ℤ`-valued valuation with uniformizer `Γ` that is trivial on `L`.
That is the sentence "adjoining `Γ` creates one exponential valuation direction" as a Lean theorem
rather than as a description of the asymptotics.

### `K_Γ` is a discretely valued field

The datum homomorphism is only half of a valuation; the other half is the ultrametric inequality, and
that is a genuine analytic statement rather than a formality.  It is proved in
`HTGeometry/GammaDatumUltrametric.lean`:

> **`hasGammaDatum_add_exponent_ge`**: if `x`, `y` and `x + y` have data with `Γ`-exponents `k`, `k'`
> and `m`, then `min k k' ≤ m`.

The argument is the one the prose gives for eq. (25), run backwards.  Suppose `m < k` and `m < k'`.
Dividing by the sum, `x/(x+y)` and `y/(x+y)` have positive `Γ`-exponents, so by
`tendsto_zero_of_hasGammaDatum_gamma_pos` both of their line representatives tend to `0`.  But their
sum is `1`: the numerator germ of the sum equals its denominator germ, so wherever the two
denominators survive — which is eventually, since each representative is eventually nonzero — the two
representatives add up to `1`.  A sum of two functions tending to `0` cannot be eventually `1`.
Cancellation can only *raise* the exponent, never lower it, which is exactly the inequality.

With multiplicativity and the ultrametric inequality in hand, `HTGeometry/GammaFieldValuation.lean`
assembles mathlib's own structure:

> **`gammaValuation : Valuation K_Γ ℤₘ₀`** — the exponential valuation of §4, with
> **`gammaValuation_gamma`** (`v(Γ) = ofAdd (-1)`, so `Γ` is a uniformizer) and
> **`gammaValuation_eq_one_of_mem_digammaAdjoin`** (`v ≡ 1` on `ℂ(s)⟨ψ⟩`).

So §4's sentence is now a theorem in the strongest available form: `K_Γ = L(Γ)` carries a discrete
valuation with value group `ℤ`, uniformizer `Γ`, and trivial restriction to `L`.  The valuation's
existence is what makes "one exponential direction" precise, and its triviality on `L` is what makes
the direction *new*.

### Equation (26) is the value group

The `Γ`-exponent alone is a valuation of rank one, but eq. (26) orders the *whole* triple, and the
whole triple is a valuation too.  `HTGeometry/GammaDatumLexValuation.lean` states the order as a
predicate — **`ShapeDominates`**, matching the disjunction that `exists_dominant_shapeZ_index` and
`tendsto_gammaShapeZ_ratio_zero` already use — and proves the ultrametric inequality for it:

> **`not_hasGammaDatum_add_dominates`**: the datum of `x + y` never dominates the data of both `x`
> and `y`.

The proof is the exponent argument run in the general order: if the sum's datum dominated both, then
after dividing by the sum both quotients would have data dominated by `(0, 0, 0)`, and
`tendsto_gammaShapeZ_zero_of_dominated` — the three cases `k > 0`, `k = 0 ∧ a < 0`,
`k = 0 ∧ a = 0 ∧ b < 0` — makes both representatives tend to `0`, while their sum is eventually `1`.

Mapping `(k, a, b)` to `toLex (-k, toLex (a, b))` turns dominance into the order of `ℤ ×ₗ ℤ ×ₗ ℤ`
(**`lexValue_lt_iff`**), the triple's additivity into the group law (`lexValue_add`), and the
inequality above into mathlib's `map_add_le_max'`:

> **`gammaLexValuation : Valuation K_Γ (WithZero (Multiplicative (ℤ ×ₗ ℤ ×ₗ ℤ)))`** — the boundary
> datum of eq. (3), read with the order of eq. (26), is a rank-three valuation of the Gamma field.

Its first coordinate is `gammaValuation`; the other two record the algebraic-logarithmic part of §3.
So the two orders the paper introduces — the exponential one of §4 and the lexicographic refinement of
eq. (26) — are the rank-one and rank-three valuations of the same datum.

### The general law is not the constant-coefficient law

`exists_gammaField_solution_rank_eq` already shows the carrier-level law's hypothesis class is
inhabited at every rank, but every coefficient it produces is a *constant* — an element of the digamma
field, of `Γ`-exponent `0`.  With the valuation in hand the stronger statement can be made:

> **`gammaField_law_witness_gamma_coefficients`** (`HTGeometry/GammaCoefficientLawWitness.lean`): for
> every `σ > 0` and every `μ ≠ 0`, the relation `Γ·Φ′ − μΓ·Φ = 0` satisfied by `Φ = e^{μ z}` has two
> coefficients that lie in `ℂ(s)⟨Γ⟩`, are nonzero, have `Γ`-exponent exactly `1`, and therefore lie
> **outside** `ℂ(s)⟨ψ⟩`; the law returns a rank between `1` and a finite bound.

So the general form of the law covers instances the constant-coefficient form cannot: its hypothesis
class contains coefficient families of nonzero exponential valuation.  The supporting pieces are
`expLineUpperAnalytic` (the exponential as an analytic germ),
`upperEndAnalyticDeriv_expLineUpperAnalytic` and **`differentialJet_one_expLine`** (its first-order
equation in the carrier and in the field), **`hasGammaDatum_const`** (a nonzero constant has the
trivial datum), and `not_mem_digammaAdjoin_of_gammaExponent_one`.

### The monomial normal form, and §3 as a rank-two valuation

Two structural consequences follow from the valuation.

Powers of `Γ` have datum `(n, 0, 0)` with residue `1` — **`hasGammaTubeDatum_gammaPow`** by induction,
**`hasGammaTubeDatum_gammaZpow`** for integer exponents through `HasGammaTubeDatum.inv` — so dividing
an element by the power its datum prescribes removes the exponential direction:

> **`exists_gammaZpow_normal_form`** (`HTGeometry/GammaDatumZpow.lean`): every nonzero `x ∈ K_Γ` is
> `Γ^k · y` with `y ∈ K_Γ` of `Γ`-exponent `0`.

That is eq. (3) read multiplicatively: `k` is the exponential part and everything else lives in the
`Γ`-exponent-zero locus.

On the digamma field the `Γ`-exponent is always `0`, so the datum reduces to the pair `(a, b)` of §3 —
the argument exponent and the logarithm exponent of the residue `c·s^a·(log s)^b`.  The same three
proofs go through with the order of eq. (26) restricted to that locus (`lexPairValue_lt_iff`), giving

> **`digammaLexValuation : Valuation L (WithZero (Multiplicative (ℤ ×ₗ ℤ)))`**
> (`HTGeometry/DigammaLexValuation.lean`) — eq. (19) as a rank-two valuation of `ℂ(s)⟨ψ⟩`.

The three valuations now in the corpus are the same datum read at three depths: `gammaValuation`
(rank one, the exponential direction), `digammaLexValuation` (rank two, the algebraic-logarithmic part
of §3), and `gammaLexValuation` (rank three, the whole triple with the order of eq. (26)).

### Equation-by-equation Lean map

Every numbered statement of §1–§8, with the Lean declaration that carries it.  All are unconditional
and `#print axioms` clean.

| (2) | `ψ = Γ′/Γ`, `L = ℂ(s)⟨ψ⟩` | **`digammaMero_mem_gammaAdjoin`**, `differentialAdjoin (RatFunc ℂ) {digammaMero}` |
| (3) | monomial valuation datum, with uniqueness | **`exists_gammaField_intDatum`**, **`existsUnique_gammaField_datum`**; tube form **`exists_tubeLimit_gammaField_intDatum`**, **`existsUnique_gammaTube_datum`**, **`hasGammaDatum_abscissa_independent`** |

| (7) | vertical uniform almost periodicity | `IsStripAlmostPeriodic` |
| (8)–(9) | autonomous ADE and finite Bohr rank | **`finite_phase_law_of_differentialAlgebraic_strip`** |

| (17) | injectivity of the formal expansion | bypassed — same replacement as (10)–(13) |
| (18)–(19) | algebraic-logarithmic residue of `L` | **`existsUnique_digammaField_datum`**, `exists_jetPolyCoeff_datum`; tube form **`existsUnique_digammaTube_datum`**; as a valuation **`digammaLexValuation`** |
| (20)–(21) | `Γ^{(n)}/Γ` Bell relations, `K_Γ = L(Γ)` | `exists_gammaJet_factor`, **`differentialAdjoin_gamma_eq_adjoin_gamma_digammaJets`** |
| §4 valuation | one exponential direction, `Γ` a uniformizer | **`gammaDatumHom`**, **`gammaExponentHom`**, **`gammaExponentHom_gammaUnit`**, **`gammaExponentHom_surjective`**, **`hasGammaDatum_add_exponent_ge`**, **`gammaValuation`** |

| (26) | the `(k, a, b)` dominance order | **`tendsto_gammaShapeZ_ratio_zero`**, **`exists_dominant_shapeZ_index`**, `exists_dominant_gammaJet_index`; as a valuation **`ShapeDominates`**, **`not_hasGammaDatum_add_dominates`**, **`gammaLexValuation`** |
| (27)–(29) | recurrence collapse to constant coefficients | **`exists_collapse_of_gammaJet_data`**, `recurrence_collapse_of_normalized` |
| (30)–(31) | Dirichlet series of infinite exponent rank | **`hypertranscendentalOver_gammaField_of_dirichlet`** |
| (32) | `ζ` differentially transcendental over `K_Γ` | **`zeta_hypertranscendentalOver_gammaCoefficientField`** (and `1/ζ`: **`zetaInv_hypertranscendentalOver_gammaCoefficientField`**) |
| (33) | infinite prime-frequency carrier requirement | **`primeAperture_no_fixed_finiteCarrier`**, `primeAperture_carrier_covering_dim_ge` |
| (34) | the finite prime aperture `Z_(N,σ)` | `primeApertureCharacterModule_finrank`, `primeAperture_dim_unbounded` |
| §6 divisor clause | observables of a solution | **`rank_finite_of_dominated_gamma_rational`** |
| §5 non-vacuity | the law with genuinely Gamma-field coefficients | **`gammaField_law_witness_gamma_coefficients`** |
| §8.1–§8.8 | boundary cases | see the §8 section above; each has its own module |

Both clauses of §1 that the prose states uniformly — the `o(1)` of eq. (3) and the convergence of
eq. (5) — are now formalized in both forms.  The line form
(**`exists_gammaField_datum`**, **`exists_dominant_gammaField_ratio`**) is what the law's own proof
consumes, since the collapse (27)→(29) runs on line data; the tube form
(**`exists_tubeLimit_gammaField_datum`**, **`exists_tubeLimit_dominant_ratio`**) supplies a single
height threshold for every abscissa in `(0, M]`, which is the reading of the prose.

### Formalization audit

Every Lean name the card names in bold was resolved to its fully qualified declaration and checked
with `#print axioms` in one pass against the `GaloisForLFunctions.HTGeometry` umbrella:

- **497 bolded names, all resolved, none missing**;
- **497 axiom reports, all `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`** (one declaration depends on no
  axioms at all); no `sorryAx`, no additional axiom, no error;
- the 227 modules they live in are all inside the `HTGeometry` umbrella, which builds clean.

The single-backtick names were audited too.  Three referred to declarations that do not exist and have
been corrected in place: `norm_Gamma_vertical_le` → `norm_Gamma_vertical_le_pow` at `k = 1`,
`gamma_monomial_valuation_residue_vertical` → `gamma_monomial_valuation_residue_of_pos`,
`uniformContinuous_expChar_bohrLine` → `IsBohrHullFrequency` /
`isBohrHullFrequency_of_bohrCoefficient_ne_zero`.  The remaining unresolved single-backtick tokens are
mathlib names and prose notation (`K_Gamma`, `F_beta`, `lambda_n`, …).

Together with the equation-by-equation map above — every numbered statement of §1–§8, both in its line
form and, for eqs. (3), (4)–(5) and (19), in its tube form — this closes the Lean gate: the paper's
content is formalized unconditionally, with the corpus's lint gate forbidding `sorry`, `admit`,
`axiom`, `opaque` and `unsafe` in the tree.

### Literature gate: what a web pass established (August 2026)

A search pass located the three references' subject matter but not their full texts (Bull. LMS, J.
Differential Equations and the Lue--Jiang--Lue paper are paywalled; no arXiv preprints surfaced).
What it did establish:

- **Wang--Yao (2024)** and **Lue (2025)** are in the *differential and difference independence* line:
  `ζ` and `Γ` cannot satisfy certain nontrivial algebraic differential or difference equations —
  Markus's conjecture and a sharpening of the difference analogue of Hoelder's theorem.  That is
  exactly the eq. (32) area, which this card already records as known and does not claim.
- **Lue--Jiang--Lue (2025)** sits in the same line for *Dirichlet series* against `Γ`, continuing the
  Acta Math. Sci. 2020 and J. Math. Anal. Appl. 2018 papers on algebraic differential independence of
  `Γ` and Dirichlet series.
- No located paper in this line states a projective boundary for `K_Γ` on a vertical tube, a
  lexicographic dominance order on boundary data, or a Bohr-rank consequence for strip almost periodic
  solutions.  So nothing found so far touches eqs. (3)--(5), (8)--(9) or (26)--(31).

This is a partial comparison: the gate asks for full text, which this pass could not obtain.  It is
recorded so a later attempt starts from the located citations rather than repeating the searches.
